# Coverage 95 % — the second owner-question round over the capability ledger (2026-09-11)

> **Operational record, not the answer sheet.** Produced 2026-09-11 by a read-only Workflow (`wf_91e94a05-513`): one READER per ledger family A–O clustered every non-covered cell (partial / absent / no verdict) of `2026-08-28-capability-ledger.md` into a decision the owner can take, with a balance-rule recommendation; one REFUTER per family tried to break each recommendation against the rendered Claude pages, the vendored SDK 0.3.247 (Claude Code 2.1.247) and the codex source. A refuter's `verdict: corrected` is a refinement in most cases, not a reversal — read its correction beside the reader's recommendation. The owner's goal: 95–100 % coverage with deliberate refusals counted as covered-by-decision (the `n/a` column with a recorded reason). The SYNTHESIS the owner answers from is `2026-09-11-coverage-95-owner-answers.md` (one line per question, options lettered). This file is the evidence.

Totals: 137 questions, 12 upheld as written, 125 corrected or refined by a refuter.

## Family A — The safety floor — what stops a harmful action

### QA1 — The model-backed judge: silenced on claude by our own hook, absent on codex, thin on Pi

- **Rows:** A1, A13, A23, A25, A30, A31, A32, A35, A80
- **Cells:** 21 non-covered of 27. Pi 3 partial (A1, A23, A32) + 3 absent (A13, A30, A80) + 3 covered (A25, A31, A35); Claude 6 absent (A1, A23, A25, A30, A35, A80) + 3 no-verdict (A13, A31, A32); Codex 5 absent (A1, A23, A30, A31, A35) + 1 partial (A25) + 3 n/a (A13, A32, A80).
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes.md — #eliminate-prompts-with-auto-mode, #what-the-classifier-blocks-by-default, #how-the-classifier-evaluates-actions, #when-auto-mode-falls-back (verbatim: "if the classifier blocks an action 3 times in a row or 20 times total, auto mode pauses and Claude Code resumes prompting… These thresholds are not configurable"), #how-auto-mode-handles-subagents; auto-mode-config.md#where-the-classifier-reads-configuration. All anchors re-derived today by curl of the RENDERED page (id=" grep), HTTP 200. Codex side: learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-approvals-security.md §Automatic approval reviews — WEAK tier (page); the strong tier is the vendored release schema at rust-v0.148.0 (approvals_reviewer 4 hits, auto_review 2 hits).
- **Native:** CLAUDE — tier 1, and the native mechanism is already enabled and never consulted. In auto mode the SDK classifier IS the permission prompt, and our PreToolUse hook answers `permissionDecision: 'allow'`, which skips it: the comment at packages/shared/src/agent/claude-agent.ts (the Options literal, ~line 7016) says so in as many words. P3.93/P3.96 (`b0e6f0044`) measured 0 classifier requests and `curl … | sh` executed with no review; P3.121 (`6f3cc6443`) measured the gateway answering the classifier's prompt in the shape the binary parses, 28 of 28, so the mechanism works on the shipped default card. NEW, measured by me at HEAD against the lifted bundle: `classifierContext` is now a real PostToolUse hook field — 1 occurrence in node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk/sdk.d.ts:2363 with a full contract docblock, and 11 in the vendored 2.1.247 binary. At 2.1.233 it scored 0, so A80's recorded 'page ahead of the artefact' is REFUTED and the field is a tier-1 enable today. CODEX — tier 1 adopted: `approvals_reviewer = "auto_review"` plus `[auto_review].policy` are already written (config-writer.ts, 9 and 18 hits), but they review approval REQUESTS, not subagent task text, not git state, not project instructions. PI — ours: the Guardian, per-action only, every consult site inside the PreToolUse gate; prompts/guardian.ts scores CLAUDE.md 0, AGENTS.md 0, projectInstructions 0 against hard_deny 6 / soft_deny 6, and the consult context carries no working-tree state (`showUntrackedFiles` has no producer).
- **Why absent today:** DECISION on A1 — the recorded word is "not covered yet", blocked on receipt/audit provenance for a second permission authority, and the claude absence is pinned by a test. Everything else is BUILDABLE with a closed record and no build: P6.191 (A13, A35) `839dea82d`, P6.199 (A23), P6.201 (A25), P6.203 (A30), P6.204 (A32) are all ✅ — they landed measurements and narrowings, none of them gave claude a judge. No plan row anywhere un-silences the claude classifier. None of these rows carries Deferred 2026-09-10 or Release sitting 2026-09-10.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build native: stop answering `allow` where our gate has no verdict of its own — return `defer`/`ask` there so the vendor's classifier runs, keep our floors ahead of it, mint its verdict into the existing audit receipt, and adopt `classifierContext` from PostToolUse** (size M) — A second permission authority on claude, which is the exact thing the recorded decision was waiting on — so the receipt is not optional. Latency and spend on every call our gate does not itself decide. A bare hook `ask` is NOT the repair (measured): it never reaches the classifier and raises a real prompt; the documented 'the classifier can still deny' holds only on the hookAskFloor path, which needs an ask RULE too.
  2. **Build host-side instead: leave claude silenced, extend Pi's Guardian with the three missing inputs (project instructions, git status, spawn-and-return review)** (size M) — The judge stays a one-runtime capability. A switch to claude or codex silently drops it — the largest defect class this fork has, re-created deliberately.
  3. **Refuse and record: Pi's Guardian is the product's judge, claude's native classifier stays pre-empted by our deterministic gate on purpose, codex's auto_review is the codex answer; all three announced in the switch-cost report** (size S) — A user in Allow-all on claude gets our deterministic gate and nothing model-backed; the measured `curl … | sh` case runs unreviewed. Honest, but the floor is lower on claude than the page promises, on a runtime whose page is the contract Pi is built to.
  4. **Defer to a later wave** (size —) — The nine rows stay non-covered and the measured un-reviewed-execution case stays live.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1, in its smallest form, for the CLAUDE arm only. It is the purest native-first case in the family: the mechanism is shipped, enabled, paid for, and proven to answer correctly on our own gateway (28/28) — we are the only thing stopping it. Least code by a wide margin against building a judge. Do NOT build a second judge on codex: auto_review is already adopted, so those cells are n/a-by-decision. Pi's three missing inputs are a separate, smaller row, not this decision. Adopt `classifierContext` in the same change because it just arrived in the bundle and it is the documented channel for handing the classifier host-asserted context.
- **Question (RU):** Снять с claude нашу блокировку штатного классификатора — заменить безусловный `allow` на `defer`/`ask` там, где наш детерминированный гейт не выносит собственного вердикта, завести вердикт классификатора в существующую квитанцию аудита и включить `classifierContext`, появившийся в бандле 2.1.247? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** All 21 non-covered cells dispositioned: ~11 move to covered or partial-with-stated-residue (the six claude absents, A80 on Pi and claude, A13/A31/A32's claude no-verdicts get a verdict), ~10 become n/a-by-decision (the codex column, where auto_review is the adopted answer).
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Cells (a) and anchors (b) all verify — the nine rows carry exactly the claimed verdicts, and all five permission-modes.md + auto-mode-config.md anchors resolve on the rendered page (HTTP 200). The bundle claim also verifies and is stronger than written: both self-reports agree at 2.1.247 (`"claudeCodeVersion": "2.1.247"`; `./node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-darwin-arm64/claude --version` → `2.1.247 (Claude Code)`), `classifierContext` is real at sdk.d.ts:2363 with a full PostToolUse contract docblock and 26 occurrences in the binary, so A80's 'page ahead of the artefact' is genuinely REFUTED. What breaks is (c)/(d): the recommendation names three mechanisms and every one of them is measured or documented NOT to do what the question says. `ask` is already excluded by the row's own measurement. `defer` is NOT a route to the classifier — claude-agent.ts:463-466 quotes hooks.md#pretooluse-decision-control withholding all three companion fields from it and the code comment at :652-656 says a defer 'is not a question about permission — it is a request to come back later, which the runtime honours by re-firing `PreToolUse` for the same call on the next turn'. And the only arm that DID reach the classifier (2 requests) — returning `{ continue: true }` with no `permissionDecision` — is a shape this repo already shipped and deliberately reverted: claude-agent.ts:570-580, 'an allow carrying no decision falls through to normal permission evaluation, and Safe Mode would start prompting on the read-only bash commands it had just approved — the regression the bypass was introduced to avoid', pinned end to end by `claude-permission-mode.test.ts`. The question also omits that this is already a CLOSED owner decision: plan row P3.139 is ✅ with sha cell 'decided — no code' and the title 'the classifier works, and enabling it is now an OWNER decision about latency and variance', and claude-agent.ts:5981-5983 records 'P3.139 decided to leave that flag where it is'. Corrected question: «P3.139 уже вынес это решение владельцу (классификатор работает, включение — вопрос латентности и разброса) — подтверждаем ли мы отказ, или переоткрываем? Если переоткрываем, то НЕ через `defer` и НЕ через `ask` (оба измеренно/документированно не доходят до классификатора) и не через возврат без `permissionDecision` (эта форма уже была и откачена — Safe Mode начинал спрашивать про read-only bash, `claude-permission-mode.test.ts`), а только через путь `hookAskFloor` с ask-ПРАВИЛОМ, и только на остаточном классе вызовов, где наш гейт не выносит вердикта, с измерением, что одобренные read-only команды не переспрашиваются. Отдельно и независимо: включить `classifierContext` (sdk.d.ts:2363), который в дереве имеет 0 вхождений.» Size is smaller than M for the classifierContext half and larger/riskier for the classifier half than stated.

### QA2 — The judge's configuration and review surfaces — trust lists, denial history, effective-config dump, drafting assistant

- **Rows:** A33, A34, A42, A44, A45, A79
- **Cells:** 18 of 18 non-covered. A33 partial/partial/partial; A34 partial/partial/absent; A42 absent/partial/absent; A44 absent/partial/absent; A45 absent/absent/absent; A79 absent/absent/absent.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/auto-mode-config.md — anchors re-derived today from the rendered page: #define-trusted-infrastructure, #override-the-block-and-allow-rules, #review-denials, #fix-a-denial-with-an-allow-rule-an-environment-entry-or-a-retry, #inspect-the-defaults-and-your-effective-config, #generate-environment-entries, #what-auto-mode-setup-reads, #turn-off-auto-mode-setup, #edit-rules-from-permissions. HTTP 200, page in scripts/claude-doc-pages.tsv.
- **Native:** CLAUDE — the whole surface is native in the shipped binary as CLI subcommands plus a /permissions tab, and unreachable because our composer owns the permission UI and the binary's TUI never renders: `auto-mode-setup` scores 22 in the vendored 2.1.247 binary (measured today). Two version blocks the ledger recorded against 2.1.233 have EXPIRED with the lift — the page gates the denials tab on v2.1.246 and the auto-mode tab on v2.1.246, and we now ship 2.1.247 — so 'the page gates it above our bundle' is no longer the reason. The raw material is already produced and thrown away: the SDK's own PermissionDenied hook mints a typed receipt on every denial whose only sink is `onDebug`. PI — managed scope only: AUTO_MODE_KEYS = ['environment','hard_deny','soft_deny','allow'], parsed entry-by-entry from the managed policy, no user scope and no UI; the four-tier precedence IS stated verbatim in the guardian prompt with a 4000-char `trustedIntent` slot, so what is unmeasured is the model's compliance, not the mechanism. CODEX — `[auto_review].policy` is one free-text prose blob with no list structure and no denial history.
- **Why absent today:** BUILDABLE/OWNER. P6.205 (A34), P6.209 (A42), P6.493 (A33) are ✅ — closed as measurements and corrections; the UI was never in their scope. The 2026-09-05 owner round already asks the A33 half — «[host] May a developer widen the judge's trust boundary on their own machine?» — and it is still open, so this question carries it forward rather than re-asking it. A79 is likewise already put on 2026-09-05 as «[host] Do we ship an environment-drafting assistant at all (`/auto-mode-setup`)?». None deferred, none release-sitting.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build the full surface: per-scope editor for allow/soft_deny/hard_deny/environment with managed entries read-only, plus the denials list, retry, and the effective-config dump** (size L) — A new settings subsystem to keep honest across three runtimes, and it re-opens the injection question A36 currently earns `covered` for — a user-scope autoMode editor is one migration away from a project-scope one.
  2. **Build the read-only half only: an effective-config dump and a denials list fed by the receipt that already exists on claude** (size M) — The user can see what the judge decided and why, and cannot edit it. Codex's denial feed has no list structure, so its cell stays partial with the residue named.
  3. **Refuse and record: autoMode stays managed-only by design, no editor, no drafting assistant** (size S) — A developer cannot name their own trusted repos, registries or domains on their own machine; every judge tuning goes through an administrator. Defensible — it is the same posture that makes A36 `covered` — but it must be said out loud in the permissions surface.
  4. **Defer** (size —) — Eighteen cells stay non-covered and the claude denial receipt keeps going to a debug sink.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 2 plus the refusal for the editor and the drafting assistant. Native-first answers it: on claude the material is already minted and discarded, so wiring an existing receipt to a list is far less code than an editor and is closest to the page's own #review-denials shape. The managed-only posture for the LISTS is a product posture already earned — A36 scores `covered` precisely because a repository cannot inject classifier rules — and A79 reads shell history and home-directory repositories, which is a privacy posture, not an engineering one.
- **Question (RU):** Показать пользователю то, что судья уже произвёл — список отказов и дамп действующей конфигурации, — но НЕ давать редактор списков доверия (autoMode остаётся managed-only) и НЕ ввозить ассистент черновиков `/auto-mode-setup`? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 18 of 18 dispositioned: A42 → covered on claude and Pi with codex partial-with-residue, A45 → covered (our own dump of buildClaudeAutoModePolicySettings plus the composed managed block); A33, A34, A44, A79 → n/a-by-decision, each carrying the recorded reason.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Cells and all ten auto-mode-config.md anchors verify. Two things break it. (1) The read-only half it recommends BUILDING is already built and landed: plan row P6.209 is ✅ (`2e582e822`), titled 'there is no place to see which permission rules are in effect, which the judge denied, or what the judge's own rules are' — that is A42/A44/A45's denials list plus effective-config dump. (2) The editor half it recommends REFUSING was already decided the other way: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-denominator-questions-triage.md` item 2 reads '**DECIDED — C** (user scope for `hard_deny`/`soft_deny` only; `allow`/`environment` stay managed-only) · consequence: row (ledger A33)', and the file's own header states the fifteen OWNER items were put to the owner and 'on 2026-09-05 the owner took every recommendation'. So «autoMode остаётся managed-only» contradicts a standing decision that already admits a user scope for the two strictness lists — and A33's own plan row P6.493 is ✅ (`df51f7132`). A79 is likewise closed, not open: triage item 8 is 'OWNER — D2' and the recorded answer line is 'D1 A · D2 A · D3 B', i.e. A = no drafting assistant. Corrected question: the whole cluster is a citation, not a decision — «A42/A44/A45 закрыты строкой P6.209 (`2e582e822`); A33 решён 2026-09-05 как вариант C (пользовательская область только для `hard_deny`/`soft_deny`, `allow`/`environment` остаются managed-only) и реализован в P6.493 (`df51f7132`); A79 решён как D2=A — ассистент черновиков не ввозим. Требуется только пересчёт восемнадцати ячеек ledger'а под уже сделанное, а не новое решение.»

### QA3 — The read boundary outside the working directories — the setting and its one-time prompt exist on no runtime we ship

- **Rows:** A27, A28, A29
- **Cells:** 9 of 9 non-covered. A27 absent/absent/absent; A28 partial/partial/partial; A29 absent/absent/absent.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference.md#permissions-blockreadsoutsideworkingdirectories — verbatim: "Stop Claude from reading paths outside the session's working directories with the Read, Grep, Glob, and LSP tools, in every permission mode including `bypassPermissions`… Requires Claude Code v2.1.257 or later." And permission-modes.md#first-read-outside-the-working-directories, which specifies the three answers verbatim: Keep allowing / Block from now on (which writes the setting) / Ask again next time, with the carve-out "The prompt doesn't appear in non-interactive `-p` runs or background sessions". Plus permissions.md#working-directories and #additional-directories-grant-file-access-not-configuration. All curl-verified today, HTTP 200.
- **Native:** NONE on any of the three, and the claude arm is measured rather than assumed. I probed the vendored 2.1.247 binary today: `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` → 0 occurrences, against positive controls in the same probe (`restricted` 138, `allowAppleEvents` 8, `failIfUnavailable` 12, `classifierContext` 11) that prove the probe reads the file. The page needs 2.1.257 and the bundle is ten patch versions short, so there is nothing to enable on claude either. PI — our gate assembles cwd, workspace root, plans/data folders and `additionalDirectories` only as auto-allow WRITE roots (core/pre-tool-use.ts around :2286-2296); there is no read floor at all, so the extension mechanism the page names exists while the boundary it extends does not. CODEX — the nearest thing is `sandbox_workspace_write` / `default_permissions` roots, which is a sandbox boundary rather than a permission rule, and the sandbox is off by the standing owner decision of 2026-08-12/13.
- **Why absent today:** BUILDABLE, size M. P6.202 (A27) and P6.213 are ✅ but landed as records; A28 and A29 have no plan row at all. Not deferred, not release-sitting. A29's second answer writes the setting A28 is about, so the two halves cannot be built separately in the other order.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build both host-side for all three runtimes: the `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` predicate in the shared gate, then the once-per-session prompt with the page's three answers** (size M) — One predicate plus one card; the extension mechanism (`additionalDirectories`, `/add-dir`) already parses on all three, so the boundary lands where the page expects it. When the SDK reaches 2.1.257 the claude arm has two enforcers — ours and the vendor's — which must agree or the stricter one must visibly win.
  2. **Build the setting only, no prompt** (size S) — An operator can block; a user is never asked. The documented three-answer prompt stays absent, and the only way to discover the boundary is to hit it.
  3. **Refuse and record: reads outside the working directories follow the permission mode and rules only, stated in the mode picker** (size S) — On the default card an agent in Allow-all reads any path on the machine and nothing asks. That is today's behaviour — the refusal only makes it visible, which is worth something, but it leaves the widest read surface in this family open.
  4. **Defer until the SDK lift reaches 2.1.257, then enable natively on claude and mirror host-side on Pi and codex** (size —) — Buys the claude arm for free later; buys nothing for Pi, which is the default runtime, or for codex.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1, host-side, in that order — setting first (it is one predicate on an existing seam), prompt second. Native-first asked and found nothing on any runtime, and waiting for 2.1.257 helps only one of three arms while Pi is the default card. This is the clearest tier-2 case in the family: the host supplies it once and the residual difference disappears rather than needing to be announced.
- **Question (RU):** Построить границу чтения вне рабочих каталогов на стороне хоста для всех трёх рантаймов — сначала настройку `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories`, затем разовый вопрос при первом чтении наружу с тремя ответами страницы (в бандле 2.1.247 ключа нет вовсе, он появляется только в 2.1.257)? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 9 of 9 → covered (or 6 covered and 3 partial-with-stated-residue if only the setting is built).
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Anchors and both verbatim sentences verify exactly (settings-reference.md:1528 'Requires Claude Code v2.1.257 or later.'; the three-answer prompt on permission-modes.md), and the binary probe reproduces: `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` → 0 in the vendored 2.1.247 binary against controls `restricted` 211 / `allowAppleEvents` 11 / `failIfUnavailable` 14 / `classifierContext` 26. But (c) and 'why_absent' are refuted at HEAD: the thing it proposes to build IS built. Plan row P6.202 is ✅ with five commits (`cb35373f4 + fbc326b7d + e5a8452b3 + 75ef8ef87 + 5e9042ae9`), it explicitly carries 'Denominator A27, A28, A29' — so the claim 'A28 and A29 have no plan row at all' is false — and its done_means is verbatim the question's ask: '(1) a `Read`/`Grep`/`Glob` outside the working directory plus `additionalDirectories` PROMPTS…; (2) with `permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` set, the same read is REFUSED with a named diagnostic in every mode including `auto` and `bypassPermissions`, on all three; (3) in auto mode the first such read offers the page's three answers, the choice persists, and `-p`/background sessions are carved out.' The code confirms it: `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` is in 17 tracked files including `packages/shared/src/agent/core/pre-tool-use.ts`, `core/permission-manager.ts`, `mode-manager.ts`, `SessionManager.ts`, `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/config-writer-permissions.ts`, `apps/electron/resources/config-defaults.json`, with `__tests__/outside-working-directory-read-answer.test.ts` and `__tests__/read-fence-gate-sites.test.ts`. Corrected question: «Ничего строить не нужно — граница чтения и разовый вопрос с тремя ответами уже построены хостом для всех трёх рантаймов строкой P6.202 (пять коммитов, 17 файлов). Нужен только пересчёт девяти ячеек A27/A28/A29 в ledger'е (сегодня они absent/partial и устарели) и решение о втором исполнителе, когда SDK дойдёт до 2.1.257: чей отказ побеждает — наш или вендорский.»

### QA4 — Changing rules or mode on a live session without respawning the runtime — three native calls, none of them called

- **Rows:** A61, A62, A63
- **Cells:** 6 of 9 non-covered. A61 covered/absent/no-verdict; A62 partial/absent/no-verdict; A63 covered/covered/absent.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions.md#working-directories and #move-the-session-to-another-directory (the `/add-dir` and `/cd` semantics); the SDK surface itself in the vendored node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk/sdk.d.ts (`applyFlagSettings` at :2505, `allowedTools?` :1426, `disallowedTools?` :1446) — an artefact reading, stronger than a page. Codex: `turn/start` / `command/exec` accepting `sandboxPolicy` / `approvalPolicy` / `approvalsReviewer` as "Override … for this turn and subsequent turns", read from the vendored app-server protocol at rust-v0.148.0 — STRONG tier (shipped artefact), the page is not the evidence here.
- **Native:** CLAUDE — tier 1, unused: `Query.applyFlagSettings()` is in the vendored SDK types and is named in two docblocks and two spec tables in our tree and called nowhere; `PermissionUpdate { type: 'addDirectories', directories, destination }` is returnable as `PermissionResult.updatedPermissions` from the `canUseTool` callback we already implement (canUseTool scores 26 in claude-agent.ts). Today `allowedTools`/`disallowedTools`/`settingSources` are hashed into the live-session identity, so any change respawns. PI — covered by construction: the gate is host-side and in-process, so session rules are added, removed and replaced on the live agent. CODEX — the per-turn override exists in the protocol we vendor and our path writes config.toml and respawns instead; our absence on claude is likewise a CHOICE, because session-scoped grants are declared on `PiAgent` alone.
- **Why absent today:** BUILDABLE. P6.221 (A61), P6.222 (A62), P6.223 (A63) are all ✅ — closed as records that named the native mechanism without adopting it. Not deferred, not release-sitting.
- **Options:**
  1. **Adopt the three native calls: `applyFlagSettings` and `updatedPermissions.addDirectories` on claude, per-turn `sandboxPolicy`/`approvalPolicy` on codex** (size M) — A tightening applies to the rest of the current turn on every runtime, as the page specifies; the session-identity hash stops forcing a respawn. Risk is narrow and known: a mid-turn widening must be refused, or a rule change becomes an escalation path.
  2. **Refuse and record: a permission change takes effect from the next message, stated once in the mode picker** (size S) — A user who tightens mid-turn is not protected for the rest of that turn — the failure direction is the loosening one, which is the worst direction for a safety row to diverge in silently.
  3. **Defer** (size —) — Six cells stay non-covered while the mechanisms sit in artefacts we already ship.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. This is the least-code decision in the whole family and the closest to each runtime's own documentation: three mechanisms, all present in artefacts we vendor, all currently at zero call sites, and Pi already behaves the way they would make the other two behave. Refuse mid-turn WIDENING explicitly in the same change so the seam can only be used in the safe direction.

The central native claim — 'all currently at zero call sites' — is false on two of three legs, and the third is a recorded refusal the question would reverse. (1) `Query.applyFlagSettings()` IS called: `packages/shared/src/agent/claude-agent.ts:9074` `live.applyFlagSettings({ permissions: { deny: [...wanted.disallowedTools] } })`, guarded at :9067, with `backend/claude/live-session.ts:346` naming P6.221; plan row P6.221 is ✅ (`ad325d4b0` `9020776d2`, 2026-09-06, 'a mid-session ban is told to the running child instead of respawning it'). (3) `sandboxPolicy` is deliberately NOT sent, with the reason measured and written at codex-agent.ts:9846-9857 — '`SandboxPolicy` is the legacy four-member union, and its `workspaceWrite` member REQUIRES `writableRoots` plus `networkAccess`… Sending it would mean restating, on every turn, four values the config layer derives in one place — a second source of truth for the sandbox, where getting `networkAccess` wrong is a silent widening. It also cannot express a NAMED `[permissions.<name>]` profile at all'. Answering «да» would re-open a measured silent-widening path, which is exactly the AGENTS.md read-don't-invent failure. Corrected question: «Строка закрыта: P6.221 (`ad325d4b0`) и P6.223 (`73f30c78b`) уже сняли перезапуск на claude и на codex. Открыто ровно одно: возвращать ли `PermissionUpdate{addDirectories}` из `canUseTool` на claude, как это уже делает Pi (R15). `sandboxPolicy` на codex остаётся отказом с записанной причиной (codex-agent.ts:9846) — не трогаем. ### QA5 — Codex's own approvals and profile vocabulary, which we deliberately write only a subset of

- **Rows:** A20, A64, A65, A66, A67, A68, A69
- **Cells:** 11 of 21 non-covered. A20 codex absent; A64 codex absent; A65 absent/absent/partial; A66 codex partial; A67 absent/absent/absent; A68 codex absent; A69 codex partial. Ten of the cells are `n/a` on Pi and claude because the axis does not exist there.
- **Docs:** learn.chatgpt.com/docs/permissions.md — §Define and select a profile, §Extend a profile, §Configuration spec, §Migrate from older sandbox settings, §Scope and enforcement (curl-verified today, HTTP 200, read as `.md` because learn.chatgpt.com pages are client-rendered and the `id=` route scores 6/25 there). learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-approvals-security.md — §Sandbox and approvals, §Automatic approval reviews, §Run without approval prompts, §Configuration in `config.toml` (which prints the `approval_policy = { granular = { sandbox_approval, rules, mcp_elicitations, request_permissions, skill_approval } }` block verbatim). Both are WEAK tier by AGENTS.md's rule. STRONG tier, probed by me today in the vendored release schema packages/codex-protocol/schema/config-schema.json at rust-v0.148.0: `granular` 2, `approvals_reviewer` 4, `auto_review` 2, `default_permissions` 1, `sandbox_mode` 2, `glob_scan_max_depth` 1, `default_tools_approval_mode` 4.
- **Native:** Codex has ALL of it and we write a subset — measured in our writer today: `CODEX_PERMISSION_MODE_TABLE` freezes exactly seven `{sandbox_mode, approval_policy}` pairs, so the two axes codex keeps separate are fused into one (A67); one profile `[permissions.nessy_runtime]` is written and no user-authored profile is ever read (A68); `granular` occurs 29 times in config-writer.ts and stays unemitted because OUR `enumOf` flattens codex's `oneOf`, not because codex lacks it (A64); `glob_scan_max_depth` is deliberately NOT written with the reason recorded verbatim at config-writer-permissions.ts:200-213 — writing it silences the warning by CAPPING the expansion, so the deny stops applying below the cap while the config still claims it (A69); `approvals_reviewer = "auto_review"` IS written, setting-gated, default off, and A66's residue is only the two-control surface (A65/A66). A20 is a live defect, not a gap: `resolve_permission_config_syntax` sets Legacy from `sandbox_mode` and then UNCONDITIONALLY sets Profiles from `default_permissions`, so our unconditional credential profile overrides the user's sandbox-off switch. Pi and claude have no equivalent axis, which is why their cells are `n/a`.
- **Why absent today:** Mixed: DECISION with a recorded reason (A69), DEFECT in our own code (A64's enum flattening, A20's unconditional profile), OWNER (A65/A67/A68 — whether the user picks a codex profile or an approvals mode at all). P6.225 (A66) is ✅ and landed the full wiring. A20, A64, A65, A67, A68, A69 have no plan row. None deferred, none release-sitting.
- **Options:**
  1. **Adopt codex's vocabulary as the codex answer: emit `granular` (fix our oneOf flattening), let sandbox and approvals be set independently, expose the two auto_review controls, and let a user-authored profile be selected** (size L) — Codex gains everything codex documents, and the product then has two permission vocabularies — the host's seven modes and codex's two axes — which is a switch-safety cost paid on every runtime change.
  2. **Take only the two cheapest and fix the defect: repair A20 (the off switch that does not turn it off) and surface the two auto_review controls (A66) over the mechanism already wired** (size S) — The one real safety defect in this cluster closes, and codex's own reviewer becomes reachable without hand-editing config.toml. The remaining axes stay unavailable and must be said out loud.
  3. **Refuse and record wholesale: the host's seven modes are the product's permission vocabulary on every runtime; codex's extra axes are deliberately not exposed** (size S) — A codex user cannot express what codex itself can — and A20's defect survives, so a user who turns the sandbox off still has it on. Not defensible while A20 is unfixed.
  4. **Defer** (size —) — A20 stays a measured defect where the product does the opposite of what the user asked.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 2 now; the rest of option 1 refused-and-recorded. A20 is a defect regardless of posture — our unconditional credential profile silently overrides a user's explicit off switch — and must be fixed under any answer. A66 is two controls over a mechanism already fully wired, which is native-first tier 1. Fusing sandbox and approvals into seven named modes is a PRODUCT POSTURE (one vocabulary across three runtimes) and per the owner rule it is stated, not decided here; `glob_scan_max_depth` already carries its recorded refusal verbatim in the source and should stay refused.
- **Question (RU):** Починить A20 (наш безусловный credential-профиль перебивает выключённый sandbox) и вывести два контрола codex `auto_review` в интерфейс, а остальной словарь codex — granular-одобрения, независимые оси sandbox/approvals, пользовательские профили, `glob_scan_max_depth` — записать как осознанный отказ в пользу семи режимов хоста? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 11 of 11 dispositioned: A20 and A66 → covered (2 cells); A64, A65, A67, A68, A69 → n/a-by-decision, each with its recorded reason (9 cells).
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Cells verify per row, though the prose miscounts ('Ten of the cells are `n/a` on Pi and claude' — there are eight: A64, A66, A68, A69 × 2). Two substantive breaks. (1) The A66 half it recommends building is already shipped: plan row P6.225 is ✅ (`51d952f63 + 3101b7a92 + 911986846`) and its done_means is verbatim 'a toggle plus a policy textarea in the AI settings page in ALL locales, writing `codexAutoReviewEnabled` / `codexAutoReviewPolicy`, plus one switch-report line saying the reviewer is codex-only' — present at HEAD in `apps/electron/src/renderer/pages/settings/AiSettingsPage.tsx` with `__tests__/codex-auto-review-settings-surface.test.ts`. What survives is A20 alone: `grep -c A20` over the plan returns 0, so it genuinely has no row, and it is a defect under every posture. ### QA6 — Tool-level narrowing: codex has no per-tool subject at all, and ours stops at the host gate

- **Rows:** A7, A10, A24, A51, A52, A55, A56, A60, A74
- **Cells:** 15 of 27 non-covered. A7 codex partial; A10 codex absent; A24 codex absent; A51 codex partial; A52 codex partial; A55 Pi partial + codex absent; A56 Pi partial + claude partial + codex absent; A60 claude absent + codex absent; A74 absent/absent/absent.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions.md — #tool-specific-permission-rules, #match-all-uses-of-a-tool, #match-by-input-parameter, #tool-name-wildcards, #agent-subagents, #mcp (all anchors re-derived today from the rendered page). sub-agents.md#restrict-which-subagents-can-be-spawned and #subagent-output-scanning. cli-reference.md#cli-flags for `--tools`. Codex STRONG tier: the vendored config-schema.json at rust-v0.148.0 — `enabled_tools` 2, `disabled_tools` 3, `default_tools_approval_mode` 4, and no tool-name-pattern type anywhere in packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/.
- **Native:** CLAUDE — `Options.disallowedTools` IS passed but from a host constant (`CLAUDE_SESSION_DISALLOWED_TOOLS`, five names) and never from the user; `Options.allowedTools` is never passed at all. `toolAliases` exists in the SDK types and scores 25 in the vendored 2.1.247 binary while occurring in zero source files of ours — that is exactly A60's mechanism. Subagent output scanning is native from v2.1.210 and we now ship 2.1.247, so it runs on claude's own subagents (A24 claude covered). CODEX — STRUCTURAL, and the structure is measured, not assumed: `enabled_tools`/`disabled_tools` cover MCP SERVERS, not tools, so a partial narrowing would WIDEN by inheritance and such a role is denied whole; the approval vocabulary is per-COMMAND, so a host tool-name glob or a `Tool(param:value)` rule has no subject there — that is the SILENT-LOOSER P6.214 measured on A51/A52, where the rule is accepted and evaluated host-side and on codex has no subject beyond `command`/`file_path`. `default_tools_approval_mode` exists for MCP and our writer emits neither it nor the per-tool `approval_mode` (A74). PI — `skillDisallowedTools` per skill or agent only; the `Agent(` FORM parses but only as a permission DENY rule.
- **Why absent today:** STRUCTURAL on codex; DECISION/DEFECT on claude, where the user simply never reaches `allowedTools`. P6.214 (A51), P6.215, P6.217 (A55), P6.219, P6.220 (A60) are ✅ records. A7's codex partial is BEHAVIOUR-measured (P2.197, `b15c92b1f`, seven approval values driven on the shipped codex-app-server with controls). None deferred, none release-sitting.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build the reachable half and make the rest honest: pass the user's tool ceiling into `Options.allowedTools`/`disallowedTools` on claude, adopt `toolAliases` so a built-in call runs the host's implementation, and REFUSE a rule codex cannot carry instead of accepting it silently** (size M) — Claude gains a user-owned tool ceiling and the host's gate stops being bypassable by the runtime's own tool copy; on codex the user is told at rule-entry time that the rule has no subject, which turns a SILENT-LOOSER into an HONEST refusal.
  2. **Refuse and record only: keep the host gate as the single ceiling and announce the codex residue in the switch-cost report** (size S) — Cheapest, and it fixes the worst half — the silence. But the claude tool ceiling stays a host constant no operator can change, and `toolAliases` stays unused, so a built-in tool call can still run the runtime's own copy past our instrumentation.
  3. **Build a codex-side equivalent** (size L) — There is no subject to build it on — codex's approval vocabulary is per-command. This would mean re-implementing tool identity beside codex's policy, which the native-first rule puts last and which the ledger's own SILENT-LOOSER finding already prices.
  4. **Defer** (size —) — A rule the user writes goes on being accepted and silently unenforceable on codex — the ADVERTISED anti-pattern this ledger names.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. Its real value is the honesty half: a permission rule that is accepted and cannot be enforced is worse than one that is refused, and the codex side cannot be fixed at all because no subject exists — so `n/a`-by-structure with a loud switch-cost line is the correct verdict there, not a build. The claude half (`allowedTools`, `toolAliases`) is tier 1 and cheap.

### QA10 — Untrusted content: fetched pages, search results, agent-to-agent messages, and the secrets we never redact

- **Rows:** A14, A21, A77, A78
- **Cells:** 11 of 12 non-covered. A14 absent/absent/absent; A21 absent/absent/absent; A77 Pi absent + claude no-verdict (codex n/a); A78 Pi partial + claude absent + codex absent.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/security.md#additional-safeguards — verbatim: "**Isolated context windows**: Web fetch uses a separate context window to avoid injecting potentially malicious prompts" — and #protect-against-prompt-injection, #core-protections, #built-in-protections (anchors re-derived today, HTTP 200). agent-sdk/secure-deployment.md for the summarise-rather-than-inline guidance. whats-new/2026-w33 for the GitLab token families.
- **Native:** A14 — NONE by construction, on any runtime, and the reason is structural rather than a gap: `send_agent_message` is OUR host-served tool carried across runtimes (runtime-switch-costs.ts records `6173c9778 carried send_agent_message across`), so no runtime's own send review can ever see it; `deliverAgentMessage` runs `admitAgentMessage` (loop-stop plus inbound policy) and no judge on any of the three. The host is the only possible seam — native-first tier 2 by necessity. A77 — claude's isolated context window is asserted by the page about a closed binary, so that cell is `unknown` and not `have`; codex has no web-fetch tool on that path (`web_fetch`/`WebFetch` score 0 in codex-agent.ts and the adapter's sources); Pi's is ours and the sub-context primitive already exists (`agent: 'fork'` / named subagents) while pi-agent-server/src/tools/web-fetch.ts:288-292 puts the converted page body straight into the turn. A78 — Pi's search provider returns snippets, which is most of the mitigation, but it is inherited rather than enforced and the fallback branch defeats it: responses-api-parser.ts:97 caps a citation description at 280 chars while :114-120 puts the provider's ENTIRE output text into context. A21 — there is NO secret-redaction mechanism at all; `deniedPaths` (19 entries) has neither `gh` nor `glab`, and session-tools-core's `credentialDenyRoots` is enforced only by the OS isolation profile, which is off by standing decision.
- **Why absent today:** BUILDABLE/DEFECT. P6.192 (A14), P6.197 (A21), P6.231 (A77) are ✅ records — each named the mechanism and none built it. A78 has no plan row. None deferred, none release-sitting. A14 additionally matches the standing `send_agent_message` security finding and plan row P0.1.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build the smallest honest step per row: put the cross-agent message through the same gate every other tool call goes through; route Pi's `web_fetch` through a summarising sub-context (the primitive exists); cap the search fallback branch; add `glpat-`/`glrt-` and the `glab` config store to the protected list** (size M) — Four narrow additions on existing seams. The web-fetch sub-context costs one extra model turn per fetch on Pi — a real latency and spend cost that should be a setting, not a silent default.
  2. **Build only A14 (the cross-agent gate) and record the rest** (size S) — Closes the one row where the tool is entirely ours, the gate already exists, and a standing security finding points at it. Leaves fetched pages and search output flowing raw into Pi's context on the default card.
  3. **Refuse and record: the product does not summarise fetched content and does not review agent-to-agent text** (size S) — The one tool that crosses a trust boundary with no gate stays ungated, against a standing security finding — the weakest position in this family.
  4. **Defer** (size —) — Eleven cells stay non-covered and P0.1 stays open.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1, with A14 first. Native-first was asked and returned nothing for A14 by construction — a host-served tool is invisible to every runtime's own review — so the host is the only seam, and the gate it needs already exists. The web-fetch sub-context is the one piece with a real user-visible cost and should ship as a setting with a stated default rather than as a silent behaviour change.
- **Question (RU):** Пропустить `send_agent_message` через тот же гейт, что и любой другой вызов инструмента (закрыв стоящую находку P0.1), а также добавить суммаризирующий под-контекст для `web_fetch` на Pi как настройку, ограничить fallback-ветку поиска и внести семейства токенов `glpat-`/`glrt-` и конфиг `glab` в защищённый список? [да]

For B18 the decisive reading is ModelsRequirements = { newThread: NewThreadModelDefaults | null } — codex's models requirement is a NEW-THREAD DEFAULT, not an allowlist, and codex's own tui/src/managed_new_thread_defaults.rs says 'Managed values are defaults rather than enforcement. Preserve explicit launch choices.' Probed against the lift target rust-v0.150.1: core/src/config/requirements.rs fetched 200 (185 lines) and scores 0 for allowed_models, mcp_servers and marketplaces; the ConfigRequirements struct has moved into the codex_config crate and two candidate raw paths returned 404, so whether 0.150.1 widens the payload is a NULL I could not resolve, recorded rather than guessed. Host side, already landed: P6.238 (0b695f9ea + 6dc7ec853) makes the diagnostic say by name that an availableModels allowlist binds session and subagent models on Pi and claude and binds NOTHING on codex; P6.247 (7f76cf14a) makes formatCodexConfigRequirementsDiagnostics name the requirement families this protocol version cannot report, and pins the formatter's answer to an all-null object.
- **Why absent today:** Neither is a defect and neither is deferred. B18's codex cell is STRUCTURAL: codex has no model-allowlist mechanism at all, and the nearest documented thing it does have — the new-thread default — is already applied by resolveCodexManagedNewThreadModel before thread/start (W7-u, f2516e44a). B31's codex cell is STRUCTURAL with the honest surface now built: an administrator believes a policy is in force, and the host's diagnostic can only speak about fields it received, so the silence used to read as compliance; it no longer does. Both cells were written from the 2026-09-05 sweep at HEAD 34be43fde and never re-read after those commits.
- **Options:**
  1. **(a) Record both codex cells as n/a-by-decision — the runtime cannot, and the honest surface naming what it cannot do is built** (size XS) — The switch report keeps carrying the asymmetry by name. Cost: an organisation with a mixed fleet must read the diagnostic to learn that its model allowlist is advisory on codex; nothing in the product enforces it there.

Balance rule: closest to the runtime's own source — codex's own comment says these are defaults, not enforcement — and the host half that makes the limit legible is already landed, so building (b) would add code to contradict a documented posture.

### QB6 — Which way the managed tier fails when it cannot be enforced — Windows inert, and a valid-JSON non-object that fails OPEN

- **Rows:** B10, B29

All curl-verified 2026-09-11.

For Windows the page prescribes C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode\ (admin ACL by default) plus HKLM and the SDK reads both for its own child; managed-system-paths.ts:110-120 names the ACL check and the HKLM route as the two candidates and parks the Windows evidence day with P3.7. Trap recorded in the row: Node derives win32 mode from the read-only attribute, so a mode test is NOT an ownership test.

B10: P6.235 (3ebebd9ea) landed — the tier now reads %ProgramFiles%\Nessy with three named refusals and a second case-sensitivity trap fixed via a realpath compare, and P3.331's recorded blocker turned out to be already spent; but NO Windows host exists on any rig (P3.7 ⬜, WIN-1 in the CDP table waits for one), so no green is claimed and the cells were left absent. B29: P6.246(a) (63a0bdd0f, 2713cafcc) closed the arm the row was written for — readHostOwnedJson's catch now calls managedPolicyUnreadableNotice, shows an error box naming the policy FILE and exits 1 — but the OTHER arm survives: a document that is valid JSON and not an object still reaches parseManagedPiPolicyTolerantV1 and fails OPEN with a drop, which is exactly what 'cannot be parsed as a JSON object' asks about.
- **Options:**
  1. **(a) Bring the non-object arm to the same fail-CLOSED as the non-JSON arm, and record B10 as covered-by-code with the evidence day explicitly parked on P3.7** (size XS–S for B29 (one branch plus its mutation control); XS for B10 (ledger wording only)) — The two arms of one predicate stop failing in opposite directions — today a corrupted file refuses the boot and an array-shaped file leaves the fleet unprotected with a drop nobody reads. Cost: a fleet that ships a malformed policy gets an unbootable app on one more shape; the row's own Why cell already says that the direction of the failure decides between unprotected and unbootable, so this is a stated product posture, not a quiet fix.
  2. **(b) Leave both stated as partial residues** (size XS) — Honest but permanently short of the target: 6 cells stay non-covered, and the fail-open shape stays a live hole whose only report is a drop.
  3. **(c) Acquire a Windows host and run the evidence day** (size L, outside this plan) — The only thing that turns B10 into behaviour-proof. Costs a machine and a day, and it does not touch B29.

Balance rule: the smallest honest thing, and the fail-closed direction is what managed-settings.md#keys-that-fail-closed prescribes for the managed tier — so this is conformance, not invention. B10's Windows day stays parked on P3.7 rather than being counted as a green, which is what HONEST means in this ledger's own vocabulary.
- **Question (RU):** Приводим ли вторую ветку разбора (валидный JSON, но не объект) к тому же fail-closed, что и первая, и записываем B10 как покрытое кодом с явно припаркованным windows-днём (P3.7), а не как absent? [да]

### QB7 — Three residues that will stay stated rather than closed — codex's mid-session policy answer, Pi's missing degradation producer, per-group release channels

- **Rows:** B5, B6, B34
- **Cells:** B5 Claude partial / Codex partial (2 cells; Pi covered); B6 Pi no-verdict (1 cell; Claude and Codex covered); B34 partial ×3 — 6 cells
- **Docs:** server-managed-settings.md#security-approval-dialogs and #approval-memory (the approval window P3.289 closed); settings-reference.md#fastmode for the sibling class; plugin-marketplaces.md lines 1054 and 1101 for the per-group delivery tier B34 needs. Codex side, STRONG tier: core/src/config/mod.rs:2089-2100 and config/requirements.rs at rust-v0.148.0, plus app-server/src/lib.rs:641-647, which is what turns a clamp into the configWarning notification we relay.
- **Native:** B5: codex enforces most of it ITSELF — CODEX_REQUIREMENT_ENFORCEMENT records 20 codex / 3 inapplicable / 1 client over 25 entries, and most fields are enforced inside codex by Constrained<T>, which REPLACES a disallowed value with the required one and announces the substitution. So 'read, reported and discarded' describes the diagnostic, not the world. The surviving residue is not a missing mechanism but a TIMING one: our composed policy is built once at bootstrap while codex's answer arrives mid-session. B6: the carrier is host-side and shared (P2.408's push-or-defer operator-notice queue, wired by P3.120 046ca590f and P3.310 c027de1a9), but the PRODUCER is codex's own configWarning and claude's own forced-mode line — Pi has NO counterpart producer, measured, so Pi's cell is a no-claim about something that may not exist to claim. B34: the marketplace half is native to us (ref-pinned registries plus extraKnownMarketplaces delivery, L13/P6.40 21664a82b); only the per-GROUP assignment is out of reach, and it belongs to a managed-settings DELIVERY tier this fork does not have. The honest sentence landed as P6.248 (4e5fd2f3c).
- **Why absent today:** None of the three is a defect and none is deferred. B5 carries three named residues in code rather than hidden: the bootstrap-vs-mid-session timing, the reasoning effort REPORTED not applied because this build records no explicit-vs-inherited bit for the thinking level, and channelsEnabled/allowedChannelServers which are a FOURTH class this vocabulary cannot state — measured, and NEITHER enforcement pole is true (nothing reads them on claude, and nothing can push there either), so calling them not-enforced would be a false alarm pointed the dangerous way. B6's Pi cell was never filled because nobody measured whether Pi degrades a setting to satisfy policy at all. B34's per-group half needs a delivery tier that does not exist.
- **Options:**
  1. **(a) Record all three as covered-with-a-named-residue, give B6's Pi cell n/a with the reason 'no degradation producer exists on this runtime', and DECLINE to mint the fifth verdict word B5's measurement asked for** (size XS) — Six cells disposition without code, and the vocabulary stays at seven values. Cost: B5's channelsEnabled pair keeps an unmeasured marker whose reason is a paragraph rather than a word, which the ledger's own preamble already calls a weakness of the vocabulary rather than of the cells.
  2. **(b) Build the two closures: mid-session recomposition of the managed policy for codex, and a Pi degradation producer** (size M each) — Closes the timing residue an administrator can actually hit (a codex session running under a policy answer that arrived after bootstrap). Cost: respawning the app-server or a second composition pass, and inventing a Pi producer for degradations Pi may never perform — which is the tier-3 build native-first puts last.
  3. **(c) Mint a fifth verdict value for 'measured, and neither enforcement pole is true'** (size XS in code, product posture in effect) — Makes B5's fourth class sayable everywhere. Cost: it changes the admin surface and the scorer's vocabulary, which P6.747 just finished pinning to exactly seven words.
- **Reader's recommendation:** (a). Balance rule: simplest host coverage and no new vocabulary — the ledger already treats a partial with a stated residue as honest, and (c) is a product posture the ledger should not decide for itself. Pi's n/a in B6 is the one real judgement here and it is stated, not assumed: no producer was found on Pi, and 'not documented, not in the bundle, not in the source' is a finding, not an absence to build against.
- **Question (RU):** Записываем ли B5, B6 и B34 как покрытые-с-названным-остатком — для Pi в B6 ставим n/a с причиной «продюсера деградации на этом рантайме нет» — и отказываемся вводить пятое слово в словарь вердиктов? [да]

- **Refuter's correction:** BROKEN ON (d) — the recommendation mints a verdict word while its own option (c) refuses to mint one, and it inflates coverage by doing so.

Evidence that broke it: the ledger's own `## Cell vocabulary` publishes exactly five verdict values — `covered`, `partial`, `absent`, `n/a`, `—` — and the section closing the vocabulary tables states "**These seven are the whole vocabulary, and `plan-status.py` now says so out loud (P6.747, 2026-09-08).** An eighth word invented in a cell used to fall through to `no-claim` in silence." There is no `covered-with-a-named-residue`. QB7's coverage_gain says "6 cells move from partial/no-verdict to covered-with-residue (5)" — that is a sixth verdict value, taken by accident, in the same question whose option (c) declines to add a fifth switch word on the ground that the vocabulary was just pinned. The ledger's existing name for "some documented clauses hold, others do not; the row says which" is `partial`, which B5's two cells and B34's three already carry. So the correct action on those five cells is **no change at all**, and the coverage claim is not available.

The underlying measurements are good and should be kept: B5 is `covered ʰ` / `partial ʰ` / `partial ᵗ`, B6 Pi is `—` with Claude and Codex `covered ᵗ`, B34 is `partial ʰ` ×3; the B5 Why cell already carries the CODEX_REQUIREMENT_ENFORCEMENT 20/3/1-of-25 correction and the `Constrained<T>` substitution reading; B6's Why cell already frames the row as "CARRIER vs PRODUCER"; P6.248 is ✅ `4e5fd2f3c`; every cited anchor resolves (server-managed-settings#security-approval-dialogs, #approval-memory; settings-reference#fastmode).

Second break, on (c) of the brief: B6's Pi `n/a` rests on an ABSENCE claim — "Pi has NO counterpart producer, measured" — with no positive control named anywhere in the question or in the B6 Why cell, against AGENTS.md's standing rule "An ABSENCE finding needs a positive control on the same rig." The vendored `@earendil-works` packages are the corpus that claim has to be made over, and neither the question nor the cell says it was.

CORRECTED QUESTION: «Оставляем ли B5 и B34 как `partial` с уже записанным остатком — без изменения ячеек и без нового слова в словаре — и ставим ли Pi-ячейке B6 `n/a` только после того, как отказ будет назван вместе с положительным контролем на том же стенде (вендорённые `@earendil-works`), а не как неподтверждённое отсутствие? [да]» Coverage gain honestly: 0 cells move now; 1 cell (B6 Pi) moves from no-verdict to n/a once the probe and its control are recorded.

- **Rows:** B35, B36
- **Cells:** B35 no verdict ×3; B36 no verdict ×3 — 6 cells, the only NO-CLAIM cells in this family (klass MEASURE, size unrecorded)
- **Docs:** admin-setup.md#decide-how-settings-reach-devices; settings-reference.md#availablemodels, #fallbackmodel, #modelpicker; and decisively managed-settings.md#compose-every-managed-source, whose merge table 'names every key in three of the rows: restriction allowlists, values taken whole, and keys read from the highest-ranked source only' and which states 'Requires Claude Code v2.1.242 or later'. All curl-verified 2026-09-11.
- **Native:** THE VERSION CHANGED UNDER THE MEASUREMENT. P6.250 (f67d1e3a6) ran against the vendored 2.1.233; the bundle at HEAD is 2.1.247 — read the two agreeing self-reports, package.json claudeCodeVersion '2.1.247' and ./claude --version '2.1.247 (Claude Code)', never a grep of the binary. So the v2.1.242 floor for the documented merge semantics is now MET, and managedSourcesBehavior scores bin=8 / sdk=6 in that bundle against a nonsense control of 0. Our own side is winner-takes-all across the two admin sources (managed-composition.ts, first source delivering a policy key wins), and the CROSS-SCOPE behaviour of availableModels / fallbackModel / modelPicker — managed vs project vs user — is stated nowhere in our code: managed-policy.ts:782 and :788 declare availableModels and enforceAvailableModels with no merge/replace note at all.
- **Why absent today:** Not absent — never claimed, which the ledger treats as its own state ('a null is not an absence'). P6.250 LANDED and reported that both premises were refuted: 'the object of four booleans B36 feared is on no page'. But nobody wrote the resulting verdict into the cells, and the measurement itself now predates the version at which the page's merge semantics arrive. B36's second half is real regardless of version: managed-policy.ts:457 declares strictPluginOnlyCustomization as a TYPE only, so if our parser rejects a shape an administrator writes, the per-surface lock degrades to a silent drop while reading as applied — the exact class this ledger exists to catch.
- **Options:**
  1. **(a) Write P6.250's measured verdict into the six cells as it stands** (size XS) — Six no-claims become claims immediately. Cost: they would be claims about 2.1.233 recorded against a 2.1.247 bundle, on precisely the surface whose contract changed at 2.1.242 — the shape of error this repo's own version rule exists to prevent.
  2. **(b) Re-run the P6.250 rig against 2.1.247 first (the existing managed-policy.test.ts scope harness, control = a valid array which must produce no drop), then write the verdicts** (size S (one rig re-run, no new rig)) — The verdicts rest on the version we actually ship, and the re-run also answers whether the newly-reachable merge table changes which source supplies availableModels. Cost: one lane-hour before six cells can move.
  3. **(c) Leave both at no-verdict** (size XS) — Six cells stay outside the target permanently, on a contract whose failure mode is a value dropped or replaced while the lock reads as applied.
- **Reader's recommendation:** (b) then (a). Balance rule: the documentation is the denominator for what we must ACCEPT and it moved at 2.1.242; the rig already exists, so this is the least code that produces a verdict which will not have to be retaken at the next lift.
- **Question (RU):** Перегоняем ли рig P6.250 против уже стоящего 2.1.247 (порог v2.1.242 для документированной семантики merge теперь взят) и только после этого записываем шесть ячеек B35/B36, вместо того чтобы переносить вердикт, снятый на 2.1.233? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 6 cells move from no-verdict to covered or n/a-by-decision after one rig re-run.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** BROKEN ON (c) — its stated premise is false at HEAD, and the version argument does not reach either row.

First break. QB8 states "managed-policy.ts:457 declares `strictPluginOnlyCustomization` as a TYPE only, so if our parser rejects a shape an administrator writes, the per-surface lock degrades to a silent drop while reading as applied." At HEAD the declaration is at `packages/shared/src/agent/pi-harness/managed-policy.ts:533` and it HAS a reader: `strictCustomizationSurfaces` (:1861-1890), called at :2830, with `isStrictCustomizationSurfaceLocked` at :3517. That function already implements the page's documented Type verbatim. settings-reference.md#strictpluginonlycustomization gives "**Type**: `true` to lock all four kinds of customization, or an array naming the kinds to lock, from `"skills"`, `"agents"`, `"hooks"`, and `"mcp"`"; the code reads `value === true → STRICT_CUSTOMIZATION_SURFACES`, `false` and empty array → no lock, an unrecognised surface NAME ignored (its docblock quoting the page's own sentence "Surface names that a Claude Code version doesn't recognize are ignored rather than failing the settings file"), and a malformed entry dropped with the drop recorded. The four dotted page entries `strictPluginOnlyCustomization.skills/.agents/.hooks/.mcp` each resolve as their own anchor but each declares "**Type**: the string `"agents"` in the `strictPluginOnlyCustomization` array" — array members, not object keys, which confirms P6.250's "the object of four booleans is on no page" and simultaneously removes the reason to re-measure it.

Second break. The v2.1.242 floor governs `managedSourcesBehavior`, which composes the four sources the vendor's OWN binary reads — that is B32's subject, not B35's. B35 is our cross-scope composition through `managed-composition.ts` ("first source delivering a policy key wins") and B36 is our parser against a page Type; neither is a function of the vendored bundle version. Probed: managed-settings.md carries five "Requires Claude Code v2.1.242" occurrences plus 2.1.259/2.1.261/2.1.267, and the bundle self-reports 2.1.247 both ways — the floor is met, but it is met for a key neither of these rows reads.

Verified and unchanged: B35 and B36 are `—` ×3, the only no-claim cells in the family; P6.250 is ✅ `f67d1e3a6` and its tail reads "**Landed 2026-09-06 (W7-d):** both premises refuted — the 'object of four booleans' B36 feared is on no page"; all cited anchors resolve.

CORRECTED RECOMMENDATION: (a) for B36 now, no rig, no re-run — the row's own control ("a valid array, which must produce no drop") plus the `true` and malformed arms are all readable in `strictCustomizationSurfaces` at HEAD, so B36's three cells take P6.250's refutation directly. For B35, run only the measurement its row already specifies — disjoint `availableModels` at managed and project scope, control `permissions.allow` which must MERGE, repeated for `fallbackModel` and `modelPicker` — and note that it prices OUR composition, so it does not have to be retaken at the next SDK lift.

CORRECTED QUESTION: «Записываем ли три ячейки B36 прямо сейчас по опровержению P6.250 (документированная форма — `true` или массив, и `strictCustomizationSurfaces` уже читает обе, так что «только тип» — устаревшая посылка), и прогоняем ли для B35 только межскоупный замер, который описан в самой строке, не привязывая его к порогу v2.1.242 — он относится к `managedSourcesBehavior` (B32), а не к этим двум строкам? [да]»

### QB9 — Tool search kept on by policy — the row moved to another plan; do its cells still count against THIS ledger

- **Rows:** B23
- **Cells:** B23 Pi absent / Claude absent / Codex n/a — 2 uncovered cells (klass BUILDABLE, size S)
- **Docs:** agent-sdk/tool-search.md (curl 200, 2026-09-11) line 49 — the mechanism is the managed override of CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_BETAS, not a standalone tool-search key; llm-gateway-protocol.md:147-153, and specifically :152, which says that with the override in place a gateway set via ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL keeps receiving tool_reference blocks — the coupling our adapter must survive.
- **Native:** CORRECTED in the row itself: the page's version floor (v2.1.227) IS met by our bundle, and now comfortably so at 2.1.247. Our own two switches are NESSY_ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH and workspaceDefaults.deferredMcpToolLoading — both developer-owned — and no tool-search key exists in the managed policy (0 hits, against classifyAllShell 11 and autoMode 18 in the same file as controls). tool-search-settings.ts:35-45 records that every platform fallback including CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_BETAS is downstream of a wire dependency our client-side mechanism does not have. Codex is n/a: it has no tool-search surface.
- **Why absent today:** THIS IS THE ONE ROW IN FAMILY B THAT CARRIES A 2026-09-10 DISPOSITION, and it is not a deferral to nowhere. P6.241's note reads: 'Deferred 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 2): → the follow-up plan (P7.7), verdicts intact', then 'Moved 2026-09-10 (P7.7 landing): → docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md row D1.36, verdicts intact. Closed HERE as moved, not as done.' D1.36 exists in that plan at ⬜ and is listed there among the four still-open host builds. The work is also sequence-blocked in a way that must survive the move: parseManagedPiPolicyV1 drops unknown keys, so the managed KEY must land before any reader — the row's original sequence was INVERTED and corrected by W7-u.
- **Options:**
  1. **(a) Keep the verdicts absent, mark the two cells 'moved to D1.36', and exclude them from THIS ledger's 95-100 % denominator** (size XS) — The target is measured over work this plan owns. Cost: the ledger vocabulary has no 'moved' value, so the exclusion lives in prose beside a cell that still reads absent — a shape this repo has been bitten by before, when a verdict written in an undefined word scored as silence.
  2. **(b) Count them against this ledger and pull D1.36 back into scope** (size S (the key plus the reader, in the corrected order)) — Two cells close and an organisation can actually keep tool search on. Cost: it reverses a 2026-09-10 owner ruling for two cells, and the row's own coupling (a gateway keeps receiving tool_reference blocks) means the adapter arm must be proven in the same pass.
  3. **(c) Count them as absent and leave the target short by two cells** (size XS) — Fully honest and costs nothing, at the price of never reaching 100 % in this family while a row that is merely relocated reads as a gap.
- **Reader's recommendation:** (a). Balance rule: the owner's own 2026-09-10 ruling moved the row with its verdicts intact, and a coverage target should be measured over the plan that owns the work. Make the exclusion machine-visible rather than prose-only — the cell should say 'moved: D1.36' in the same place the scorer reads, so it cannot be mistaken later for an unmeasured silence.
- **Question (RU):** Исключаем ли две ячейки B23 из знаменателя этого ledger'а как перенесённые решением от 2026-09-10 в план D1.36 (вердикты сохранены), пометив перенос там же, где считает скорер, а не только в прозе? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 2 cells leave the denominator as moved-by-decision; nothing is claimed as covered.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** UPHELD IN SUBSTANCE, broken on one fact — and this is the slot for the family's orphan cells.

What verifies. B23 is `absent ʰ` / `absent ʰ` / `n/a ʰ`; P6.241 is ✅ `802de0622` and reads verbatim "**Deferred 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 2): → the follow-up plan (P7.7), verdicts intact.** **Moved 2026-09-10 (P7.7 landing): → `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md` row `D1.36`, verdicts intact.** Closed HERE as moved, not as done." D1.36 exists in that plan at ⬜. agent-sdk/tool-search.md returns 200. The sequence inversion (parseManagedPiPolicyV1 drops unknown keys, so the key precedes the reader) is in the row. Recommendation (a) is right, and its insistence that the exclusion be machine-visible is right for the reason the ledger's own vocabulary section gives — an eighth word "used to fall through to `no-claim` in silence", and `moved` is not one of the five verdict values.

The fact that broke it: "THIS IS THE ONE ROW IN FAMILY B THAT CARRIES A 2026-09-10 DISPOSITION" is false. `P3.7` carries the identical one — "Moved 2026-09-10 (P7.7 landing): → …row `D1.96`, verdicts intact. Closed HERE as moved, not as done." — and B10's parked Windows evidence day depends on it (see the QB6 correction). So the `moved:` marker must be designed for TWO customers, not one, and applied to B10's park pointer in the same pass. Also stale-but-harmless: the B23 ledger cell still reads "its version floor (v2.1.227) IS met by our 2.1.233 bundle"; the bundle is 2.1.247, which only strengthens the cell.

ORPHAN CELLS — the answer to (f), and it is not small. On the runtime axis the nine clusters are complete: every `partial`/`absent`/`—` cell of B3, B5, B6, B9–B32, B34, B35, B36 is claimed by some cluster, and the rows in no cluster (B1, B2, B4, B7, B8, B33, B37, B38) are `covered` ×3 or `n/a` ×3, which `plan-status.py` disposes (Pi 184+133+55+217+43 = 632, remainder 405 excludes n/a). But **the Switch column is untouched by all nine questions**: B9 through B38 — thirty rows — every one reads `` `—` ``, and B8's is a deliberate null. Those cells are counted in the ledger's own denominator (`plan-status.py` reports Host 519 no-verdict of 632, ИТОГО 2528 cells) and they sit in its remainder. Two rows are orphaned entirely, having no cluster and no runtime gap but a no-verdict Switch cell each: **B37 and B38** — both `covered ᵗ` ×3 with Switch `` `—` ``. **B33** is likewise in no cluster (`n/a` ×3, Switch `` `—` ``). A ten-question set that promotes up to ~70 runtime cells and leaves 30 of 38 Switch cells unmeasured cannot reach the family's coverage target, and per AGENTS.md's native-first rule the Switch verdict is the half that makes a divergence honest rather than silent.

CORRECTED QUESTION: «Исключаем ли две ячейки B23 из знаменателя этого ledger'а как перенесённые решением от 2026-09-10 в план D1.36 (вердикты сохранены) — пометкой `moved:` там же, где считает скорер, а не только в прозе, — и применяем ли ту же пометку ко второму такому же случаю, P3.7 → D1.96, от которого зависит припаркованный windows-день B10? И отдельно: заводим ли строку на тридцать неизмеренных ячеек колонки Switch (B9–B38, включая полностью осиротевшие B33, B37, B38), которых не касается ни один из девяти кластеров? [да]» Coverage gain: 2 cells leave the denominator as moved-by-decision; the Switch orphans are named rather than claimed.

- **Options:**
  1. **Re-derivation lane — one pass over the ten rows, each cell re-derived against the named commit and its done_means, marker ᵗ where a commit is the evidence** (size S–M) — 22 cells leave the non-covered column with zero new code; the family's reading stops under-reporting work already shipped and paid for
  2. **Leave the cells as they stand** (size XS) — family C keeps reporting roughly a third of its cells as gaps that are closed; the next reader re-dispatches lanes for work that landed five days ago — the exact failure the ledger exists to prevent

It is the balance rule at its cheapest: nothing is invented, every cell moves against a landed commit and a page anchor that is already quoted in the plan row, no code is written, and the host coverage is already uniform because the host was the seam in every one of these rows. It is also the only option that makes the owner's "with refusals counted" reading honest — three of the ten rows (C7 codex, C11-adjacent C16 codex boundary, C24) resolve to n/a rather than covered, and a re-derivation is what separates those.

(1) C14 is ALREADY re-derived and must not be promoted. Its cell in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-28-capability-ledger.md carries, verbatim: "VERDICT RE-DERIVED 2026-09-08 (P6.890, from the sources above and nothing else): pi `absent` -> `partial`", and it names P6.257 by id. A scan of all 30 family-C rows for that phrase returns exactly one hit — C14 — so the premise "none of them is named anywhere in the ledger / the cells still carry the state before this work" holds for nine rows and is false for the tenth. (2) The row list is short by one plan row and one ledger row. P6.483 (`b44af523f`, closed) landed the owner's Q13 option-3 override and scores 0 hits in the ledger. The sitting question this comment used to record is answered: `DO_NOT_TRACK=1` DOES silence a claude child." So the lane is THIRTEEN plan rows over ELEVEN ledger rows: add P6.483 and row C3 (see QC5, which mis-files it as owed to P7.7).

### QC2 — OS-level network containment — refused by the standing isolation decision, and the refusal is already built and disclosed

- **Rows:** C6, C8, C9, C26
- **Cells:** C6 Pi absent / claude absent / codex partial; C8 Pi no-verdict / claude no-verdict / codex absent; C9 Pi absent / claude absent / codex partial; C26 codex absent (Pi and claude n/a) — 10 non-covered cells
- **Docs:** curl-verified 2026-09-11: admin-setup.md#decide-what-to-enforce, verbatim — "Permission rules and sandboxing cover different layers. Denying WebFetch blocks Claude's fetch tool, but if Bash is allowed, curl and wget can still reach any URL. Sandboxing closes that gap with a network domain allowlist enforced at the OS level." (C6). sandboxing.md#network-isolation, verbatim — "Network access is controlled through a proxy server running outside the sandbox" — plus sandboxing.md#custom-proxy-configuration, which is where httpProxyPort/socksProxyPort are specified, and settings-reference.md#sandbox-network-strictallowlist and #sandbox-network-tlsterminate (C8, C9). For C26 the codex side is source/binary tier, not page tier.
- **Native:** claude — CONFIRMED native and now measured unmeetable: all eight transport keys live in the SDK's own sandbox tier and are present in the vendored bundle (strictAllowlist → 3, tlsTerminate → 1, allowMachLookup → 1, controls Cookie → 5 and git-subdir → 4), our projection writes deniedDomains / allowManagedDomainsOnly / allowedDomains and none of the eight, and P6.253 measured that the SDK's own filter copies 1 of the 8 — so even a correct projection would not arrive. codex — genuinely narrower and measured at HEAD on the shipped codex-app-server (178 126 224 B, rust-v0.148.0): a BOOLEAN network_access under [sandbox_workspace_write] which our config writer already writes, plus (deny default) in the seatbelt profile, plus network_proxy → 337 and allow_local_binding → 10 in the binary against control CODEX_HOME → 36; source tier (github.com/openai/codex, strong) says Feature::NetworkProxy is Experimental/default-off and its gate requires network_sandbox_policy().is_enabled(), i.e. a no-op exactly where it is wanted and a live egress regression in danger-full-access. Pi — none, and nothing is contained under our wrapper today, but the STRICT branch exists and was measured to contain, so it is a switched-off control rather than an impossibility.
- **Why absent today:** DECISION, twice over, and both are the owner's. (1) The standing decision of 2026-08-12/13 keeps isolation OFF and records it as a posture, not a gap; sitting §1.1 is the named revisit trigger and its measurement table is already before the owner (SEATBELT_TESTING_WILDCARD=true: writes outside cwd/home succeed on the shipped arm and are refused on the flip). (2) C26's enclosing question was DECIDED 2026-09-05 by the owner, by recommendation, in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-ledger-closure-owner-questions.md — option A, "egress stays denied and the denial is made LEGIBLE; B stays conditional on sitting §1.1/§3.1, and C is not taken on the schema alone" — so the private-address exception C26 asks for has no subject under the answer already given. C8's refusal is not a shrug either: it was measured against the codex source and the deciding argument is written at the site — a network denial never becomes a protocol event, so its verdict could not mint an audit receipt, and AGENTS.md's rule is that an unauditable influence on permission outcomes is worse than an absent one. honest_surface is present on all four: IsolationNetworkPosture.tsx, apps/cli/src/doctor.ts (sandboxEgress + source), formatCodexSandboxEgressDiagnostics (one diagnostic per writeCodexConfig naming both refused switches and why, covered by config-writer-sandbox-egress.test.ts), and a parser that throws by name on each of the eight keys. None of the four rows carries Deferred 2026-09-10 or Release sitting 2026-09-10.
- **Options:**

It is closest to the pages in the only sense that matters here — the pages describe a mechanism whose enclosing feature this product deliberately does not ship, and the pages themselves say what is lost (the admin-setup sentence above is the loss, stated by the vendor). It is the least code, the host coverage is already uniform because all three runtimes are equally uncontained-or-deny-default and all three disclose it, and it re-decides nothing: two owner rulings already point this way and a third would be a lane widening the intranet boundary by one line, which the ledger explicitly warns against.

- **Refuter's correction:** The refusal is right; the DISPOSITION is not, and it would inflate the family's score.

(1) "n/a-by-decision" is an invented eighth word. The ledger's own §"Cell vocabulary" defines `n/a` as "Not this runtime's surface — the capability is meaningless here, with a reason" and `absent` as "The user cannot do it, and we know that"; DECISION is a value of the SEPARATE "Why absent" column ("An owner or engineering call with a recorded reason. Reversible — argue with the reason"). The string "n/a-by-decision" occurs 0 times in the ledger. The same section warns in as many words: "Before inventing a value, check whether one of the seven already means it."

(2) The relabel is not cosmetic — it moves the denominator. scripts/plan-status.py line 308 reads `denom = total - c['n/a']`, with the comment "n/a is resolved, not owed", while `absent` stays in `rest`. Ten cells absent->n/a would raise family C's coverage percentage with no capability changing and the shell still uncontained. (3) Family C already contains the correct precedent, twice. (4) C26 is not disposed of by the 2026-09-05 answer. Its own cell ends, verbatim: "No prior ruling found." Also, that line reads "B stays conditional on sitting §3.1" — not "§1.1/§3.1".

What verified: all four honest surfaces exist (IsolationNetworkPosture.tsx; `sandboxEgress`/`sandboxEgressSource` in apps/cli/src/doctor.ts; formatCodexSandboxEgressDiagnostics in config-writer-permissions.ts/config-writer.ts/codex-agent.ts; config-writer-sandbox-egress.test.ts); admin-setup.md#decide-what-to-enforce and sandboxing.md#network-isolation carry the quoted sentences verbatim; the codex binary is 178 126 224 B with allow_local_binding present and controls live.
