### QC3 — A credential helper that runs a command — refused as a class since 2026-08-08, with one scope left unruled

- **Rows:** C11
- **Cells:** C11 Pi absent / claude absent / codex absent — 3 non-covered cells
- **Docs:** curl-verified 2026-09-11: llm-gateway-connect.md#rotate-credentials-with-apikeyhelper (heading present, page 200, 56 755 B) and authentication.md#authentication-precedence, whose #restrict-login-to-your-organization section states verbatim that sessions authenticated by "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN, or apiKeyHelper" are blocked at startup when login enforcement is set — i.e. the vendor treats the helper as one of three peer credential sources.
- **Native:** claude HAS it and it is structurally unreachable for us: apiKeyHelper → 65 grep-lines in the shipped native binary (control CLAUDE_CODE_NOT_A_REAL_NAME → 0) plus CLAUDE_CODE_API_KEY_HELPER_TTL_MS → 7, and 4 / 1 occurrences respectively in sdk.mjs, sitting at precedence tier 4 — while our claude path always writes tier-3 ANTHROPIC_API_KEY into the child (the loopback adapter's 'not-needed' literal), so tier 3 always outranks tier 4 and a helper an operator configures in their own settings is read and can never be consulted. codex has no provider-side helper: ModelProviderInfo offers env_key and experimental_bearer_token, and the only http_headers_helper is on the MCP block; cli_auth_credentials_store and features.secret_auth_storage are storage backends, not a mint-on-demand command. Pi: 0. So tier 1 is answered NO on two runtimes and unreachable on the third; tier 2 (the host once) is what the refusal is about.
- **Why absent today:** DECISION. R50, owner 2026-08-08, "out of scope for this product", extended to apiKeyHelper in packages/shared/src/config/managed-env.ts — verbatim: "this product does not accept an admin-supplied executable as a settings source … the two helper keys here differ from policyHelper only in what the executable's output is used for … it is not built, not deferred, and not a gap". The honesty half landed 2026-09-06 as P6.254 (72c8154c6): setting the helper in any scope yields one operator-visible line naming the ruling, never a value, and removing the line fails a test. What the ledger records as still open is narrow and precise: the ruling was made for ADMIN scope and the USER scope was recorded as unruled. Not deferred and not on the release sitting.
- **Options:**
  1. **A. Extend the refusal to every scope and say so — the diagnostic already built simply stops distinguishing scopes** (size XS) — a user who wants a vault-backed rotating credential must paste a static key into the connection card instead; the refusal is one line they can read, and the reason (this product runs no admin-or-user-supplied executable as a settings source) is the same one that dropped policyHelper
  2. **B. Allow user scope only, TTL-cached** (size M) — re-opens an executable-as-settings path — on one runtime of three, because Pi and codex have no counterpart, which manufactures exactly the silent divergence native-first exists to prevent; and it re-decides R50 for the half the owner did not exclude
  3. **C. Option B plus an OS-keychain read with no shell** (size L) — the safest shape of the capability and the most code: a keychain integration per platform, a new credential lifecycle, and still only on the runtimes where we control the credential seam
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option A. The nearest documented solution is the vendor's own apiKeyHelper, and it is measurably unreachable through our loopback adapter — so "enable what the runtime already has" is answered and answered NO. Least code by a wide margin (the sentence exists), simplest host coverage (identical on all three runtimes), and it closes the one scope the 2026-08-08 ruling left open rather than inventing a mechanism where the documentation is silent about our shape.
- **Question (RU):** Распространяем ли мы отказ R50 («внешняя команда не может быть источником настроек») и на пользовательский scope, закрывая C11 как «отказано по решению» на всех трёх рантаймах? [рекомендуется: да]
- **Coverage gain:** 3 cells move from absent to n/a-by-decision, with the residue stated: credential rotation stays manual, and the operator-visible line naming the ruling is the whole surface.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** The question to the owner is sound and un-ruled — R50 (2026-08-08) covers admin scope and the C11 cell says verbatim "user scope unruled", with the three options (A) extend, (B) user-scope TTL-cached, (C) plus OS keychain, exactly as listed. The managed-env.ts reasoning is real (packages/shared/src/config/managed-env.ts, the docblock ending "So the exception is not built, not deferred, and not a gap"). Two things break the write-up.

(1) Same disposition error as QC2: "3 cells move from absent to n/a-by-decision" cannot be executed. `n/a` means "Not this runtime's surface", and claude's surface is exactly where apiKeyHelper lives — the row's whole point is that the vendor HAS it and our tier-3 write makes it unreachable. C11 must stay `absent` x3 with the Why-absent reason `DECISION`, which is what it already does under R50; the coverage_gain is zero cells, and the deliverable is the reason text plus the widened diagnostic, not a verdict change.

P6.9's plan row records: DEFERRED TO LAST BY OWNER RULING (2026-08-28) — «этого не будет, я бы не стал сейчас это делать», then «я бы сместил это аж в самую последнюю очередь» — "Deferred to the very end of the queue. P6.79 (d82df5e52) then moved that announcement off the NESSY_DEBUG=1 channel, where a packaged build emitted it nowhere, onto the latched operator-notice queue — which is C28's deliverable on the one arm that has a subject. The shipped route is on-demand recovery through a named pointer: `HANDOFF_IMAGE_NOTE` (`packages/server-core/src/sessions/runtime-handoff.ts`), which since P3.280 (`823bf4f6c`) states the condition per MODEL — "A model that accepts image input receives it there as visual content, not raw bytes; a model that does not is offered no way to open it." P2.125 (replay a structured image block, omnigent-style) is refused at the seam on three independent grounds. One residue only: P6.93 ⬜, the codex `view_image` live case, carrying "Release sitting 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 5): gates 1.36.0 — P7.5".

Both score covered, so the accurate word costs nothing; assert one only with the argument in the cell. Two things to carry with the answer: (a) `REPORTED` on D5/D6 is partial in a second sense the cell should name — `switchCostCertainty` never fires a row whose SOURCE claim is `'conditional'`, so a `REPORTED` verdict describes only the legs the table actually fires on; (b) ORPHAN CHECK — none. Family D has exactly seven rows (ledger lines 1741-1747); every non-covered cell is in a cluster: D1 ×3 (QD1), D3 ×3 (QD2), D4 ×3 (QD3), D5/claude (QD4), D6/pi+claude (QD5), D5/D6/D7 Switch (this question). Corrected question: «D5/D6/D7: проставить `REPORTED`, `REPORTED` и — для D7 — выбрать между `HOST` и `HONEST`, аргументировав выбор прямо в клетке: словарь определяет `HOST` как «переключение ничего не меняет», а массив `capabilities` меняется с трёх значений на `[]`, что по собственному тексту клетки и есть честная деградация; оба слова считаются как covered, так что точность здесь бесплатна. Плюс отметить, что `REPORTED` описывает только те плечи, на которых строка вообще срабатывает («conditional» в источнике не срабатывает никогда). [Да]»


## Family E — The context window

### QE1 — Codex owns its own auto-compaction: no off-switch exists in the source, and "when" can only be lowered and never observed

- **Rows:** E1, E5 (codex arm)
- **Cells:** E1: Pi covered / Claude covered / **Codex absent ᵗ**; E1 Host `REPORTED ᵗ` = partial. E5 codex `partial ᵗ`. 3 non-covered cells.
- **Docs:** Contract for the capability, Claude side: `settings-reference.md#autocompactenabled` (anchor curl-verified 2026-09-11 on the rendered page) — verbatim: *"`DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT` turns auto-compact off for one session; whichever of the two turns it off, the other can't turn it back on"*. Codex side the evidence is the STRONG tier, not the page: `codex-rs/config/src/config_toml.rs` at `rust-v0.150.1` fetched 2026-09-11 (HTTP 200, 39 309 bytes) carries exactly five compaction keys — `model_context_window` (:165), `model_auto_compact_token_limit` (:168), `model_auto_compact_token_limit_scope` (:172, NEW since 0.148), `compact_prompt` (:249), `experimental_compact_prompt_file` (:516) — and no off-switch of any spelling; `codex-rs/models-manager/src/model_info.rs:169-171` still carries `context_window: Some(272_000)` with `auto_compact_token_limit: None`.
- **Native:** Tier 1 asked and answered on all three. **Pi:** its own resolver, honours the off-switch. **Claude:** native `DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT`, projected onto the SDK's own env rather than fought with (`buildClaudeAutoCompactionEnv`, `ed58d8488`). **Codex: no native mechanism exists** — behaviour-proven on the shipped binary (`da6ad0281`, two-arm rig with two negative controls): `auto_compact_token_limit()` takes a `min`, so the config key can only LOWER the trigger and `i64::MAX` buys nothing; withholding both keys does not disable anything, it substitutes codex's fabricated 272 000 and fires at 90 % of it. Re-read at `rust-v0.150.1` today: still no key. **Tier 2 (host) is already built:** `apps/electron/src/renderer/components/app-shell/input/runtime-switch-costs.ts` carries `autoCompactionOff` (`pi: true, anthropic: true, codex-runtime: false`) and `autoCompactionKnobs` (same shape), plus the per-session transcript diagnostic.
- **Why absent today:** STRUCTURAL, measured rather than assumed. The ledger's own words: *"On codex a user cannot turn auto-compaction off. Behaviour-proven; the cell stays `absent ᵗ` because the vocabulary has no stronger marker to move it to."* P3.234 and P3.286 are both ✅ (`63d9c32e6`, `da6ad0281`); no open plan row targets this, and it carries neither "Deferred 2026-09-10" nor "Release sitting 2026-09-10".

  1. **Refuse and record — rule the codex arm n/a-by-decision: codex owns its own compaction, and the loss is told twice (switch report + session transcript)** (size XS) — A user on codex still cannot turn compaction off; nothing changes for them except that the ledger stops calling a measured runtime ceiling an open defect. One cell leaves the denominator.
  2. **Build a host ceiling — stop or warn the turn before codex's own 90 % trigger** (size M) — A second authority over one number, and it can only end turns rather than stop compaction — the same shape E4 refused on claude once the SDK's native breaker was found at the same threshold. It would also have to be re-derived at every codex lift.
  3. **Defer to the 0.150.1 lift and re-read the source afterwards** (size XS) — The three cells stay non-covered through the release; the 0.150.1 config surface has already been read today and it adds `..._scope`, not an off-switch, so the re-read is very likely to return the same answer.

Native-first step 1 was asked and answered (no mechanism at 0.148 or 0.150.1), step 2 is already delivered (both switch-cost rows plus the transcript diagnostic), so what remains is a record, not code. The balance rule prefers an honest stated residue to a second authority over codex's own number.

CORRECTED RECOMMENDATION: option 1's SUBSTANCE (refuse the host ceiling by name, record the residue) but not its bookkeeping — E1:codex stays `absent ᵗ` with the reason changed from DEFECT to `DECISION`, naming the 0.150.1 re-read and refusing option 2 by name so it is not re-proposed; E1:host stays `REPORTED ᵗ`; E5:codex stays `partial ᵗ`.

CORRECTED QUESTION: «Зафиксировать отсутствие выключателя авто-компакции в codex как ОСОЗНАННЫЙ ОТКАЗ — причина ячейки меняется с DEFECT на `DECISION` (источник rust-v0.150.1 такого ключа не содержит; хост-потолок отклонён поимённо), при этом вердикт остаётся `absent ᵗ`, потому что легенда реестра (строка 876) определяет `n/a` как «не поверхность этого рантайма», а выключение авто-компакции — именно поверхность codex? CORRECTED COVERAGE GAIN: 0 cells move. All three non-covered cells stay where they are; what changes is that one of them stops reading as an open defect. QE1's claim that "one cell leaves the denominator" is the part to drop — inflating coverage by re-labelling a measured impossibility as "meaningless" is the exact move :1267 warns against.

### QE2 — Controlling WHEN compaction fires: the env projection is at the top of Claude's own precedence chain — is that the whole answer, or do we owe the user the newly documented `/autocompact` surface?

- **Rows:** E5
- **Cells:** E5: **Pi partial ᵗ / Claude partial ᵗ** / Codex partial (QE1); E5 Host `SILENT` = absent — and that host cell is STALE. 3 non-covered cells.
- **Docs:** `settings-reference.md#autocompactwindow` (anchor curl-verified 2026-09-11) — verbatim: *"**Per-session overrides**: `--autocompact` takes precedence over this key for one session, and `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` takes precedence over both"*, type *"number of tokens, from `100000` to `1000000`"*. `model-config.md#set-the-auto-compact-window` (anchor curl-verified the same day) — verbatim: *"**In scripts and cloud environments**: set `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`. While it's set, it takes precedence over the command, the flag, and the setting"*. Both pages are NEWER than the ledger row, which knew only the env var.
- **Native:** **Claude tier 1, four channels, and we already hold the strongest one.** The page's precedence is env > flag > `/autocompact` command > `autoCompactWindow` setting; the host writes the env (`buildClaudeAutoCompactionKnobEnv`, `a4bfdaae4`). Probed today: `autoCompactWindow` → 0 files in `packages`+`apps`, 57 occurrences in the vendored `claude` binary (2.1.247, self-reported by both `--version` and the SDK manifest); `/autocompact` → 0 hits in `packages/shared/src/slash-commands`. **Pi:** `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@0.84.2` `dist/core/compaction/compaction.d.ts:29-31` `CompactionSettings { enabled; reserveTokens; keepRecentTokens }` — narrows to any value, no floor. **Codex:** downward-only (QE1). **Host tier 2 already built, and the ledger has not caught up:** P6.96 ✅ (`b4488e688` + `0b9242d41`) lifted the knob divergence from `debug` to the session banner (`claudeAutoCompactionKnobDiagnostics`, called at `packages/shared/src/agent/claude-agent.ts:4864`) and added the `autoCompactionKnobs` switch row naming all three behaviours in one sentence.
- **Why absent today:** The real residue is the documented floor: the SDK accepts 100 000–1 000 000 where Pi narrows from 1, so the same operator number binds differently — diagnosed on the transcript rather than smoothed. The host cell reads `SILENT` because the ledger predates P6.96; the mechanism it says is missing shipped. No E5 row is deferred or in the release sitting.
- **Options:**
  1. **Accept and record — refresh the host cell off P6.96's landed evidence and name the 100 000 floor as the stated residue; the knob stays operator-side** (size XS) — The operator's number binds on Pi and Claude and the difference is on screen twice; a *user* still cannot change the window from inside the app, which is a documented Claude affordance we do not offer.
  2. **Expose the user surface — an in-app `/autocompact <value|auto>` writing the same host setting, honouring the documented precedence and saying when a managed scope wins** (size S) — A fourth spelling of one number, and a command whose semantics differ per runtime (inert on codex, floored on claude, exact on Pi) — every one of those has to be said at the moment the command is used, or it becomes the advertised-but-cannot anti-pattern.
  3. **Defer the user surface, keep the operator knob** (size XS) — Same as option 1 minus the ledger refresh; the host cell keeps saying SILENT about a mechanism that ships, which is the exact staleness this family already paid for twice.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1, plus one sub-decision the owner should make explicitly: whether the E5 Switch verdict is `REPORTED` (told at the switch) or `HONEST` (the capability itself says so on the session's own transcript). P6.96 delivered BOTH halves, so `HONEST` is defensible and moves the host cell to covered; `REPORTED` is the conservative reading and leaves it partial. Option 2 is a product posture — who holds the knob, operator or user — and is stated, not decided by a lane.
- **Question (RU):** Принять текущую схему как ответ по E5 (env-проекция — верх документированной цепочки приоритетов Claude, порог 100 000 назван в транскрипте, строка о переключении есть) и обновить host-ячейку с `SILENT` на `HONEST`, не добавляя пользовательскую команду `/autocompact`? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** E5:host `absent` → `covered` if the Switch verdict is ruled `HONEST` (→ `partial` if `REPORTED`). E5:pi and E5:claude stay `partial` with the floor named — they reach `covered` only under option 2. Best case 1 of 3 cells to covered, with the other 2 explicitly accepted.
- **Refuter verdict:** upheld

### QE4 — The /context occupancy card: claude's arm has landed and only the vendor's "optimization suggestions" clause has no runtime twin; codex has no context method at all

- **Rows:** E6
- **Cells:** E6: Pi covered ʰ / **Claude partial ʰ (STALE) / Codex partial ʰ**; E6 Host `—`. 3 non-covered cells.
- **Docs:** Four pages, one capability, every anchor curl-verified on the rendered page 2026-09-11: `context-window.md#check-your-own-session` — verbatim *"run `/context` for a live breakdown by category with optimization suggestions, including which CLAUDE.md and auto memory files loaded"*; `debug-your-config.md#see-what-loaded-into-context`; `features-overview.md#context-cost-by-feature`; `agent-sdk/typescript.md#query-object` (plus `#sdkcontextusage` and `#sdkcontrolgetcontextusageresponse`, both real ids).
- **Native:** **Claude tier 1, and it is now READ.** At the vendored 0.3.247 (`claudeCodeVersion` 2.1.247, agreed by the binary's own `--version`): `Query.getContextUsage(): Promise<SDKControlGetContextUsageResponse>` at `sdk.d.ts:2571` (no argument) and `SDKAssistantMessage.context_usage?: SDKContextUsage` at `:3130`. P6.264 ✅ (`83aa07004 + 742bbcc4f + 00733b3dc`) shipped `ClaudeAgent.requestContextUsage` + the SDK→wire mapper, and `resolveContextUsageCapability` now returns `kind: 'fresh-local', refresh: true, thresholdKind: 'formula-claude'` for `anthropic` (re-read at HEAD in `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/composer-capabilities.ts:166-193`). **The one residue, measured today at 0.3.247:** the documented `SDKContextUsage` carries `model`, `total_tokens`, `raw_max_tokens`, `percentage`, `over_limit`, `categories`, `mcp_tools`, `memory_files`, `agents`, `skills` — and **no suggestions field**; `grep suggestion sdk.d.ts` returns only permission, prompt and file-mention suggestions. So the page's *optimization suggestions* clause has no vendor twin on the claude arm. **Codex: structural.** `thread/tokenUsage/updated` gives totals and `modelContextWindow`; there is no `thread/context/*` method in the generated client-request table, and the honest surface already ships — `kind: 'unavailable', reason: 'unsupported'` and `/context` withdrawn from the composer menu by `resolveExcludedCommandIds`.
- **Why absent today:** The claude cell is stale — it describes the amputated consumer signature that P6.264 replaced. The codex cell is a measured structural absence with the honest surface already built. Neither row is deferred nor in the release sitting.
- **Options:**
  1. **Record: claude → covered with the suggestions clause named as the stated residue; codex → n/a (structural, and the command is withdrawn rather than offered-and-refused); host Switch → HONEST** (size XS) — The card the user sees on claude is the vendor's own data; the one page clause we cannot serve is written down by name instead of being carried as an unexplained partial.
  2. **Build host-computed suggestions for the claude arm out of the categories we already receive (Pi's `context-categories.ts` is the shape)** (size M) — Our suggestions sit beside vendor-measured categories on the same card and are indistinguishable from them — advice we invented where the runtime is silent, on the one surface whose whole value is that it mirrors the vendor exactly.
  3. **Leave both cells partial** (size XS) — Two cells keep describing a defect that is fixed and an absence that is deliberate; the next sweep pays the same reading again.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1, and refuse option 2 by name. Where the documentation is silent the rule is transparent-and-simple, and "we do not compute suggestions; the runtime does not publish them" is the smallest honest thing. Option 2 would invent product advice on a card the user reads as the vendor's.

### QF3 — Retry — the operator's budget, the user's countdown, and the one route with no idle abort at all (three rows landed; the claude loopback stall is the live residue)

CLAUDE — TIER 1 for the budget (`CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_RETRIES` / `CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG` / `API_TIMEOUT_MS`, already honoured via `applyInteractiveRetryCeiling`), but NOT for the idle abort on OUR route: the SDK's byte watchdog requires the base URL to BE api.anthropic.com, which a loopback adapter never is, so `CLAUDE_ENABLE_BYTE_WATCHDOG` + `CLAUDE_STREAM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` cannot be armed here — measured, not inferred. HOST — the primitive exists and is wired on three of four backends: `shared/src/llm/stream-idle.ts` `readWithIdleTimeout` (300 000 ms default, clamped [10 000, 1 800 000], `NESTY_STREAM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`), used by Pi's proxy, codex's `onStall → controller.abort()`, and nestor-cloud; the claude loopback route at codex-responses-adapter/src/server.ts:555-571 deliberately has none.

F7's claude cell is the real one and is behaviour-proven: a real `sdk.query` against a stub that holds the socket open and silent ends at 544 907 ms (~9 minutes), and NONE of the four documented levers shortens it (default 60 s, `API_TIMEOUT_MS=8000` 90 s, + `API_FORCE_IDLE_TIMEOUT=1` 120 s, + `CLAUDE_ENABLE_BYTE_WATCHDOG=1`/`CLAUDE_STREAM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS=10000` 120 s — nothing). The SILENT-and-inverting switch verdict becomes UNIFORM.
  2. **B — re-derive the three landed rows and refuse the claude idle abort, recording that a stalled claude turn ends at the SDK's ~9-minute bound and that no documented lever shortens it on a loopback route** (size XS) — 6 cells re-derived, 1 becomes covered-by-decision. Cost to the user: a stalled claude turn hangs for nine minutes with a spinner, and switching onto claude silently loses a guarantee Pi and codex both keep.

### QF11 — Prompt history across sessions — the one cell in this family whose closure reverses a recorded privacy choice

- **Rows:** F36
- **Cells:** F36 absent ×3 — 3 non-covered cells
- **Docs:** interactive-mode.md §#command-history and §#reverse-search-with-ctrl-r (heading present at line 270; the page is 743 lines, curl 200); checkpointing.md §#rewind-and-summarize for the Note that supplies the in-session half verbatim: "If the prompt input contains text, double `Esc` clears it instead of opening the menu. The cleared text is saved to your input history, so press `Up` to recall it after you finish in the rewind menu."
- **Native:** None on any runtime — a composer prompt history is a host surface over host state, and no runtime writes one for us. HOST — measured and recorded at the site rather than inferred: the composer's history is an in-memory ref whose ONLY writer is the double-Esc clear and which is dropped on every session switch by design, with the leak reason written down (apps/electron/src/renderer/components/app-shell/input/double-escape.ts:59-71 and FreeFormInput.tsx:713-723). `promptHistory`, `inputHistory`, `recentPrompts` and an `ArrowUp` recall all measured ZERO before that row, with `ArrowUp` itself as the positive control. The sibling env spelling exists and is honoured on the create path: `NESSY_SKIP_PROMPT_HISTORY` / `CLAUDE_CODE_SKIP_PROMPT_HISTORY` (packages/shared/src/config/skip-prompt-history.ts, now read by SessionManager as well as the CLI).
- **Why absent today:** DECISION, and the decision is on the record with its reason: the in-memory-and-dropped-on-switch shape exists precisely so that one conversation's half-typed prompt cannot be recalled in another. Persisting prompts per directory reverses that choice. The ledger's own options are (a) in-memory per session, (b) persist honouring NESSY_SKIP_PROMPT_HISTORY, (c) document the divergence, with a recommendation of (c) then (b). It is asked in the 2026-09-05 owner questions under "[host] Prompt history across sessions — reverse a recorded privacy choice, or document the divergence" and has NOT been answered in the first round; it has no plan row at all (no "Denominator F36" in the tracker).
- **Options:**
  1. **A — persist prompts per project, honouring `NESSY_SKIP_PROMPT_HISTORY` / `CLAUDE_CODE_SKIP_PROMPT_HISTORY` and the never-persist setting that already ships** (size M) — 3 cells to covered, matching the documented product. Cost: prompt text — the most sensitive text a user types — becomes durable on disk in a product whose current design deliberately keeps it in memory; the opt-out exists but is off by default.
  2. **B — refuse and record: prompt history stays in memory and per session, the divergence from the documented product is stated, and the reason (a prompt typed in one conversation must not be recallable in another) is named on the surface** (size XS) — 3 cells to n/a-by-decision. Cost to the user: no `Ctrl+R` reverse search, no recall of yesterday's prompt in this project — a real convenience the page describes and we do not offer.
  3. **C — defer** (size —) — The only cell in family F with no plan row and no owner answer stays exactly that.
- **Reader's recommendation:** B, as the ledger itself recommends ((c) then (b)). The balance rule's tie-breaker here is that this is a PRODUCT POSTURE about durable storage of user-typed text, and the current shape was chosen deliberately with the reason written at the site — reversing it is the owner's call, not a lane's, and refusing it with the divergence named still counts the cells as covered-by-decision under the 2026-09-10 goal.
- **Question (RU):** Оставляем ли мы историю промптов в памяти и в пределах одной сессии — записав дивергенцию от документированного продукта и причину (промпт, набранный в одном разговоре, не должен всплывать в другом) — вместо того чтобы разворачивать зафиксированное решение о приватности и класть текст промптов на диск? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 3 cells to n/a-by-decision under B, or to covered under A. Either answer disposes of the last uncovered row in family F.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Substance stands; the citation does not, and the miss is the exact class AGENTS.md names. The rendered page publishes `id="reverse-search-with-ctrl+r"` — the `+` SURVIVES the slug, it does not hyphenate — so `#reverse-search-with-ctrl-r` resolves to nothing; measured by curl of https://code.claude.com/docs/en/interactive-mode (173 ids, `#command-history` present as claimed). Fix the anchor and add the sentence that makes B stronger rather than weaker, from that same section: in fullscreen rendering `Ctrl+S` "cycle[s] the scope through this session, this project, and all projects" — so the documented feature is cross-session BY CONSTRUCTION, which is precisely the privacy choice being asked about, and refusing it is refusing the whole feature rather than a corner of it. Otherwise QF11 verifies: F36 has no plan row (`Denominator F36` scores 0 in the tracker) and Q-den 25 has no answer anywhere in docs/, so it is genuinely unanswered and genuinely an owner posture. Keep recommendation B with the anchor corrected. (f) ORPHAN CHECK — none. Family F (ledger lines 1769-1817) holds 48 live rows plus the struck `~~F20~~` (merged into F9); F15, F19, F38, F44, F45 and F47 are covered on all three; the remaining 42 rows, 110 non-covered cells, are each in exactly one of QF1-QF11 and the per-question cell counts (12/15/7/13/12/12/12/6/12/6/3) all reconcile against the ledger's own cells.


## Family G — Hooks and automation

### QG1 — codex's own [hooks] table and the two of its eleven events our configuration never asks for

- **Rows:** G1, G4, G5
- **Cells:** Pi covered (not in cluster) / Claude covered (not in cluster) / Codex partial x3 — G1-codex `partial ᵗ`, G4-codex `partial ᵗ`, G5-codex `partial ᵏ`
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md#hook-events and #hook-locations (curl 200, both ids present in the rendered page). Codex side, STRONG tier: the ts-rs-generated bindings we ship, packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/HookEventName.ts, read verbatim at HEAD. Weak tier for intent only: learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks.md.
- **Native:** TIER 1, and it is already partly enabled. codex's HookEventName declares ELEVEN events verbatim: preToolUse, permissionRequest, postToolUse, preCompact, postCompact, sessionStart, sessionEnd, userPromptSubmit, subagentStart, subagentStop, stop. Nine already reach a codex turn (P6.11 `342c4e0ad`: four from codex-agent.ts, five from shared code), and PostToolUse is minted host-side since P3.335. The projection door is deliberately shut: codex-agent.ts `hostAuthoredHookProjection()` returns `{}` and the writer refuses an unminted plan whole. subagentStart/subagentStop are codex's OWN events, so their absence is our configuration, not codex's limit — P6.13 built `automations/runtime-hook-events.ts` precisely to keep that distinction after measuring that the obvious global-deferral repair would have deleted a working capability on two runtimes to describe an absence on a third.
- **Why absent today:** DECISION, ruled and implemented: P2.414 (2026-08-29, option 2) — project ONLY hooks the host itself authored; authorship, not trust, is the boundary. `8d7b4f399` (mintHostAuthoredCodexHooks) + `e2707e0c2` (the notice) + `325b1dff8` (the switch names hook events that stop firing). The residue is P6.14 `cef00cd4e`, closed with the two events DELIBERATELY LEFT, and it names two costs that fall due on the day the host authors its first hook: the TOML key is `timeout`, not the wire spelling `timeoutSec` (which silently reverts to codex's 600 s default), and an entry stays INERT until `[hooks.state."<key>"]` names it with a hash codex publishes through hooks/list and which is not re-derivable — a two-pass write→boot→read→write plus a true CodexHostHookRevocation.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build — ask codex for subagentStart/subagentStop through runtime-hook-events.ts** (size S) — Two of codex's own eleven events start firing; no new vocabulary is invented. But the codex protocol bindings are regenerated by the pending lift to rust-v0.150.1 (P7.2, still ⬜), so building now means reading the event list twice.
  2. **Refuse-and-record — keep the authorship boundary and mark G5-codex covered-by-decision** (size XS) — The user keeps a codex session where a plugin-authored or user-authored hook never reaches codex's own [hooks] table. The switch surface already says so on three surfaces, so the refusal is honest rather than silent.
  3. **Defer both to after P7.2** (size —) — The two cells stay partial for one more lift cycle; nothing regresses.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Refuse-and-record the projection boundary (it is ruled, implemented and already reported at a switch — least code, closest to the ruling) PLUS the S configuration of the two subagent events, sequenced after the rust-v0.150.1 lift so the eleven-event list is read once at the tag we actually ship. Building the projection would re-open a decision, and asking for the two events is configuration, not construction — exactly the native-first tier-1 move.
- **Question (RU):** Зафиксировать границу проекции хуков в codex (только host-authored, P2.414) как covered-by-decision, а два собственных события codex — subagentStart/subagentStop — добрать конфигурацией уже после лифта до rust-v0.150.1? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** G5-codex moves to n/a-by-decision immediately (1 cell). G1-codex and G4-codex become partial-with-a-stated-residue now and covered when the S configuration lands (2 cells). 3 of 46 dispositioned, none of them by building a new mechanism.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Rows/cells check out (G1-codex `partial ᵗ`, G4-codex `partial ᵗ`, G5-codex `partial ᵏ`), the eleven events read verbatim from packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/HookEventName.ts, hostAuthoredHookProjection() really does `return {}` (packages/shared/src/agent/codex-agent.ts:2798), and hooks.md#hook-events / #hook-locations both resolve. TWO breaks. (1) The recommended S half is refuted by our own measured table. packages/shared/src/automations/runtime-hook-events.ts, CODEX_HOOK_EVENT_EMISSION, verbatim: SubagentStart `kind: 'unreachable'`, note "`subagentStart` IS in codex's own HookEventName enum, so the binary has the moment — but our codex sessions spawn no subagent (`/subtask` withheld; `thread/fork` re-forks the thread and produces no background subagent) and the host emitter hangs off the `provider === 'pi'` child-session port, so nothing reaches it as we drive codex"; SubagentStop the same. So their absence is NOT our configuration — it is an absent subagent lifecycle, and both events are already in CODEX_NATIVE_HOOK_EVENTS as config vocabulary. Asking codex for them would fire nothing. (2) 'Configuration, not construction' is refuted by the door itself: hostAuthoredHookProjection's docblock says a future lane adding one writes it through `mintHostAuthoredCodexHooks` — "the only door" — and "owes two more things before it ships: the trust second pass (a projected entry is INERT until `[hooks.state.\"<key>\"]` names it … so it is write -> boot -> read -> write), and an answer to {@link CodexHostHookRevocation} that is true." That is the first host-authored hook, not an S knob, and runtime-hook-events.ts is a READING table with no configuration channel. Minor: G5-claude is `—`, not 'covered'. CORRECTED RECOMMENDATION: refuse-and-record the P2.414 authorship boundary only, and record the two subagent events as `unreachable` for a stated reason (no codex subagent lifecycle, `/subtask` withheld) — the precondition is a subagent lifecycle, not a [hooks] entry. Do NOT verdict any cell 'n/a-by-decision': the ledger's Cell vocabulary has five values and `n/a` means "Not this runtime's surface — the capability is meaningless here"; a refused-but-meaningful capability stays `absent`/`partial` with Why absent = DECISION. ### QG3 — Permission-bearing hook outputs: codex's exit-code contract is its binary's, and PermissionRequest updates are refused-and-named on Pi

- **Rows:** G7, G8
- **Cells:** Pi partial ʰ (G8) / Claude covered (both, not in cluster) / Codex partial ʰ (G7) + partial ʰ (G8)
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md#exit-code-2-behavior-per-event — verbatim: "Exit code 2 is the way a hook signals \"stop, don't do this.\" The effect depends on the event" — plus #other-exit-codes ("Any other exit code doesn't block on its own for most hook events"), #permissionrequest-decision-control and #permission-update-entries. All four ids verified in the rendered page.
- **Native:** TIER 1 on codex and it is the problem: codex runs its OWN hook processes, so the exit-code contract there is the binary's at rust-v0.148.0, not ours — the two contracts must be compared rather than assumed equal. NOTE A NULL, recorded rather than filled: the codex hook source file was not located by unauthenticated raw fetch (four path guesses under codex-rs/ all 404 at rust-v0.150.1 while the tag tree itself returns 200, and GitHub code search requires auth), so the strong-tier evidence available in-tree is the ts-rs bindings at rust-v0.148.0. codex's `permissionRequest` event fires natively (HookEventName.ts), so the EVENT half needs nothing. Host side: hook-decision-contract.ts (33 459 bytes at HEAD) owns the verdict for Pi; on claude the SDK owns it; the permission manager is shared by all three (core/permission-manager.ts).
- **Why absent today:** G7-codex: UNVERIFIED, **Deferred 2026-09-10** → D1.41 (P6.290, size M); the adjacent open row P6.143 shares the same rig. G8's open arm is the claude path where the SDK does the writing, **Deferred 2026-09-10** → D1.42 (P6.291, size S). G8-pi is `partial` for a DELIBERATE reason: pi-agent.ts `applyPermissionRequestUpdates` REFUSES a setMode at any destination other than `session` with `updated_permissions_refused` ("permissions.defaultMode has no reader, so the write would change nothing"), refuses bypassPermissions unless already present, refuses removeDirectories for configured roots, and drops any unrecognised entry type to `updated_permissions_deferred` ("applied nothing") — every refusal emitted as a named __PERMISSION_UPDATE__ record.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build/measure both — one sitting on the pinned codex binary: drive one hook per outcome (exit 2, exit 1, exit 0 with a decision JSON on stdout, a command that cannot start) with the identical four on Pi as the control, and the six PermissionRequest update kinds at each destination on claude** (size M + S) — Any divergence found is a PERMISSION divergence and earns its own switch-cost sentence. This is the only way to know whether a codex hook can block what a Pi hook blocks.
  2. **Refuse-and-record the Pi refusals as a stated posture** (size XS) — The user gets a hook that cannot silently widen permissions on Pi and is told by name each time it tries. That is HONEST by this ledger's own vocabulary and needs no code.
  3. **Defer both** (size —) — An unmeasured exit-code contract on the runtime whose hook processes we do not run keeps sitting under a `partial` that reads as 'mostly fine'.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Split the cluster by what the evidence can settle. Record G8-pi as covered-by-decision NOW — the refusals are deliberate, each has a named diagnostic, and the reason (permissions.defaultMode has no reader) is a fact about our tree, not a gap. Keep G7-codex as a MEASURE at the sitting, because an exit-code divergence between codex's binary and our contract is a permission divergence and no reading can substitute for it; G8-codex rides the same sitting.
- **Question (RU):** Записать pi-отказы PermissionRequest (setMode вне session, bypassPermissions, removeDirectories для сконфигурированных корней — каждый с именованной диагностикой) как covered-by-decision, а сравнение exit-кодов хуков codex с нашим контрактом оставить измерением D1.41 на посадке? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** G8-pi moves to n/a-by-decision immediately (1 of 3 cells, no code). G7-codex and G8-codex are dispositioned as measure-bound with a named rig and a date — they stop being open questions and become scheduled ones.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Host claims verify (pi-agent.ts:5071 applyPermissionRequestUpdates, the named refusals at :5101-5235, the verbatim 'permissions.defaultMode has no reader, so the write would change nothing' at :5342; hook-decision-contract.ts is at packages/shared/src/automations/, 34 991 bytes at HEAD, not 33 459) and all four hooks.md anchors resolve. But the recorded NULL is refuted, and it is the null the M rig was priced against. QG3 says 'the codex hook source file was not located by unauthenticated raw fetch (four path guesses under codex-rs/ all 404 … GitHub code search requires auth)'. The unauthenticated GIT TREE API finds it in one call: `curl -s https://api.github.com/repos/openai/codex/git/trees/rust-v0.148.0?recursive=1` returns 200 and lists a whole crate — codex-rs/hooks/src/engine/{command_runner,output_parser,dispatcher,mcp_runner}.rs, codex-rs/core/src/hook_runtime.rs, plus GENERATED per-event schemas at codex-rs/hooks/schema/generated/*.command.output.schema.json. Read at the tag we ship: pre-tool-use.command.output.schema.json declares `PreToolUseDecisionWire` = approve|block and `PreToolUsePermissionDecisionWire` = allow|deny|ask; and output_parser.rs (604 lines, the decision layer) contains ZERO occurrences of `exit_code`, while command_runner.rs carries it only as telemetry on the completion record. So the strong-tier reading available today says codex decides from parsed stdout JSON and not from an exit code — which is exactly the divergence the sitting was meant to discover, and AGENTS.md's ordering (for codex the SOURCE outranks the page; read before you build or schedule) puts that read ahead of an M rig. CORRECTED RECOMMENDATION: replace the recorded null with an XS SOURCE READ of codex-rs/hooks/src/engine/output_parser.rs + command_runner.rs + the five generated output schemas at rust-v0.148.0, and let the residue (whether a nonzero-exit hook with no stdout blocks anything on the shipped binary) size the rig — most of D1.41 may close by reading. Keep G8-pi at `partial` with Why absent = DECISION and the named diagnostics; do not move it to 'n/a-by-decision', which is not one of the ledger's five verdicts and would say the capability is meaningless on Pi when session-scope updates in fact apply. CORRECTED QUESTION: «Сначала прочитать исходник хуков codex (`codex-rs/hooks/src/engine/output_parser.rs` — 0 упоминаний exit_code — и сгенерированные output-схемы на rust-v0.148.0), заменив им записанный null, и лишь остаток вынести в D1.41; pi-отказы PermissionRequest оставить `partial` + Why=DECISION с именованными диагностиками? [да]»

### QG4 — Three hook OUTPUT fields are enforced host-side and driven on Pi alone — terminalSequence, async/asyncRewake, and the effort input

- **Rows:** G18, G19, G21

- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md — #emit-terminal-notifications (terminalSequence with an OSC allowlist, no /dev/tty), #run-hooks-in-the-background and #configure-an-async-hook (async: true, asyncRewake on exit code 2), #common-input-fields (the effort.level field and $CLAUDE_EFFORT). All four ids verified in the rendered page.
- **Native:** Probed at HEAD against the vendored SDK, which is now claudeCodeVersion **2.1.247** (package.json `"claudeCodeVersion": "2.1.247"`, `"version": "0.3.247"`): in the shipped darwin-arm64 binary `asyncRewake` scores 12, `terminalSequence` 17 and `CLAUDE_EFFORT` 8, against negative controls `zzzNotAField` 0 and `nessyWatchPathsControl` 0 on the same file — so all three exist natively on claude. On codex the async half is native (`HookExecutionMode = "sync" | "async"`, and ConfiguredHookHandler's command variant carries `async`) and there is NO rewake vocabulary anywhere in the 23 hook types under packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/. Host side the fields are ours and uniform: hook-command.ts quotes the OSC allowlist verbatim, defines allowlistedTerminalSequence, gates the field before setting universal.terminalSequence and merges it across handlers; format-adapters/claude.ts implements "asyncRewake implies async (background, never decision-bearing; command hooks only)"; hook-command.ts sets CLAUDE_EFFORT from input.effort?.level. Only the Bash-CHILD export of the effort variable is Pi-only (pi-agent.ts spreads { NESSY_EFFORT, CLAUDE_EFFORT } into the tool child; on claude the binary supplies its own).
- **Why absent today:** UNVERIFIED for the delivery half, **Deferred 2026-09-10** → D1.43 (P6.298, size M, merged rig). The cells are partial for two different reasons and the row note already says so: terminalSequence delivery depends on an on-screen interface at that moment (the page's own limits: interactive sessions only, and a WorktreeCreate command hook cannot return JSON), while codex's rewake half is a genuine absence in its vocabulary and the effort export is a Pi-only host convenience.
- **Options:**
  1. **Measure all three on claude and codex (one rig, a hook returning each field per runtime)** (size M) — Delivery observed or honestly refused for terminalSequence; the codex split recorded as one row note rather than one verdict. Sitting work.
  2. **Refuse-and-record the two codex halves — asyncRewake absent by codex's own vocabulary, $CLAUDE_EFFORT's Bash-child export Pi-only** (size XS) — The user on codex gets background hooks but no mid-idle rewake, and a Bash child that does not see the effort level. Both are stated rather than discovered. Building a host-side rewake beside codex's native async would be a second implementation of the same mechanism on the runtime least able to report it.
  3. **Defer the whole cluster** (size —) — Four cells stay partial with no residue written anywhere the user can see.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Refuse-and-record the two codex halves (G19-codex, G21-codex) — codex has the async half natively and no rewake vocabulary at all, so inventing one is exactly the silent divergence the native-first rule exists to prevent — and keep ONLY the terminalSequence delivery arm as a MEASURE, since that is the single clause where the answer is not already readable from the bindings.
- **Question (RU):** Записать отсутствие asyncRewake на codex и Pi-only экспорт $CLAUDE_EFFORT в Bash-ребёнка как covered-by-decision со сформулированным остатком, оставив измерением только доставку terminalSequence на claude и codex? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 2 of 4 cells (G19-codex, G21-codex) move to n/a-by-decision with no code. G18-claude and G18-codex collapse into one M rig at the sitting instead of three.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Every probe reproduces exactly at HEAD (vendored claudeCodeVersion 2.1.247 / version 0.3.247, binary self-report `2.1.247 (Claude Code)`; asyncRewake 12, terminalSequence 17, CLAUDE_EFFORT 8, controls zzzNotAField 0 and nessyWatchPathsControl 0), the codex halves verify (HookExecutionMode = "sync"|"async"; `rewake` scores 0 files across packages/codex-protocol), and the host sites verify (allowlistedTerminalSequence at automations/hook-command.ts:517 gating :541; setSanitized(env,'CLAUDE_EFFORT', input.effort?.level) at :97; format-adapters/claude.ts:1799-1803 'asyncRewake implies async'; pi-agent.ts:7816 spreads NESSY_EFFORT+CLAUDE_EFFORT into the tool child). The break is the disposition, not the evidence. The proposed move — 'G19-codex, G21-codex → n/a-by-decision' — is not in the ledger's vocabulary and is the wrong word for both: the file's own table defines `partial` as "Some documented clauses hold, others do not. The row says which", which is EXACTLY these two cells (codex has async natively and no rewake; codex gets the `effort.level` input from the shared host hook-command.ts and only the Bash-child export is Pi-only), and `n/a` as "Not this runtime's surface — the capability is meaningless here", which is false for both. Moving them would take two measured `partial`s out of the partial count into n/a with no behaviour change — coverage-flattering, and against §Counts, honestly, which scores the columns separately. CORRECTED RECOMMENDATION: keep G19-codex and G21-codex at `partial ʰ` and write the residue into the row note by name (no rewake vocabulary in codex's 23 hook types; the $CLAUDE_EFFORT Bash-child export is Pi-only), plus one switch-cost line for the rewake half; keep terminalSequence delivery as the single MEASURE arm of D1.43. CORRECTED QUESTION: «Оставить G19-codex и G21-codex как `partial` с дословно записанным остатком (у codex нет словаря rewake; экспорт $CLAUDE_EFFORT в Bash-ребёнка — только Pi) плюс строка switch-cost, а измерением оставить только доставку terminalSequence — вместо перевода двух измеренных ячеек в `n/a`? [да]»

### QG5 — Matcher semantics: the documented comma separator matches NOTHING on the path a person actually writes, and `*` is a dead matcher

- **Rows:** G20, G23
- **Cells:** Pi no verdict (G23) / Claude no verdict (G23) / Codex no verdict x2 (G20, G23)
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md#matcher-patterns — verbatim, a matcher of only letters, digits, `_`, `-`, spaces, `,` and the pipe is "Exact string, or list of exact strings separated by" pipe "or `,` with optional surrounding whitespace". And debug-your-config.md (curl 200): "The `matcher` field is a single string that uses" pipe "to match multiple tool names… A `,` separator is equivalent, so `\"Edit,Write\"` matches the same tools. Before v2.1.191, a comma fell through to regex evaluation and the matcher never matched." The `if` field is hooks-guide.md#filter-by-tool-name-and-arguments-with-the-if-field.
- **Native:** claude gets comma equivalence FROM THE BINARY (upstream v2.1.191; the vendored bundle is 2.1.247, so it is well past the pin) — but only for hooks declared in the SDK's own settings tiers, because mergeClaudeSdkHooks drops every non-internal PreToolUse matcher and the internal gate runs the automations itself. codex: read at rust-v0.148.0, ConfiguredHookMatcherGroup is `{ matcher: string | null, hooks: Array<ConfiguredHookHandler> }` — there is NO `if` equivalent on codex at all, and the matcher semantic there is codex's own (weak-tier claim about its binary, recorded as such). Host: matchesClaudeMatcher (automations/utils.ts) splits on /[|,]/ but is reached ONLY by a matcher carrying `matchMode: 'claude'`, which exactly two importers set (pluginHookMatcher and claude-setup-import).
- **Why absent today:** MEASURED AND NAMED RATHER THAN REPAIRED — P6.299 `d337ef8ce` drove four tools per matcher: with matchMode=claude both `"Edit,Write"` and the pipe form match Edit and Write and neither matches EditFile or Bash; with the DEFAULT (everything a person writes in this app is a NATIVE automation) `"Edit,Write"` matches **NOTHING**, and the documented `*` spelling is a dead matcher. The divergence is not per-runtime — it is per-matcher inside one host function, keyed on a field nothing a person writes ever sets. matchMode's own docblock records the reason it was not repaired: flipping the default would silently re-scope every stored automation.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build — warn at registration when a native matcher contains a comma or is `*`, naming the documented spelling and what it will actually do** (size S) — Transparent and changes nothing on disk: a user who wrote the documented spelling is told it is not the semantic here, instead of a hook that silently never fires. One enforcement point, a warning and never an error.
  2. **Build — flip matchMode's default to 'claude' and migrate stored automations** (size M) — Full page parity, and it silently re-scopes every automation already stored: a regex matcher that happens to contain a comma changes meaning under the user's feet. This is a behaviour change to hooks people are already relying on.
  3. **Refuse-and-record** (size XS) — The divergence stays, and the only place it is written down is a commit message and a docblock — invisible at the moment a user types the matcher.
- **Reader's recommendation:** The S warning. Where the page and our storage genuinely disagree, the transparent-and-simple move is to make the disagreement visible at the moment it is authored, not to re-scope hooks that already run. G20-codex is a real null and should be recorded as such: codex has no `if` equivalent, so the cell is n/a-by-measurement rather than absent.
- **Question (RU):** Оставить нативные matcher'ы регулярками, но предупреждать при регистрации, если matcher содержит запятую или равен `*` (документированные написания, которые у нас не совпадают ни с чем), и записать отсутствие `if` в codex как n/a по измерению? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** G20-codex moves to n/a-by-measurement (1 cell). G23's three cells move to covered-by-decision with the warning as the honest surface (3 cells). 4 of 46, for one S guard.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** The measurement is real and reproduces. hooks.md#matcher-patterns says verbatim "Exact string, or list of exact strings separated by `|` or `,` with optional surrounding whitespace"; debug-your-config.md line 64 carries the v2.1.191 sentence verbatim; automations/utils.ts:365-370 shows the default path is `new RegExp(matcher.matcher).test(matchValue)` inside a try/catch returning false — so `"Edit,Write"` matches only the literal string and `*` throws and is caught, a dead matcher — while matchesClaudeMatcher (:384) splits on /[|,]/ and special-cases `*`; types.ts:999-1008 records why the default was not flipped ("flipping that default would silently re-scope saved automations"). The S warning is the right instrument and I endorse it. What breaks is the coverage arithmetic and the two verdict moves. (1) 'G23's three cells move to covered-by-decision (3 cells)' is false: G23 asks that both separators 'match the same tools', and after the warning they still do not on native automations — by the ledger's own vocabulary that is `partial` ("Some documented clauses hold, others do not"), never `covered`, and there is no 'covered-by-decision' verdict. (2) 'G20-codex → n/a-by-measurement' is the wrong word: ConfiguredHookMatcherGroup is `{ matcher: string | null, hooks: Array<ConfiguredHookHandler> }` with no `if`, and ConfiguredHookHandler's four variants carry none either — so filtering a hook by its arguments is meaningful on codex and unavailable, which is `absent` with Why = STRUCTURAL, not "meaningless here". CORRECTED RECOMMENDATION: take the S registration warning (transparent-and-simple, no stored automation re-scoped), verdict G23 `partial` on all three with the warning named as the honest surface, and verdict G20-codex `absent ʰ` (STRUCTURAL) citing ConfiguredHookMatcherGroup and ConfiguredHookHandler at rust-v0.148.0. CORRECTED QUESTION: «Оставить нативные matcher'ы регулярками, но предупреждать при регистрации на запятую и на `*`, записав G23 как `partial` (обе записи по-прежнему не совпадают — предупреждение делает расхождение видимым, а не закрывает его), а отсутствие `if` в codex — как `absent`/STRUCTURAL со ссылкой на ConfiguredHookMatcherGroup? [да]»

### QG6 — Documented hook fields the host accepts and no runtime honours: eleven Notification types with no emitter, and two SessionStart directives declared inert

- **Rows:** G10, G17
- **Cells:** Pi partial ʰ x2 / Claude no verdict (G10) + partial ʰ (G17) / Codex absent ʰ (G10) + partial ʰ (G17)

- **Why absent today:** **ANSWERED, not open.** docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md §"Owner decisions 2026-09-05, part 3", entry **D5**: "how does a user's own environment reach a host-run command hook? · denominator G15 · **Option A:** the six-name whitelist stays, and the divergence is disclosed. The 2026-09-05 question (denominator slice G#0) had recommended (B), an operator-configurable passthrough allowlist; the owner chose (A). The consequence was 'ledger' — a record, not a build — and that record was never written into these three cells, which is the only reason they still read as gaps.
- **Options:**
  1. **Record only — write D5 into the three cells as covered-by-decision and add the switch-cost disclosure** (size XS) — The user learns that `ENABLE_STOP_REVIEW=0` and every `SECURITY_GUIDANCE_*` value governs on claude and governs nothing on Pi or codex — the exact silent divergence this fork counts as its largest defect class, made honest.

  3. **Defer** (size —) — An answered question keeps counting as three open cells.

### QG9 — Pausing a tool call and answering it from outside — codex has no defer vocabulary at all

- **Rows:** G16

- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md#defer-a-tool-call-for-later (id verified in the rendered page) — the page names this exact surface as "an Agent SDK app or a custom UI built on top of Claude Code": a PreToolUse hook returns "defer", the -p run exits with stop_reason: "tool_deferred" carrying deferred_tool_use, the caller collects the answer in its own UI and resumes with updatedInput.
- **Native:** codex: `permissionRequest` is native but carries NO defer/resume vocabulary — verified with a positive control at HEAD, `grep -rl 'Defer'` over packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/ returns **0 files** against **33** for 'Approval' on the same run, and codex answers an approval IN-BAND rather than by process exit. So the host would have to hold the call open itself. Pi and claude both deliver it (pi-harness/deferred-tool-call.ts; the claude event adapter reads deferred_tool_use off the message and quotes the page's error clause; carried on the wire in core/src/types/message.ts and settled at SessionManager.turnDeferredToolUse).

Related and already ruled on the claude side: `5249afe2f` closed claude's own defer as a stated POSTURE — the runtime CAN defer but the host cannot receive it, so claude degrades exactly one rank to `ask` carrying its own user-facing reason rather than stranding the turn.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build — hold the call open host-side on codex and synthesise the resume** (size M) — A second implementation of a permission-shaped mechanism on the runtime least able to report it, beside a native approval flow that answers in-band; the failure mode is a stranded turn with nothing on screen saying why, which is the exact trade `5249afe2f` already refused on claude.
  2. **Refuse-and-record** (size XS) — A user switching onto codex loses defer/resume and is told so at the switch. The residual difference is honest rather than silent — this ledger's stated pattern to copy.
  3. **Defer** (size —) — One cell stays absent with the switch cost already reported, so deferring changes nothing the user sees.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Refuse-and-record. codex genuinely cannot defer (its pre-tool-use output schema has no fourth member), the switch cost is already reported in both locales, and the host-side alternative was measured and refused once already on claude for a reason that applies verbatim here.

The break is that the question buys nothing and mis-verdicts what it touches. G16-codex is ALREADY `absent ʰ`, which is the correct verdict by the ledger's own table ('The user cannot do it, and we know that'); the proposed move to 'n/a-by-decision' is not one of the five verdicts and would assert the capability is 'not this runtime's surface — meaningless here', which is false for a capability the page names for exactly this app shape ('an Agent SDK app or a custom UI built on top of Claude Code') and which the other two runtimes deliver. ### QG11 — SIGTERM on a headless run: exit 143 is delivered and the other three clauses are unmeasured on every runtime

- **Rows:** G24
- **Cells:** Pi no verdict / Claude no verdict / Codex no verdict
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/headless.md#stop-a-run-with-sigterm (id verified in the rendered page), verbatim: "On SIGTERM, Claude Code terminates the process tree of any Bash command that is still running. Claude Code then runs `SessionEnd` hooks and exits. While exiting, Claude Code starts no new tool call, sends no new model request, and runs no hook other than `SessionEnd`" — and, earlier, "Claude Code exits with code 143… leaves the turn that was in progress unfinished and records no result for it."
- **Native:** The EXIT-CODE half is already built to the page and is ours: apps/cli/src/index.ts `signalExitCode` quotes the page whole in its docblock and is `signal === 'SIGTERM' ? 143 : 130`, with process.on('SIGINT'/'SIGTERM') registration and cleanup() in cmdRun and the mapping documented in --help. codex-agent-server's runtime-manager.test.ts matches SIGTERM for the codex child. NO cross-runtime assertion exists anywhere in the tree — recorded as a null rather than assumed. Unmeasured on all three: process-tree termination, the SessionEnd receipt, and resumability of the unfinished turn.
- **Why absent today:** UNVERIFIED, and **Deferred 2026-09-10** (owner ruling, point 2) → moved to the follow-up plan as **D1.44** (P6.301, size M), verdicts intact. The rig is already written down: a headless run per runtime with a backgrounded sleep, SIGTERM, then check pgrep for the orphan, the SessionEnd hook's receipt and --resume on the unfinished turn; control = SIGINT on the same rig.
- **Options:**
  1. **Measure — the three-clause rig on each of the three runtimes** (size M) — Three clauses x three runtimes recorded as behaviour readings; any runtime that leaks a Bash process tree or skips SessionEnd earns a row of its own. It is three commands per runtime and needs no packaged app.
  2. **Refuse-and-record — record the exit-code half as covered and the three residual clauses as an admitted null** (size XS) — A leaked Bash process tree after a supervisor kills the run would stay undetected, and SessionEnd cleanup hooks that never ran would be indistinguishable from cleanup hooks that ran and did nothing.
  3. **Defer again** (size —) — Three cells stay empty on a clause the page states unconditionally.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Measure. A leaked Bash process tree is a containment failure rather than a cosmetic gap, and SessionEnd is the hook every cleanup story in this family depends on; the rig is small and needs no credentials, which makes this the cheapest MEASURE in G.
- **Question (RU):** Провести отложенный прогон D1.44 (SIGTERM на headless-запуске: дерево процессов, SessionEnd-квитанция, --resume; контроль — SIGINT) на всех трёх рантаймах, вместо того чтобы записать три клетки как признанный null? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 3 cells move to covered, or to a named absence per clause with a row of its own for any runtime that leaks a process tree.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Anchor and quote verify (headless.md#stop-a-run-with-sigterm, 'Claude Code exits with code 143 … leaves the turn that was in progress unfinished and records no result for it'), apps/cli/src/index.ts:1649 is `signal === 'SIGTERM' ? 143 : 130` with run.test.ts:451 pinning it, and D1.44 exists at the follow-up plan :246. Two breaks, both in the rig. (1) 'Unmeasured on all three: … the SessionEnd receipt' overstates the null — the clause is BUILT and says so at apps/cli/src/index.ts:1698-1700, verbatim: 'Signal handling — cancel + clean up on SIGINT/SIGTERM. `cleanup()` must stay BEFORE the exit: stopping the spawned server is what runs SessionEnd, the other clause of `headless.md:69`', with onSignal ordering `sessions:cancel` → cleanup() → exit. So SessionEnd is built-and-unobserved, not unmeasured, and the process-tree half likewise has a named owner (packages/server-core/src/pi-harness/process-supervisor.ts). (2) The rig does not say WHICH process gets the signal, and the answer decides the whole result: the SIGTERM handler is the CLI's, the CLI spawns the server (apps/cli/src/server-spawner.ts), and the Bash children hang off the server's ProcessSupervisor — so `kill` on the CLI and `kill` on the server measure different things, and 'check pgrep for the orphan' is ambiguous between them. The cost claim is wrong too: 'needs no credentials' cannot hold when each arm must reach the model to get a backgrounded Bash call into flight on Pi, claude and codex. CORRECTED RECOMMENDATION: keep D1.44 but re-specify it — read apps/cli/src/index.ts's ordering and process-supervisor.ts first (they already answer the shape), then drive one headless run per runtime naming the signalled PID explicitly (CLI vs spawned server, both arms), with SIGINT as the control, and price it with credentials, not without. Also fix the decayed anchor in that file's docblock: the sentence it cites by line number in headless.md now sits eight lines lower — cite headless.md#stop-a-run-with-sigterm plus the sentence. ORPHAN CHECK (f): none. Family G holds 25 unique rows G1-G25 and every partial/absent/no-verdict cell falls in a cluster — G1,G4,G5 (QG1); G1,G3,G5 (QG2); G7,G8 (QG3); G18,G19,G21 (QG4); G20,G23 (QG5); G10,G17 (QG6); G12 (QG7); G15 (QG8); G16 (QG9); G6,G9,G13,G22,G25 (QG10); G24 (QG11). G2, G11 and G14 are covered on all three and correctly outside; G25-pi and G25-codex are `n/a`. CORRECTED QUESTION: «Провести D1.44 в переспецифицированном виде — сначала прочитать уже построенный порядок в apps/cli/src/index.ts (cleanup ПЕРЕД exit — именно остановка сервера запускает SessionEnd) и process-supervisor.ts, затем прогон на каждом рантайме с явно названным сигналимым PID (CLI и порождённый сервер — обе ветки), контроль SIGINT, с учётом того что кредлы нужны? [да]»

### QH3 — Codex: the per-role fields the runtime cannot express — seven refusals and one missing line

- **Rows:** H7, H21, H23, H26, H27, H31, H33, H34
- **Cells:** 8 codex cells — H7 `absent`, H21 `absent`, H23 `absent`, H26 `absent`, H27 `absent`, H31 `absent`, H33 `absent`, H34 `absent` (the Pi and Claude halves of these rows are clustered elsewhere)
- **Docs:** sub-agents.md#write-subagent-files (the frontmatter field list a definition may carry); sub-agents.md#enable-persistent-memory; sub-agents.md#run-subagents-in-foreground-or-background; sub-agents.md#disable-specific-subagents; agent-sdk/subagents.md#agentdefinition-configuration — all four ids verified present on the rendered pages
- **Native:** Asked, and the answer is strong-tier because it is read from the schema generated from rust-v0.148.0: codex's `[agents.<role>]` table declares a name, a description and a role file. `config-schema.json` carries `AgentsToml` ×2 and `MultiAgentV2ConfigToml` ×5 with `subagent_developer_instructions` ×1 — that last one IS the native equivalent of H34's append-to-every-subagent knob, and the V2 surface it lives on is stable/FALSE in features/src/lib.rs. There is no `skills` key, no per-role `memory` (the session-wide `MemoriesToml` is a different subsystem), no `maxTurns`, no `background`, no `isolation` and no per-agent deny — a role is declared or it is not. The one exception is H33: `[agents] enabled` DOES exist and our writer deliberately does not emit it, reserving the name as a role-collision guard.
- **Why absent today:** STRUCTURAL for seven of the eight, and the drop is already AUDIBLE rather than silent: P6.309 shipped `reportUnexpressibleCodexAgentFields`, which names every definition declaring one of these fields, per definition on the diagnostic channel and once in aggregate on the operator queue. H33 is different — a deliberate non-write whose reason is at the emit site ("the spawn tools are already on, and a role is additive guidance, not an authorization"), and P3.347 already emits the bare `[agents]` table for the two ceilings, so it is one more line.
- **Options:**
  1. **Record the seven as n/a-by-decision (the reporter already speaks them) and write the single `[agents] enabled = false` line for H33** (size XS — one line in buildCodexConfigToml plus a host setting to request it) — eight cells disposition; the operator gains a real off-switch codex publishes itself, and the seven losses stay visible in the diagnostic and the operator queue
  2. **Emulate the seven host-side — inline skill bodies into the role file, a host turn cap, a host memory directory, a host worktree per role** (size M to L per field, seven fields) — three parallel mechanisms running beside codex's own, which is the switch-safety problem this fork exists to avoid; and each one silently diverges the moment codex ships its own
  3. **Leave them absent with no disposition** (size none) — eight cells stay non-covered while the reason for every one of them is already written down
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option (a). Native-first tier 1 for the one field codex actually has (`enabled`), refuse-and-record for the seven it does not — and the refusal is already spoken, so the cost of the disposition is a ledger edit.
- **Question (RU):** Фиксируем ли семь полей codex-роли (skills, memory, background, maxTurns, isolation, per-agent deny, subagent-append) как отказ-по-решению — они уже озвучены через reportUnexpressibleCodexAgentFields — и дописываем ли одну строку `[agents] enabled = false` для H33? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 8 cells: 7 → n/a-by-decision, 1 (H33 codex) → covered
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** The seven-refusals direction survives; four evidence statements do not, and one of them makes the proposed one-line build conditional in a way the question does not disclose.

(1) `[agents] enabled` is NOT an unconditional off-switch. `packages/codex-protocol/schema/config-schema.json`, `AgentsToml.enabled`, verbatim: *"Whether multi-agent tools are enabled. Defaults to true. An enabled `features.multi_agent_v2` setting takes precedence."* So writing `enabled = false` binds only while `features.multi_agent_v2` stays off — which is today's posture but is a second condition the row must state, and it must be pinned by a test, or the operator's off-switch silently stops working the day v2 is enabled.

(2) `subagent_developer_instructions` is not the native equivalent of H34's append knob. Same file, `MultiAgentV2ConfigToml.subagent_developer_instructions`, verbatim: *"Overrides inherited developer instructions for subagents without role-specific instructions."* It OVERRIDES rather than appends, applies only to roleless subagents, and lives on the V2 surface we deliberately keep off — so H34/codex has no native counterpart at all, which is a stronger refusal than the one written.

(3) `AgentRoleToml` (`additionalProperties: false`) declares `config_file`, `description`, `nickname_candidates` — there is no `name` field; the role's name is the table key.

(4) 'a role is declared or it is not' understates `AgentsToml`, which also carries `default_subagent_model`, `default_subagent_reasoning_effort`, `max_concurrent_threads_per_session`, `max_depth` and `interrupt_message` — relevant because two of those are exactly the per-subagent model/effort facts QH6 records as unpublishable on codex.

Corrected question: «Фиксируем ли семь полей codex-роли как отказ-по-решению (уже озвучены через `reportUnexpressibleCodexAgentFields`, 5 файлов) — с исправлением: `subagent_developer_instructions` НЕ эквивалент H34 (он overrides, только для ролей без инструкций, и живёт на выключенной V2-поверхности) — и дописываем ли `[agents] enabled = false`, зафиксировав тестом, что переключатель действует лишь пока `features.multi_agent_v2` выключен (схема: "An enabled `features.multi_agent_v2` setting takes precedence")?»

So the premise 'the panel renders status/phase and never lastActivity' is false at HEAD; H12/Pi is a re-score, size XS, not the S the question prices.

Corrected question: «Пересчитываем ли H12/Pi как уже закрытую (SubagentCard.tsx:110-111, P6.305) и записываем ли H13 как отказ-по-решению с ТОЧНОЙ формулировкой: "поток SDK, который мы читаем, не несёт модель и effort субагента (`SDKTaskProgressMessage.usage` = tokens/tool_uses/duration_ms); сам Claude Code показывает их в своём `/tasks` начиная с 2.1.242" — не синтезируя значения из дескриптора спавна?»

- **Rows:** I3, I4, I5, I26

- **Native:** claude: does the reopen-reset natively and publishes all five states; and `git worktree lock` is git's own interlock, which we adopted (P3.312 `edbd4f42c`) — measured on real git: `git worktree remove` exits 128 on a locked worktree and so does `--force`, only `-f -f` overrides. codex: no worktree surface, so nothing native. Pi: none. For the setup script there is NO documented setting on any runtime — `settings-reference.md` re-probed today publishes four `worktree.*` keys and no setup or platform key — so P6.60 decided it TRANSPARENT and SIMPLE (`setupScript` takes a string or `{ windows, posix }`, unknown buckets refused by name).
- **Why absent today:** Three DEFECT residues and one measured absence, none deferred, none in the release sitting. I3: `sweepOrphanedWorktrees` has three provider-agnostic call sites and the interlock landed, but the pass still walks ONE session's ledger while worktrees share ONE namespace root — a scheduled task makes a new session per run, so run #2 opens an empty ledger and run #1's worktree is unreachable, identically on all three (P3.288 correctly REFUSED a switch-cost row for it). I4: P6.135 `439c8ee95`/`0db4d1f6f` landed the keep-or-remove card and P6.547 `5df0095f7` the branch removal, but our age-based cleanup still removes without snapshotting, and the page's unnamed-session auto-removal was deliberately not built. I5: the platform half is built (P6.60 `a5c4e225e`) and the port is supplied unconditionally on every construction (P3.87 REFUTED the hypothesis that the claude/codex cells score nothing) — the remaining half is a recorded REFUSAL at `worktree-settings.ts`: «a repository you clone must not be able to run a command by being opened». I26: P6.337 `e3ba6baeb` measured it — the documented reset is ABSENT in all five states by construction (note: that row's own citation to `worktree-resume.ts` is void at HEAD).
- **Options:**
  1. **A — record all four as they are: I3/I5 partial with the residue quoted, I4 refused-and-recorded (the user is asked before a removal that would delete work), I26 absent-by-construction with the page's five states named** (size XS (ledger edit)) — twelve cells dispositioned with no code; the two data-loss-class residues (I3's cross-session leak, I4's age-based removal) are written down rather than closed
  2. **B — build all three missing halves: a namespace-wide sweep instead of a per-session ledger (M), a snapshot before age-based removal (M), the documented reopen-reset (S)** (size M+M+S) — closes the data-loss class on all three runtimes at once because every seam is host-side; the sweep change is the risky one — a namespace-wide sweep can remove a live worktree if the realpath compare is wrong, which is exactly how P3.312 was nearly mis-built
  3. **C — build ONLY the reopen-reset (the one half with a published contract and five named states), record the other three** (size S) — the smallest build that closes a documented capability; the leak and the snapshot stay as stated residues, mitigated by the `git worktree lock` interlock and by the keep-or-remove card respectively
- **Reader's recommendation:** C. The balance rule picks the half that has a page behind it: `#reuse-a-worktree-name` publishes the exact conditions and our `inspect` already reads all three inputs (`status --porcelain`, `branch --show-current`, `merge-base --is-ancestor`), so it is S and provider-agnostic. I3 and I4 are real data-loss-class residues, but both already have a mitigation in place and neither has a documented shape to build to; recording them is the transparent answer, and B can be bought later if a user meets one.
