### QJ2 — Egress from the agent's shell — a per-host allowlist, and codex's blocked network that reads as a DNS miss

- **Rows:** J11, J61
- **Cells:** J11 Pi — (no verdict) / Claude — (no verdict) / Codex absent (3). J61 Pi absent / Claude absent / Codex absent (3). 6 non-covered cells.
- **Docs:** No Claude page defines a per-host egress allowlist for the agent's shell — searched settings-reference (sandbox keys are filesystem + the network master switch) and sandboxing.md; recorded as a null. Codex evidence is STRONG tier (source, not page): rust-v0.148.0 `features/src/lib.rs`, `core/src/config/mod.rs`, `protocol/src/permissions.rs` — `[features] network_proxy` is gated on `network_sandbox_policy().is_enabled()`, and `[features] request_permissions_tool` is `Stage::UnderDevelopment, default_enabled: false`; confirmed by a binary string probe with a negative control.
- **Native:** Asked and answered in the source. codex HAS the keys (`network.domains`, `unix_sockets`, `allow_local_binding`) and they are INERT in our shape: the allowlist is enforced by a MITM proxy that never runs, and `Restricted` is what `resolveRuntimePolicy` produces in every configuration this product ships — including master-switch OFF, because `danger-full-access` maps onto `:workspace` and `deniedReadPaths` is a hardcoded literal at all three CodexAgent call sites. Pi HAS a real mechanism: `denyNetworkEgress` threaded through `process-supervisor.ts:158,264` into all three platform profiles, and on Windows written as codex's OWN `[permissions.<profile>.network] enabled = <bool>`. Claude has NONE — the tree's only network gate is `unified-network-interceptor.ts`, which is not the agent's shell. Audit-receipt half: `record_network_sandbox_violation` emits only a `tracing::warn!`, never a protocol event, so a codex denial can never mint a receipt.
- **Why absent today:** J11 is DECISION-shaped and unmade: the plan carries it as OQ-11 (OPEN) with three costed options in `2026-08-28-codex-sandbox-enforcement.md` §3; the measured failure mode is worse than the block — loopback HTTP refused (`curl: (7)`), `https://1.1.1.1/` refused with no DNS, while `dscacheutil -q host` exits 0 with empty output, so it reads as *no such host* rather than *denied*. The honesty half LANDED (P6.149 / W6-a, halves (b)+(c): the diagnostic is keyed on the emitted `[permissions.<profile>]` and names the supersession). The Pi and claude arms are the two no-verdicts and belong to P6.150 ⬜, which carries **Release sitting 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 5): gates 1.36.0 — P7.5** — a timing marker; the disposition is still owed. J61 is STRUCTURAL and stated so.
- **Options:**
  1. **Record J61 as a deliberate refusal (STRUCTURAL: the enforcing proxy never runs) and let P6.150's sitting measurement fill J11's Pi and claude cells** (size XS for the record; the measurement is already funded as P7.5) — the user keeps all-or-nothing egress with a named refusal, and the switch copy can finally say the real Pi↔claude↔codex difference instead of guessing it.
  2. **Build a host-side per-host egress allowlist for all three runtimes** (size L) — a second network enforcement path beside codex's inert one; on claude there is nothing to hook, so it means owning a proxy or an interceptor for the agent's shell — a new permanent surface.
  3. **Turn codex's own `network_proxy` on** (size M) — refused by its own source: it is gated on a policy that is `Restricted` in our shape, and where it WOULD run it starts denying live egress. A MITM proxy is also a product posture nobody asked for.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. Native-first was asked and the answer came from codex's own source: the mechanism exists and cannot fire in our shape, so building beside it is tier 3 work with a tier 1 answer already on the table. The residue that IS buildable is small and already named — `formatCodexSandboxEgressDiagnostics` early-returns [] on `danger-full-access`, the shipped default, so the honest surface is PARTIAL; that is an S, not an L.
- **Question (RU):** Зафиксировать ли пер-хостовый allowlist сетевого доступа шелла как осознанный отказ (STRUCTURAL — прокси, который его исполняет, в нашей конфигурации не запускается), закрыв только S-остаток честной диагностики на `danger-full-access` и оставив измерение Pi/claude за P7.5? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 6 cells dispositioned: J61's 3 → n/a-by-decision with the source-tier reason; J11 codex absent → absent-by-decision; J11's 2 no-verdicts leave the no-claim column at the P7.5 sitting.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** The single piece of work the recommendation funds is already built, and the sentence funding it is a stale ledger quote. The question says «`formatCodexSandboxEgressDiagnostics` early-returns [] on `danger-full-access`, the shipped default, so the honest surface is PARTIAL; that is an S, not an L» — that is copied verbatim from the ledger's own J61 `honest_surface` clause, and P6.149 is ✅ `2784ffa9f` (2026-09-05), whose subject line is «fix(codex): a denied network call stops reading as a DNS miss in the shipped default, keyed on the resolved policy». At HEAD `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/config-writer.ts` carries «P6.149 · KEYED ON THE RESOLVED POLICY, NOT ON THE MODE WE WROTE. This line used to return `[]` for `danger-full-access` … So the diagnostic was DEAD in the shipped configuration», and `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/__tests__/config-writer-sandbox-egress.test.ts` pins it with the case «fires with the switch OFF once a profile is emitted» plus the negative control «still says nothing with no profile». So the residue is zero, not S. ### QJ4 — Deferred tool loading — tool search on claude and codex, and the `alwaysLoad` pin that is inert behind our own loopback

- **Rows:** J2, J12, J26
- **Cells:** J2 Pi covered / Claude absent / Codex absent (2). J12 Pi covered / Claude partial / Codex n/a (1). J26 Pi covered / Claude absent / Codex absent (2). 5 non-covered cells.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/tool-search#configure-tool-search — anchor verified by curl 2026-09-11, verbatim: “Tool search is enabled by default: MCP tools are deferred and discovered on demand. Claude Code disables it when `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` points to a non-first-party host, since most proxies don't forward `tool_reference` blocks. Set `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` explicitly to override that fallback.” And code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp#exempt-a-server-from-deferral, verbatim: “If a server's tools should always be visible to Claude without a search step, set `alwaysLoad` to `true` in that server's configuration. Every tool from that server then loads into context at session start regardless of the `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` setting.”
- **Native:** Tier 1 exists on claude and is deliberately half-taken: `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` is written by `options.ts` and only ever as `'false'`; Q-den 66 = option 3 (LANDED P6.529 / `2e980d842`) now lets the ON directions cross where the base URL IS first-party, keeping the forced OFF on our loopback. The pin itself is written correctly per TOOL (P2.454 / `ff28207ea`), matching both spellings the page gives — but a pin exempts a tool from DEFERRAL and deferral is off on our claude path, so the pin is measurably INERT (P6.358 drove the clause). codex: the config keys are `Stage::Removed`, and the gate IS reachable via `model_catalog_json` or by serving the `/models` endpoint our adapter 404s — decided 2026-09-05 (decision 21: decline and record the null), do not re-answer. Pi's own client-side catalogue carries the same threshold vocabulary and is the only runtime where the capability is real.
- **Why absent today:** STRUCTURAL-by-construction on claude: our gateway adapter points `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` at a per-session loopback, which disables SDK tool search, and the override would fail anyway because the translator does not implement `tool_reference` blocks. The honest-surface half is BUILT — `reportToolSearchDisposition` now runs from all three launch paths (P6.145 / W6-g), so the per-runtime state is reported rather than silent. The gating measurement (does `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=true` survive our own loopback) is P6.141, **Deferred 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 2)** and MOVED to `2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md` row `D1.22`, verdicts intact.
- **Options:**
  1. **Record the absence as a deliberate refusal with the disposition report as its honest surface, and revisit only if D1.22 measures the loopback surviving** (size XS) — claude and codex keep every tool definition upfront — a context cost, not a capability loss; the report already names which arm applied, so nothing is silent.
  2. **Teach the loopback adapter `tool_reference` blocks and set `auto:N`** (size L) — closes J2/claude on every connection including the gateway card — and a proxy that mishandles the beta block drops tool definitions SILENTLY, which is exactly the failure the SDK's own fallback exists to prevent; the beta shape can change under us with no version we control.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. Closest to the runtime's own documentation is to leave the SDK's fallback doing its job — the page itself explains why it disables tool search on a non-first-party host — and the operator's choice is already honoured where it can be (Q-den 66 = 3). J12's claude cell is `partial` because the pin is correct and its enabling switch is off; that is inert-by-design and belongs in a comment, not a build.
- **Question (RU):** Записать ли отсутствие tool search на claude и codex как осознанный отказ (адаптер не реализует `tool_reference`, SDK сам отключает поиск за не-first-party URL), а `alwaysLoad`-пин на claude — как inert-by-design с честной строкой в отчёте, не открывая работу до измерения D1.22? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 5 cells: J2's 2 and J26's 2 → n/a / absent-by-decision with the reason and the disposition report as the honest surface; J12's claude `partial` keeps its residue stated (the pin is written, the switch is off by design).
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** The disposition is sound — the SDK's own fallback is the nearest documented answer, the codex arm was decided 2026-09-05 (plan decision 21: «Decline and record the null»), P6.145 ✅ `a7d9c8ce3` already ships the disposition report, and D1.22 owns the gating measurement — but the citation presented as verbatim is a paraphrase, which is the one thing this repo's rules forbid outright ('quote sentences', 'Quote raw docs, not summaries'). The question quotes «Tool search is enabled by default: MCP tools are deferred and discovered on demand. Claude Code disables it when `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` points to a non-first-party host… Set `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` explicitly to override that fallback.» The page (curl https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/tool-search.md, 2026-09-11) says instead: «Tool search is on by default.» and «The SDK also disables tool search when `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` points to a non-first-party host, since most proxies don't forward `tool_reference` blocks. You can override that default with the `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` environment variable». Note the subject is the SDK, not 'Claude Code', and the page adds two override-proof cases the question does not carry (a Microsoft Foundry deployment hosted on Azure, and Agent Platform models earlier than Claude 4.5). The `alwaysLoad` quote IS verbatim (`mcp.md:1432`). Nothing else broke: cells match, #configure-tool-search and #exempt-a-server-from-deferral both resolve as ids, and `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` scores 2 in `sdk.mjs` / the binary. CORRECTED QUESTION: identical in substance, with the citation replaced by the two sentences above and the Foundry/Agent-Platform carve-outs named, so a later reader does not discover that `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=true` is overridable in fewer cases than the question implied.

### QJ5 — MCP channels — session-scoped arming, cross-runtime delivery, and channels shipped inside a plugin

- **Rows:** J20, J29, J30
- **Cells:** J20 Pi partial / Claude absent / Codex absent (3). J29 Pi covered / Claude absent / Codex absent (2). J30 Pi absent / Claude absent / Codex absent (3). 8 non-covered cells.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/channels-reference — anchors verified by curl 2026-09-11: #gate-inbound-messages, #relay-permission-prompts, #package-as-a-plugin, #notification-format. code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp#push-messages-with-channels (verified). AGENTS.md names `channels-reference.md` among the eleven plugin-format pages, and `plugins/manifest-reference.md#component-path-forms` (was plugins-reference § component-path-fields) is where a `channels` component would have to be declared.
- **Native:** No runtime supplies channels itself. claude — the vendored CLI has channels, but every MCP server reaches the SDK as an in-process proxy (`createSourceProxyServers`), so its client never connects to a channel-capable server. codex — nothing in `packages/codex-protocol/generated`. Host owns the dispatcher (`applyChannelPolicy`/`subscribeChannel`) and the sink (`deliverMonitorEvent`, already ungated and runtime-neutral) — case (2), the host is the seam. For plugin-shipped channels the adapter recognises the component and records a deferral (`plugin.deferred.claude_channel`, `CLAUDE_RETAINED_SURFACE_FIELDS` `{ externalKind: 'channel', field: 'channels' }`) and nothing consumes it.
- **Why absent today:** J20 is ANSWERED — delegated decision 2026-09-05, **Q-den 37 = option 2** (a per-session arming set SEEDED from the persistent preference; option 1 refused because a server that may push text into a turn keeps that right in every future session, option 3 refused because re-arming every session is the shape people switch off entirely), LANDED P6.510 (`9376f8501 + cf0d85dbd + d775f0e48 + 06fec66c2`). J29 is LANDED too — P6.341 armed a channel on the server's own declaration on all three runtimes (both halves). Both ledger cells are stale. J30 alone has no ruling: searched the plan (21 `channels` hits, all substring noise) and the out-of-scope register (0).
- **Options:**
  1. **Leave plugin-shipped channels permanently deferred and SURFACE the deferral by name** (size XS (the deferral reason code already exists; the missing half is one diagnostic line)) — a plugin that ships channels installs and says so instead of silently dropping a component; nobody gains the ability to put text before the model on a grant given for something else.
  2. **Build them host-side for all three, gated on the plugin's hook trust grant** (size L) — one implementation serving every runtime — and a plugin's MCP server gains the right to inject text mid-turn on the strength of a consent the user gave for hooks; the blast radius of 'trusted plugin' grows in a direction nobody was asked about.
  3. **Build them only where mid-turn steering already exists (Pi)** (size M) — re-creates the Pi-only shape P6.341 just removed — a new silent divergence at a switch.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1, which is also the sweep's own recommendation (A). Simplest honest thing: the residue is stated, the trust boundary does not move, and nothing is built that a runtime does not already ask for. J20 and J29 need only a re-read against the landed commits.
- **Question (RU):** Оставить ли `channels` из плагинов постоянно deferred с явной причиной, видимой при установке (вариант A), пересчитав J20 и J29 по уже приземлившимся Q-den 37 / P6.510 и P6.341? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 8 cells: J20's 3 and J29's 2 move to covered on the re-read; J30's 3 → n/a-by-decision with a surfaced deferral.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** All three rows are already answered or landed; nothing in this cluster needs the owner. J30 — the question asserts «J30 alone has no ruling: searched the plan (21 `channels` hits, all substring noise)», but the ruling is not in the plan: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-denominator-owner-questions.md` §'[host] Plugin-shipped message channels — allow third-party text injection into a turn?' closes exactly J30 and ends «Recommendation: **option 1.** The deferral is already honest, the capability's whole value is injecting third-party text into a turn, and no user has asked for it. Revisit if a first-party plugin needs it, at which point option 2 with a channel-specific grant (not the hook grant) is the shape to price.» — and `2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md:2668` records that the owner answered every question in that file «with one sentence in chat — «по вопросам — давай тогда как рекомендуешь»», so option 1 is the standing answer, not a live choice. J20 — Q-den 37 = option 2, LANDED P6.510 ✅ `9376f8501 + cf0d85dbd + d775f0e48 + 06fec66c2`. J29 — LANDED P6.341 ✅ `dba775d30`. CORRECTED QUESTION: none — replace with «`channels` из плагинов остаётся deferred по решению 2026-09-05 (вариант 1, с явной причиной при установке); J20 и J29 пересчитываются по `9376f8501+cf0d85dbd+d775f0e48+06fec66c2` и `dba775d30`.» If anything is still owed it is the XS the answer itself names — surfacing `plugin.deferred.claude_channel` by name at install — and that is a lane row, not an owner question.

### QJ6 — What an MCP configuration reaches — the model's own resource tools, the CLI's own discovery, project `.mcp.json` approval, and subagent-scoped servers

- **Rows:** J4, J17, J18, J21
- **Cells:** J4 Pi covered / Claude partial / Codex partial (2). J17 Pi n/a / Claude absent / Codex — (2). J18 Pi absent / Claude partial / Codex absent (3). J21 Pi partial / Claude absent / Codex absent (3). 10 non-covered cells.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp#project-server-approvals-and-workspace-trust — verified by curl 2026-09-11, verbatim: “A cloned repository can't approve its own servers: `enableAllProjectMcpServers` or `enabledMcpjsonServers` committed to the project's `.claude/settings.json` is ignored in an untrusted folder, and the server stays at `⏸ Pending approval`… A `disabledMcpjsonServers` entry in any settings file still rejects the server.” settings-reference#enabledmcpjsonservers / #disabledmcpjsonservers verified. sub-agents#scope-mcp-servers-to-a-subagent verified, plus the explicit `<span id="inline-server-trust" />` anchor (an explicit id, not a heading slug). mcp#use-mcp-resources and #execute-mcp-prompts verified.

J18 — all three keys are in the vendored bundle and resolve from project/local settings through `settingSources`, so a claude session already honours them; Pi/codex reach the host pool with our own consent model. J21 — the SDK DOES accept it (`AgentMcpServerSpec = string | Record<string, McpServerConfigForProcessTransport>`); codex's `[agents.<role>]` has no MCP field. J4 — the claude arm was reached through the SDK's instance factory (`McpSdkServerConfigWithInstance`), refuting P6.25's earlier `cannot-at-this-seam` reading.

J4: the claude arm landed (P6.139 / W6-b); the switch-cost half P6.147 carries **Deferred 2026-09-10** and moved to row `D1.24`. J21: P6.342 ✅ for the reference half; the INLINE half fails at PARSE on all three — `definitions.ts:349` puts `mcpServers` in ARRAY_FRONTMATTER_KEYS and `:739` normalizes it with `normalizeExplicitStringList`, so a server OBJECT is rejected everywhere.

### QJ7 — The one host MCP pool's operability — timeouts and startup blocking, mid-session reconnect, discovery cache, the `/mcp` surface, and named configuration diagnostics

- **Rows:** J5, J43, J44, J46, J47
- **Cells:** J5 Pi partial / Claude covered / Codex partial (2). J43 Pi absent / Claude absent / Codex absent (3). J44 Pi absent / Claude absent / Codex absent (3). J46 Pi partial / Claude partial / Codex partial (3). J47 Pi partial / Claude partial / Codex partial (3). 14 non-covered cells.

Verbatim: “Claude Code reconnects a dropped remote server with exponential backoff: up to five attempts, starting at a one-second delay and doubling it each time. … Stdio servers are local processes, and Claude Code doesn't reconnect them automatically.”

Two of the page's three retries already exist host-side (first-connection `MCP_CONNECT_RETRY {attempts:3, baseDelayMs:250}` and discovery-request retry); the mid-session arm was the missing one. The vendored bundle is now **0.3.247 / Claude Code 2.1.247** (read from node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk/package.json, 2026-09-11) — past the 2.1.238 at which the page records `MCP_DISCOVERY_CACHE` as OFF by default, so the ledger's version reasoning for J44 is stale in our favour.
- **Why absent today:** Mostly landed and stale in the ledger. J43+J44: P6.351 / W7-j landed both entries, and corrected the premise — `callTool`'s cold-start branch was guarded by the very `failedConnections` set the close handler adds to, so a dropped server was lost for the whole SESSION, not just its tool list; J44's capability ships under another name (`toolDescriptorStore`). J47: P6.350 / W7-k landed 2 of 3 diagnostics and found the third already at HEAD as `capability.collision.mcp-scope-precedence`. J46: P6.855 / W9-ap read all three unread clauses — the READ half (tool count, connected-but-zero-tools, the non-interactive summary) is whole on `/mcp` for all three, the WRITE half (Switch + Reconnect) is whole in the panel, and the residue is exactly **P6.750, a HOST gap — 'the three verdicts move together or not at all'**. J5: the startup-blocking half is measured at STRONG tier on codex (P2.372, byte-identical binary, `required = true` fails both `thread/start` and `thread/resume`) and DELIBERATELY NOT TAKEN (P2.508): `writeCodexConfig` emits exactly TWO `[mcp_servers.*]` entries, `session` and `sources`, so the key could only ever mark our own infrastructure. The connect-default flip is P6.144 ⬜, **Release sitting 2026-09-10 — gates 1.36.0 (P7.5)**.
- **Options:**
  1. **Take P6.750 — the single host residue that moves J46 on all three runtimes at once — and record the rest against the landed rows** (size S) — one capability closes on three runtimes for one piece of work; the `/mcp`-vs-panel split stops being a partial verdict nobody can act on.
  2. **Also drive the SDK's unused `reconnectMcpServer`/`toggleMcpServer` setters from the panel** (size M) — native-first tier 1 taken on claude specifically — and it makes the panel behave differently per runtime unless the host wraps it, which is the divergence the host exists to prevent.
  3. **Leave J46 partial and state the residue** (size XS) — three cells stay partial forever over a gap that is already scoped and named.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. It is the smallest thing that moves the most cells, it is host-side (case 2, the simplest coverage), and P6.855 already established that the three verdicts are one verdict. J5's codex `required` should be recorded as a stated null with P2.508's reason rather than written — configuration that can only mark our own two infrastructure entries is not the documented capability.

### QJ8 — The five deferred measurements — root-level combinators, MCP auto-backgrounding, parallel-batch failure, and the two unprobed Write behaviours

- **Rows:** J41, J45, J49, J50, J51
- **Cells:** J41 Pi partial / Claude partial / Codex — (3). J45 Pi covered / Claude — / Codex absent (2). J49 — / — / — (3). J50 — / — / — (3). J51 — / — / — (3). 14 non-covered cells, 11 of them outright no-claims.
- **Docs:** code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp#tool-input-schemas-with-a-root-level-combinator — verified, verbatim: “Tools with a root-level combinator stay available. Before sending the tool to the API, Claude Code flattens the schema into a single object and prepends a sentence to the tool's description that tells Claude which parameter groups belong together.” #automatic-backgrounding-of-long-tool-calls — verified, verbatim: “An MCP tool call in the main conversation that is still running after two minutes moves to a background task instead of blocking the session… Automatic backgrounding requires Claude Code v2.1.212 or later.” tools-reference#write-tool-behavior — verified, verbatim: “Claude Opus 4.6, Claude Haiku 4.5, and older models always require the read, so a Write to an unread existing file fails with an error. Newer models can overwrite a file they never read this session under the same conditions as read-before-edit… Jupyter notebooks, and files Claude has read only partially with a `PARTIAL view` notice, require the read on every model.”
- **Native:** J45 — claude has it natively and the bundle is now **2.1.247**, well past the documented 2.1.212, but our servers reach the SDK as in-process proxies and `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS` appears in no agent file, so the claude cell is genuinely unknown rather than absent; Pi built its own (`mcpAutoBackgroundDelayMs`, consumed only inside the subprocess); codex has no equivalent message in codex-protocol/generated. J41 — three uncoordinated treatments and none is the page's: claude's root converter `jsonSchemaToZodShape` reads only `schema.properties`, so a root combinator with no sibling properties yields an EMPTY parameter shape and the tool ships argument-less; Pi's gateway proxy STRIPS the four keywords; codex unknown. J49/J50/J51 — decided inside each runtime's own loop (claude in the closed 2.1.247 binary, codex in rust-v0.148.0, Pi in our fork), nothing host-side mediates them, so there is no seam to unify and nothing to build until they are measured.
- **Why absent today:** All five plan rows are ✅-as-moved, not done: P6.354 (J41), P6.355 (J45), P6.356 (J49), P6.357 (J50, J51) — and P6.353 (J40, clustered separately) — each carries **Deferred 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 2): → the follow-up plan (P7.7), verdicts intact** and was MOVED into `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md`. The owner is deciding their DISPOSITION here, not their timing. Note the shape of the risk: J41's claude failure is SILENT argument loss, which no user would report as a schema problem.
- **Options:**
  1. **Run all five in ONE rig sitting on the packaged build — they share a rig shape (one turn, one control each)** (size M for the sitting; each individual rig is S) — 11 no-claims and 3 partials leave the no-verdict column with real verdicts; the ledger's weakest column shrinks by the largest single amount available in this family. Requires a packaged build and, for the Write arms, one CDP case row.
  2. **Leave them in the follow-up plan as declared no-claims** (size XS) — honest but permanently uncounted — a `—` is not a refusal, so 14 cells can never reach 95-100 % under the stated goal.
  3. **Mark them n/a-by-decision without measuring** (size XS) — refused: it would score 'nobody measured it' as 'we decided not to have it', the exact inversion §Cell vocabulary was written to prevent.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. This is the only cluster in family J where the blocker is neither a build nor a posture but an unmade measurement, and the goal counts refusals — not nulls. One sitting converts the family's entire no-claim block; the balance rule favours it because no code is written at all until a verdict says which arm is wrong.

That plan's own header records its birth «under the owner ruling of 2026-09-10, points 2 and 6 — "the non-essential is deferred to the very end, and probably to a separate plan, with a clear pass over what is left, so the owner can read what each remaining row is for"». So option 2's «honest but permanently uncounted» is wrong — they are counted, in a live plan, each with a `done_means` — and the question's own claim that «The owner is deciding their DISPOSITION here, not their timing» contradicts option 1, which is purely a timing proposal (run one sitting now). CORRECTED QUESTION: «Оставить ли пять измерений (J41→D1.54, J45→D1.55, J49→D1.56, J50/J51→D1.57) в follow-up плане по решению 2026-09-10, или — если 14 ячеек «нет вердикта» нужно закрыть раньше — передвинуть эти четыре строки вперёд ВНУТРИ того плана одной упакованной сессией, не переоткрывая диспозицию?» The substantive finding worth keeping is J41's claude arm: `jsonSchemaToZodShape` reads only `schema.properties`, so a root combinator with no sibling properties ships an argument-less tool — a SILENT argument loss, which is the reason to move D1.54 forward rather than to re-open the ruling.

### QJ9 — What a host tool may hand back — exact fields, pictures and documents, and the read-only hint that lets calls run concurrently

- **Rows:** J27, J28, J48
- **Cells:** J27 Pi partial / Claude covered / Codex covered (1). J28 Pi partial / Claude partial / Codex partial (3). J48 Pi partial / Claude covered / Codex absent (2). 6 non-covered cells.

- **Native:** All three accept more than text: claude via `tool()` (the SDK we vendor, so the page IS the contract), codex via `McpToolCallResult` whose `content` is an untyped `Array<JsonValue>`, Pi via its MCP client. The ceiling is entirely OURS — the shared registry's result type is `content: TextContent[]` (session-tools-core/src/types.ts:159-168), and `browser-tools.ts:17-24` proves the runtimes accept images by declaring its OWN local result type carrying `{ type: 'image', data, mimeType }` outside the registry. For concurrency: claude is SDK-native and the host already takes the documented opt-in (`readOnlyHint` on its own custom tools); Pi HAS a native `agent.toolExecution: 'parallel' | 'sequential'` knob which the host sets to `'parallel'` unconditionally — one blanket setting, no read-only/state-changing distinction; codex has a per-MCP-server `supports_parallel_tool_calls` written by nothing in this tree.
- **Why absent today:** P6.345 / W7-r landed the shared tool-result contract (the text ceiling lifted for `structuredContent`), and the ledger's 'claude DROPS it' clause was refuted at HEAD — `session-scoped-tools.ts:214-220` forwards it, pinned by `session-tool-result-fidelity.test.ts`. Two residues remain and both are named: P6.617 (Pi's sidecar `extraContent`/`details` path is the last of the four adapters to honour it) and the session MCP server, which maps only `{ name, description, inputSchema }` at `session-mcp-server/src/index.ts:329-333` — `readOnly` scores 0 in that whole `src`, so codex and Pi never see the hint the registry already carries.
- **Options:**
  1. **Map `readOnly` in the session MCP server and finish P6.617** (size S — one field in one mapper plus a named residue row) — the hint the registry already carries reaches the two runtimes that cannot see it; J48's codex cell stops being absent over a one-line omission.
  2. **Widen the shared registry result type to carry image and resource blocks** (size M) — real parity with `#return-images-and-resources` on all three — and a shared contract change that touches every handler family; the browser tools' local bypass would then be retired, which is more churn than the capability is worth today.
  3. **Record the image/resource ceiling as a stated residue with the browser-tools bypass as the sanctioned exception** (size XS) — a host tool still cannot hand back a picture unless it declares its own type; stated, not silent.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1 plus option 3's record. Balance rule: the S moves two cells for one field in one file and takes a native opt-in that already exists on two runtimes; the M is real parity but its whole benefit today is served by one deliberate bypass that already works and is documented at its site. Audio is out either way — the page itself says the TS SDK saves it to disk and Python drops it with a warning.

(1) «the session MCP server … maps only `{ name, description, inputSchema }` at `session-mcp-server/src/index.ts:329-333` — `readOnly` scores 0 in that whole `src`» is false at HEAD: `packages/session-mcp-server/src/index.ts` reads «P6.345 — `readOnly` lives on the canonical registry def and six tool families set it, but this map used to carry only `{ name, description, inputSchema }`, so `readOnlyHint` reached no MCP client», then `const readOnly = registry.get(def.name)?.readOnly === true;` and `...(readOnly ? { annotations: { readOnlyHint: true } } : {})`, pinned by `packages/session-mcp-server/src/read-only-hint.test.ts` («P6.345 — the MCP advertisement carries readOnlyHint», with a writer tool as the negative control). `readOnly` scores 5 in that file and 12 in the test, not 0. CORRECTED QUESTION: «Пересчитать ли J27/J28/J48 по приземлившимся P6.345 `5a181b9fc` и P6.617 `1a7da5be7` — `readOnly` домаплен в session-mcp-server, `extraContent` несёт картинки и ресурсы через общий реестр — оставив единственным (XS) остатком снятие локального типа результата в `browser-tools.ts`, и не открывая ни S, ни M?»

- **Why absent today:** Half of this cluster is landed and stale, half is a stated asymmetry. LANDED: J36 (P6.348 / W8-i, `5b9c42dd9` — and the premise INVERTED: claude's Glob is the only file search of the three that ignores `.gitignore`, Pi's `find` and codex's `respect_gitignore` both default on); J34's output-ceiling half and J33's route (P6.346, `05e9a8c46 + 188d1f76d + d98a3a291`) with the memory-pressure reaper shipping OFF; J35 CONFIRMED and sharpened but NOT built (P6.347 / W7-h); J38 carried by P6.690 — Pi's `createBashToolDefinition(cwd, options)` binds `cwd` ONCE at construction and every exec passes it, so there is no carry-over to turn off and `Shell cwd was reset to` scores 0 files: the user is never told. J1's codex cell has never been scored. Every one of these rows' own `done_means` offers the same alternative verbatim: “…or the difference is named in the switch report.”
- **Options:**
  1. **Take the page's own alternative: name the claude-only names and keys in the switch report, and BUILD only J35's safety clause (a `PARTIAL view` read must not satisfy Write)** (size S for the switch rows (the `webSearch` row is the working template: a per-runtime `on` map, both locales, a gaps-doc row), S–M for the Write coupling) — an operator learns at the switch that a cap, a shell choice or a cwd carry-over applies on one runtime only — instead of meeting silence; and the one clause with a correctness consequence (a partial read counting as a read) is closed everywhere.
  2. **Honour every documented name host-side on Pi and codex** (size M per name, ~5 names → L in total) — true parity across four env names and two settings keys — a permanent second implementation of behaviour the vendor owns, for knobs that mostly buy latency and ergonomics rather than correctness.
  3. **Do neither and record the asymmetry only in the ledger** (size XS) — silent divergence, which this fork already names as its largest defect class.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. It is what the rows' own `done_means` sentences admit, it is the cheapest honest coverage, and it separates the one safety clause from seven ergonomics clauses. Of the whole cluster only J35's second half changes what can go wrong: a partial read satisfying Write is data loss, everything else is a preference an operator can see.

Verbatim: “Set `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_POWERSHELL_TOOL=1` in your environment **or in `settings.json`**”, and “`"defaultShell": "powershell"` in settings.json: routes interactive `!` commands through PowerShell. - **Native:** claude has the tool in the binary and in the SDK types (`CLAUDE_CODE_USE_POWERSHELL_TOOL` → 13 in the vendored binary; `PowerShell` → 1 in sdk.d.ts). Our permission surface is half-built for it already: `claude-powershell-rule.ts` exists and the DSL carries a `powershell` matcher, and hooks can already request PowerShell. Pi would need a second shell executor; codex has no PowerShell tool in `packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/`.

LANDED as P6.530, pinned to the vendor's `0` on every arm. The residue is recorded as **P6.688**: the page names a SECOND route — the `env` block in `settings.json` — and we pass `settingSources: ['user','project','local']`, so a repository can still switch the tool on where our refusal does not reach.

All three cells then close as refusal-by-decision.
  2. **Leave P6.688 open and state it** (size XS) — a repository can enable a shell tool our permission floor does not fully cover — the one direction where an unclosed refusal is a safety hole, not an ergonomics gap.
  3. **Reverse Q-den 67 and enable PowerShell properly** (size M) — re-opens a decision made six days ago and creates the switch divergence the owner refused; only in scope if Windows becomes a supported deployment.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. It is the S that makes an already-taken decision true, and it is closest to the page — which itself names both routes in the same paragraph, so a refusal that covers one is not the documented shape.
- **Question (RU):** Закрыть ли вторую задокументированную дверь PowerShell (ключ в блоке `env` файла `settings.json`, достижимый через `settingSources`) остатком P6.688, чтобы отказ Q-den 67 стал полным и три ячейки J31 закрылись как отказ-по-решению? [да]

The SDK's only model input is model ('Model alias … or full model ID'), which a TIER is not — so K7's tier cannot travel by construction. codex — agents.default_subagent_model and default_subagent_reasoning_effort are native in AgentsToml and ARE now written (codexOperatorSubagentModelOption into config-writer.ts defaultSubagentModel, pinned by __tests__/config-writer-agent-roles.test.ts), which is what moved K10-codex to covered; but codex has NO alias vocabulary at all — alias, model_alias and aliases each score 0 in config-schema.json against present controls. Pi — the host resolver IS the mechanism and binds, but spawner.ts resolves resolveSubagentModelOverride() FIRST, above per-call and frontmatter, i.e. in the order the page retired at v2.1.251. K6 native: NONE on any runtime — measured on the shipped darwin-arm64 codex-app-server (P6.164), a pinned model = "opus" is FORWARDED VERBATIM: nothing is substituted, refused or resolved, and the warning the binary does emit fires identically for the gateway's own default id, so it carries no information about servability.
- **Why absent today:** K6 STRUCTURAL: on a gateway or BYO endpoint an alias has nothing to resolve against, so absent is the honest verdict, and the switch is already REPORTED (P6.161 promoted it from SILENT). K7 STRUCTURAL on claude: the SDK takes model, not tier, so a bundled agent runs on the SESSION model there and on its tier's model on Pi (P3.277); codex absent because per-tier routing is not codex's shape. K10-claude partial: the projection exists but only under FORCE, because the vendored binary implements the retired order and applying it unconditionally would OUTRANK a subagent's own model instead of defaulting it (P6.364). K11: Pi partial because it implements the superseded order; claude/codex absent because the FORCE knob is not in the bundle and codex has no equivalent — and the page's own skill-origin carve-out ('A skill that runs in a subagent with model: inherit') is UNEXPRESSIBLE on every runtime, recorded at the gate rather than built (P6.627, 4d5e910ef). No row here carries Deferred or Release-sitting; all four are closed rows with stated residues.
- **Options:**
  1. **Record the whole family as refused-by-structure with its measured reasons, and revisit K10/K11 when the bundle reaches 2.1.257** (size XS) — 8 cells become n/a-by-decision or absent-with-a-named-reason; nothing new can silently diverge; the gateway user keeps being told at switch time which alias-pinned agents change meaning (already built), and the FORCE precedence stays whatever the shipped binary does rather than whatever we wish it did.
  2. **Build a host-side precedence layer that reorders subagent-model resolution to the page's post-2.1.251 order on all three, ahead of the bundle** (size M) — One order everywhere, matching today's page; but it fights the vendored binary on claude (we would re-order underneath a runtime that re-orders again), and it is the invent-a-mechanism shape §READ-do-not-INVENT ranks last — the same shape that cost P2.306 and P3.30.
  3. **Build a host alias resolver mapping opus/sonnet/haiku onto real gateway ids before the child starts** (size M) — K6 would resolve rather than forward — but it invents a mapping the gateway does not publish, and a wrong mapping is silent: the run succeeds on the wrong model.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. Three of the four causes are the runtime's own shape rather than our omission, and each already has a user-visible surface (the REPORTED alias switch line, the FORCE debug sentence, the recorded skill-origin carve-out). Waiting for 2.1.257 is configuration; building underneath the vendor is construction against a moving contract.

The cluster says 'Pi — spawner.ts resolves resolveSubagentModelOverride() FIRST, above per-call and frontmatter, i.e. in the order the page retired at v2.1.251'. At HEAD packages/pi-agent-server/src/orchestrator/spawner.ts:504-536 resolves it at TIER 3: `if (perCallModel === 'inherit' || explicitModel === 'inherit') return { resolved: inherited() }` … `if (envModel) return orInherited(envModel)`, with the pre-2.1.251 order restored only under `isSubagentModelOverrideForced()` and the page's fork carve-out honoured. The same commit gave codex the reach (codex-agent.ts:4148 codexOperatorSubagentModelOption → [agents] default_subagent_model) and records, at the site, why FORCE is deliberately NOT projected there ('the FORCE distinction is unobservable here and inventing a projection for it would be a mechanism, not a configuration') — so the codex `absent` is mis-framed too. Codex's residue is protocol-level and correctly disclosed — building the sink on an unproven id correlation is precisely the invent-a-mechanism ordering §READ-do-not-INVENT ranks last.

- **Options:**
  1. **Re-score the family off the closed plan rows and their commits, marker `ᵗ`, one documentation pass, no code** (size S–M) — Up to 156 cells stop lying. Rows whose done_means was fully met become `covered`; rows that closed with a stated residue (L30 — of the four forms its title names one arrived; L61, L74, L77) stay `partial` with the residue named; L22's Pi/Claude blanks become `n/a`. Cost: the evidence stays at the `ᵗ` tier — a landed commit, not a live turn — so nothing here becomes behaviour-proven, and the six behaviour-proven cells in the whole file stay six.
  2. **Re-measure every cell live before moving it** (size L) — The strongest possible grid, and unreachable at this size: 156 cells over three runtimes is a wave of its own, and the ledger has run exactly six behaviour-proofs in its life. It would also make the 95–100% target depend on rig time rather than on decisions.
  3. **Defer — leave the cells as they are until the follow-up plan P7.7** (size —) — The published coverage figure keeps understating the product by roughly a quarter of this family, and every future reader re-derives the same staleness by hand, which is what cost this reading two hours.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 1. It is the balance rule applied literally: closest to the runtime's own documentation (nothing new is invented — each row already cites its page), least code (none), simplest host coverage (one pass over one file). The ledger's own vocabulary already rates a landed commit as the strongest evidence it accepts, so this is not a lowering of the bar — it is the bar being applied to work that already met it. The three spot checks all confirmed the build shipped, in the direction of pessimism, which is the direction §"What this is NOT" already warns this file drifts.

The measured reason per-KEY provenance has no home: there is no whole-settings merge in this host — four narrow readers of `.claude/settings.json` (`capabilities/native-sources.ts`, `agent/memory/instruction-excludes.ts`, `config/skill-overrides.ts`, `config/config-doctor.ts`) with three genuinely different combine rules (`last-wins`, `union`, `winner-takes-all`) that never meet, so a per-key table would be a fictional fifth resolver. What is left is four residues nobody has ruled on: per-key provenance, a session-level report of which sources are active (including how managed settings reached the machine), a narrowable `settingSources`, and the documented config-dir spelling. A product posture is being stated here, not decided in passing: this fork owns settings composition and does not delegate it, and that is why per-key provenance has nowhere to live.

### QL4 — Auto-memory: background distillation, per-chat scope, where it is written, and codex's whole native subsystem sitting inside an ephemeral home

- **Rows:** L37, L49, L51, L52
- **Cells:** Pi 3 partial / 1 absent; Claude 2 partial / 1 absent / 1 no-verdict; Codex 2 partial / 2 absent — 12 non-covered cells
- **Docs:** `memory.md#auto-memory`, `#enable-or-disable-auto-memory`, `#claude-md-vs-auto-memory`, `#storage-location`, `#audit-and-edit-your-memory` (221 ids, verified today); `settings-reference.md#automemorydirectory` (the page's own id list, 2 271 ids). Codex, STRONG tier — re-fetched from source today at `rust-v0.148.0`: `codex-rs/config/src/types.rs:290-319` `pub struct MemoriesToml` with exactly twelve fields (`disable_on_external_context`, `generate_memories`, `use_memories`, `dedicated_tools`, `max_raw_memories_for_consolidation`, `max_unused_days`, `max_rollout_age_days`, `max_rollouts_per_startup`, `min_rollout_idle_hours`, `min_rate_limit_remaining_percent`, `extract_model`, `consolidation_model`), plus `config_toml.rs:441 pub memories: Option<MemoriesToml>`.
- **Native:** codex — HAS the entire capability, extraction model and consolidation model included, and it is unreachable for a HOST reason rather than a codex one: `~/.codex/memories/` lives inside the per-session `mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(),'nessy-codex-home-'))` that `cleanupRuntimeResources` deletes at teardown. Making it reachable means making CODEX_HOME durable, which is a supply-chain posture, not a feature toggle. claude — the SDK maintains its own memory tree we do not address; the OFF switch does reach it (`applyAutoMemoryOffSwitch` in `packages/shared/src/agent/options.ts`), so L3 is covered on all three. Pi/host — `remember` is a host session tool, model-invoked only; there is no per-session memory scope at all (`memory-store.ts:546` `if (!isMemoryEnabled()) return ''` is one global switch). Already shipped and NOT absent: `autoMemoryDirectory` end to end (P6.390(2), `getAutoMemoryDirectory` + `parseAutoMemoryDirectory`), and the four documented kinds (`AUTO_MEMORY_KINDS = ['user','feedback','project','reference']`, `stampMemoryType`, refused-by-name on an unknown kind). Still open: `plansDirectory`, and the topic-file leak named verbatim in our own source at `capabilities/native-sources.ts:685-691` — an imported layer's body is not index-capped, so a topic file loads eagerly while the line requesting it does not.
- **Why absent today:** Mixed. L51 and L52 are `partial` because half of each shipped and the other half did not (`plansDirectory`; the skip-what-the-codebase-already-says clause). L37 is a live divergence our own source names. L49 is OWNER-class size L and was NOT among the thirteen questions the owner answered on 2026-09-05. No plan row covers any of the four.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build the whole page: a durable CODEX_HOME so codex's native memories run, plus host-side background distillation for Pi and claude** (size L) — Background distillation is a privacy and retention posture (finished chats become durable model input with no user action), and a durable CODEX_HOME changes what a repository can influence. Two postures for one row.
  2. **Finish the two cheap halves — `plansDirectory` beside the shipped `autoMemoryDirectory`, and on-demand reading of auto-memory topic files — and refuse-and-record background distillation and per-chat memory scope** (size S + S) — Users get memory only when the model asks for it, which is what they have today and understand; the topic-file leak stops costing startup context; codex's native subsystem is recorded as deliberately unreachable with the ephemeral-CODEX_HOME reason attached, so the next reader does not re-open it as a defect.
  3. **Defer to P7.7** (size —) — The topic-file leak keeps charging every session for material nobody asked for, and our own source keeps naming it with nothing acting on it.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 2. Native-first says ask the runtime first, and the answer came back honestly: codex has the mechanism and the host is what blocks it, so this is not a missing capability but a stated cost of our per-session-home topology. Everything else here is host-owned, and the two S-sized halves are the smallest honest things that close real user-visible divergences. Background distillation stays refused because it is a product posture — chats becoming durable inputs without a user action — and stating it is the job, not deciding it quietly.
- **Question (RU):** Закрываем ли `plansDirectory` и ленивое чтение topic-файлов авто-памяти (S+S), а фоновую дистилляцию завершённых чатов и пер-чатовую область памяти отказываем и записываем причину — включая то, что нативная система памяти codex недостижима из-за эфемерного CODEX_HOME, а не из-за codex? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 6 cells (L37 ×3, L51 ×3) move to `covered`; 4 cells (L49 Pi/Claude/Codex plus its blank) become `n/a`-by-decision with the recorded reason; L52's 3 stay `partial` with the one unshipped clause named.
- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** REFUTED on the first of its two 'cheap halves'. `plansDirectory` is not open — it landed. `packages/shared/src/config/storage.ts` carries a full docblock headed '**P6.390(2) — `plansDirectory`: where plan files are written.**' quoting `settings-reference.md#plansdirectory` verbatim and implementing the bundle's own containment-and-fallback rule; `packages/shared/src/agent/claude-agent.ts` reads it at four sites tagged P6.704 ('the configured `plansDirectory` when set and contained'); tests exist at `packages/shared/src/config/__tests__/memory-and-plans-directories.test.ts` and `packages/shared/src/agent/__tests__/plans-directory-agent-sites.test.ts`. So the S+S becomes S. The topic-file leak SURVIVES but its citation is wrong: it is not at line 685 of `packages/shared/src/capabilities/native-sources.ts` (that is `markdownAgentRecords`) — the leak comment sits ~270 lines later, reading 'not index-capped, so that topic file still loads while the line requesting it does not.' CORRECTED QUESTION: «Закрываем ли ленивое чтение topic-файлов авто-памяти (S — `plansDirectory` уже закрыт P6.390(2)+P6.704), а фоновую дистилляцию и пер-чатовую область памяти отказываем и записываем причину, включая недостижимость нативной памяти codex из-за эфемерного CODEX_HOME?»

- **Native:** codex — three requests published and NONE called; a tree-wide grep outside `codex-protocol/` returns only unrelated `skillsList.*` i18n keys. `skills/extraRoots/set` is codex's own answer to L4's added-directory skills and `skills/config/write` its own answer to L9's per-skill disable, and today we re-implement both by writing files codex then re-reads. One key IS already projected — `bundled.enabled` via `codexBundledSkillsOption` (P6.534, `e2b4c1fbb`) — so `include_instructions` and the per-skill `[[skills.config]]` selectors are the deliberate residue. `agents/openai.yaml`'s `policy.allow_implicit_invocation: false` is codex's per-skill implicit gate and is never read; a test even pins that we do not read it. claude — `SDKSystemMessage` carries `skills: string[]` beside `slash_commands: string[]` on the init message (`sdk.d.ts` of the vendored 0.3.247, `skills` 42 hits) and `backend/claude/event-adapter.ts:1267` reads only the last; `Options.skills` is never passed; the native `Skill` tool is BANNED by a recorded decision (L16) because a skill's `allowed-tools`/`disallowed-tools` are, per Claude's own docs, not applied through the SDK, so handing skills back would lose per-skill tool restrictions. Pi — `disableModelInvocation` exists in the extension descriptor and on the base agent, so the axis is real there. Host — `skill-overrides.ts` defines four states with three ungated enforcement points, so the per-skill layer is uniform across all four backends; `disableAllSkills` scores 0 files, while its sibling `disableAllHooks` is honoured from a settings file with a managed half that outranks it, so the shape to copy already exists one layer over.
- **Why absent today:** DECISION for L11 and L16 (one host marker for three runtimes; our skill layer is stronger than the vendor's), DEFECT for L57 and L58, and MEASURE-not-yet-run for L66/L67 — P6.408 is the only ⬜ plan row in this family and carries "Release sitting 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 5): gates 1.36.0 — P7.5", so it is already queued and the owner is deciding its disposition, not its timing.
- **Options:**
  1. **Adopt the native interfaces: read `init.skills` on claude, call `skills/list` on codex, write `[[skills.config]]` selectors, read `openai.yaml`'s implicit gate, add a session-wide `disableAllSkills`** (size M) — Two tier-1 reads cost a call site each and turn L66's `partial` into an honest roster on the runtimes that publish one. The `[[skills.config]]` half re-implements host state into codex's file and can drift from our own overrides — two authorities for one question.
  2. **Refuse-and-record: the host layer is the only skill authority; `$plugin:skill`/`@mention`, the native `Skill` tool, codex's skills interface and `openai.yaml` are all deliberate refusals with their reasons** (size S) — A user copy-pasting a vendor prompt template that reads `Use $codex-security:triage-finding to …` still gets no resolution, and the session still cannot answer "which skills actually loaded" on any runtime.
  3. **Defer L66/L67 only (already sitting) and rule on the rest** (size —) — Splits cleanly: the measurement row is already queued behind 1.36.0 and does not need re-deciding.

The documented operator override IS honoured (`SLASH_COMMAND_TOOL_CHAR_BUDGET`, P3.303) and now on all three rather than only on claude, which is the divergence that closure removed. The two unread settings keys are a STATED residue, and the residue already meets the operator: `apps/cli/src/doctor.ts` carries `SKILL_LISTING_BUDGET_DOCTOR` (landed `3084bf664`), which tells an operator who set `skillListingBudgetFraction` that this build does not read it and names the two channels that are read, reading both numbers out of the module's own source so doctor and budget cannot drift.

The cell reads `partial` only because nobody re-scored it against that answer.

### QL8 — Plugin peripherals and the runtime's own plugin loader: a language-server supervisor this fork does not have, and `plugins: []`

- **Rows:** L15, L27, L65, L83

Codex, STRONG tier: a grep of the shipped protocol bundle for any method name containing `workflow` returns zero matches, with the `Skills/*` family matching four as the positive control — codex has no workflow runtime to hand a plugin script to.
- **Native:** claude — `Options.plugins?: SdkPluginConfig[]` accepting `{type:'local',path}` is real in the vendored 0.3.247 (`plugins` → 42 hits in `sdk.d.ts`) and is deliberately handed `[]`, because our adapters install the same servers, agents and hooks and enabling both double-delivers. codex — no plugin loader; only `$CODEX_HOME/state/plugins/<name>/` as a durable home. Pi — the host installer IS Pi's loader, so `n/a` is right there. The peripheral question was SPLIT by P6.175 into `PLUGIN_MONITOR_SUPPORT` and `PLUGIN_LSP_SUPPORT`, so a switch report can now say that monitors survive while language servers do not (`stoppedMonitors` empty on a pi→claude switch while `lostLsp` names the servers, in the same delta) — the monitor half is COVERED on claude since that split. What genuinely does not exist anywhere in this fork is a language-server supervisor with a restart policy or diagnostics gating: `CLAUDE_UNREAD_LSP_FIELDS` names all eight knobs (transport, initializationOptions, settings, startupTimeout, shutdownTimeout, restartOnCrash, maxRestarts, diagnostics) and the rationale at `format-adapters/claude.ts:2236-2264` says exactly that — *"tuning knobs for a language-server supervisor Nessy does not have"*. Its catalogue evidence: 12 of 276 official entries declare `lspServers` and TWO declare any knob. On the failure path the reason is computed and dropped into a log line (`announcePluginLspUnavailable`) with nothing writing a `PluginDiagnostic`.
- **Why absent today:** DECISION for `plugins: []` (double-delivery), STRUCTURAL for the missing supervisor and for plugin-distributed workflows (`canDispatchWorkflowCommands()` is false everywhere and overridden only on Pi; a non-Pi session already gets `settings.ai.workflows.dispatchUnavailable`). L82 and L83's Pi cells sit behind CLOSED plan rows (P6.417) that did not change the verdict, which is the honest outcome for a loss that is still a loss.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build an LSP supervisor honouring the eight knobs, and delegate plugin loading to the SDK's own loader** (size L) — A whole supervision subsystem for a surface 12 of 276 catalogue entries use and 2 configure; delegating the loader re-opens double-delivery, which is the reason `plugins: []` was chosen.
  2. **Refuse-and-record permanently: no supervisor, plugin LSP stays a REPORTED switch loss, plugin-distributed workflows stay Pi-only — plus one XS fix, a `PluginDiagnostic` where today there is only a log line** (size S (XS of it code)) — A user installing a code-intelligence plugin gets an error they can act on instead of a log line, and the eight knobs are declared unread at the adapter rather than implied. The capability itself stays unavailable, visibly.
  3. **Defer** (size —) — The failure path keeps writing to a log nobody reads, which is the one part of this cluster that costs a user something today.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 2. Native-first was asked and answered in both directions: claude HAS a loader and we decline it for a measured reason (double-delivery), codex has neither loader nor workflow runtime, and Pi already is the loader — so there is nothing to enable, and building is L-sized work against a surface almost nobody uses. The XS diagnostic is the smallest honest thing: it converts a silent failure into a visible one, which is the pattern this file calls HONEST and asks lanes to copy.
- **Question (RU):** Записываем ли отказ навсегда — собственного супервизора language-server не будет, плагинные LSP остаются потерей, о которой сообщает switch-отчёт (`lostLsp`), а рабочие процессы из плагинов остаются только на Pi — и добавляем при этом единственный XS: `PluginDiagnostic` вместо log-строки при отсутствующем бинарнике языкового сервера? [да]

- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** REFUTED on its only code item. The failure path does NOT end in a log line — `SessionManager.announcePluginLspUnavailable` already sends a user-facing session event (`type: 'info'`, `level: 'warning'`, i18n key `chat.pluginLsp.unavailable`, fingerprinted per registration so it does not repeat per capability generation), and its own docblock states the reader's premise in the past tense: 'Both failure sites used to end in `sessionLog.warn` ... the author of a plugin whose `command` resolves to nothing, and the user who installed it, had exactly no way to learn the server was never running.' A sibling crash notice landed with it (P6.417 — 'a plugin language server crashed, and the user is told what the supervisor did about it'). So the XS the whole recommendation rests on is already delivered; what is absent is only the specific `PluginDiagnostic` TYPE, not the user-visible answer. Minor: `CLAUDE_UNREAD_LSP_FIELDS` is not at lines 2236-2264 of `packages/shared/src/plugins/format-adapters/claude.ts` — it is ~350 lines further down, with its filter beside it. CORRECTED QUESTION: «Записываем ли отказ навсегда — собственного супервизора language-server не будет, плагинные LSP остаются потерей, о которой сообщает `lostLsp`, рабочие процессы из плагинов остаются Pi-only — БЕЗ кода: пользовательское уведомление при недоступном/упавшем сервере уже есть (`announcePluginLspUnavailable` + P6.417)?»

### QL9 — Plugin install and marketplace mechanics: two silent mis-resolutions, a native reload seam never called, and the scope/CLI half that is a posture

- **Rows:** L13, L28, L75, L76, L79, L87, L97
- **Cells:** Pi 4 partial / 2 absent / 1 no-verdict; Claude 4 partial / 2 absent / 1 no-verdict; Codex 4 partial / 2 absent / 1 no-verdict — 21 non-covered cells
- **Docs:** `plugins/cli-reference.md#plugin-marketplace-add` (was plugin-marketplaces § plugin-marketplace-add) — verbatim, *"To pin to a branch or tag, append `@ref` to the GitHub shorthand or `#ref` to a git URL"* — plus `#host-on-other-git-services`, `#private-repository-authentication-fails`, `#git-repositories`, `#plugin-sources` (657 ids, verified today, and note the page publishes BOTH `#required-fields` and `#required-fields-2`, so a hand-derived anchor for the second occurrence would be wrong); `plugins/loading.md#check-which-stage-a-plugin-reached` (was discover-plugins § apply-plugin-changes-without-restarting), `#add-from-other-git-hosts`, `#use-cli-commands`, `#manage-installed-plugins`; `plugins/overview.md#understand-install-scopes` (was plugins-reference § plugin-installation-scopes), `#cli-commands-reference`, `#plugin-caching-and-file-resolution`, `#distribution-and-versioning-reference`.
- **Native:** claude — `Query.reloadPlugins()` is declared in the vendored SDK (`sdk.d.ts`, 1 hit) and has ZERO callers; the shipped 2.1.247 binary scores `reload-plugins` **57** (probed today; it was 33 at 2.1.233, so the surface is growing, controls `Cookie` → 72 and `git-subdir` → 30). That is a tier-1 answer for the cost of one call site. codex and Pi — none; a host respawn is the mechanism. Host — `SessionManager.refreshPluginRuntime` already rebuilds a live session's runtime and returns `{applied, deferred, untouched}`, deferring busy sessions to the turn boundary, exposed as Plugins → Refresh-runtime; `describePluginActivation` has two states and no cache-cost notice and no force affordance. The two DEFECTS are measured and both are SILENT rather than errors: a `#ref` pin is dropped because the parser never reads the URL fragment, so `https://github.com/acme/plugins#v2.0.0` installs the default branch with zero diagnostics, and the scp form our own add dialog offers (`git@github.com:acme/plugins.git#v1.2.3`) resolves to `unsupported` — and our tests pass only because our GitLab host happens to contain the substring `gitlab`, so `git.tcsbank.ru`, Bitbucket, CodeCommit or Azure DevOps would mis-resolve. On scope: `PluginScope` is three values plus managed and none of the three is the gitignored project-local one the page names; the install RPC takes no scope at all (`grep -an scope … rpc/plugins.ts` → 0 hits); what exists is ENABLEMENT scope, not INSTALL scope. The CLI implements 3 of 10 verbs with five more named as deliberate omissions, and has no install verb at all, which is why `--config` (L87) has no entry point. L97's version fallback chain has no located implementation (`bundle-hash.ts` is a whole-tree content hash for same-version repair, not the page's chain).
- **Why absent today:** DEFECT for L13 (doubled, both silent), BUILDABLE for L75/L76/L87/L97, OWNER for L79 — and L79's question WAS put to the owner on 2026-09-05 ("Is plugin INSTALL scope a user choice, or does one store plus enablement scope stand in for it?") and is one of the few in that round without a landed answer row. L28 (git credentials for a private marketplace) is klass STRUCTURAL size L and has no plan row.
- **Options:**
  1. **Build the whole cluster: fix the ref/scp parse, call `reloadPlugins()`, add the gitignored install scope, add the missing CLI verbs and `--config`, implement the version fallback chain** (size M–L) — Closes the page but mints an install-scope model beside the enablement-scope model we already have — two scope vocabularies for one product, which is the shape that generates support questions.
  2. **Build the DEFECT half only — the `#ref` fragment, the scp form, and non-GitHub/GitLab hosts, plus the one-line `reloadPlugins()` call and the reload-cost notice — and refuse-and-record the scope/CLI/`--config`/version-chain half** (size M) — A tag pin stops silently installing the default branch on our own git host, and plugin changes apply through the runtime's own seam instead of a respawn. Scripted provisioning stays unavailable, stated at the CLI where five verbs are already declared deliberate omissions.
  3. **Defer to P7.7** (size —) — The ref-pin defect is the one item in family L that mis-resolves silently against THIS fork's own git host — deferring it keeps a wrong answer in the product with no diagnostic.
- **Reader's recommendation:** Option 2. The defect half is not a posture and needs no decision — it is a parser that never reads a fragment the page documents, on a host shape that is ours. `reloadPlugins()` is native-first tier 1 at its purest: shipped, growing (33 → 57 hits across two bundle versions), never called, one call site. ### QM10 — Memory diagnostics: a heapdump that cannot contain the process actually leaking, and the leak indicators beside it

- **Rows:** M3, M22
- **Cells:** M3 Claude partial / Codex absent (Pi covered); M22 partial ×3 — 5 non-covered cells
- **Docs:** troubleshooting.md — `#high-cpu-or-memory-usage` (id verified on the rendered page 2026-09-11).
- **Native:** NONE on any runtime, and this was measured rather than assumed: codex publishes no heap-snapshot request at all (the cell is `absent ᵗ`, STRUCTURAL and honest), and the claude SDK exposes no equivalent control request — the loop runs in a child the host's dump does not include. `process.memoryUsage()` and `process.getActiveResourcesInfo()` are the host's own, so this is a host diagnostic end to end.
- **Why absent today:** M3 was promoted `absent` → `partial` for HONESTY rather than capture (`de21285c4` + `e21e4a46b`): `describeHeapdumpConversationCoverage(provider)` now says, before the privacy warning, what the file does and does not contain, and it is a `Record<ModelProvider, …>` so a new runtime BREAKS THE BUILD rather than inheriting a silent “captured”, with an unknown provider reading as NOT captured — the safe direction. The Switch cell is `HONEST` and the finding it records is that the output was MISLEADING rather than missing. M22's growth rate landed (`dc2509cea`, P6.453 — two thirds were already true at HEAD); the residue is the open-handle count and the one comparison sentence naming JS heap against native, on top of `unaccountedNative` which is already computed.
- **Options:**
  1. **A — record the child-process capture gap as covered-by-decision (STRUCTURAL, already named honestly at the point of use) and finish M22's two lines — a handle count and the JS-vs-native sentence** (size XS) — A reader handed one dump knows whether it can explain their leak; nothing pretends to snapshot a process no vendor lets us reach.
  2. **B — build a child-process snapshot path** (size L) — Needs a control request neither vendor publishes; would mean driving the child's own debugger port, a new attack surface for a diagnostic.
  3. **C — leave both partial** (size 0) — Five cells stay open for a gap that is structural on two runtimes and two lines wide on the third.
- **Reader's recommendation:** A. This is the pattern the ledger's own vocabulary calls HONEST and names as “the pattern to copy”: the capability degrades visibly and refuses to pretend.
- **Question (RU):** Незахват дочернего процесса в /heapdump на claude и codex фиксируем как структурный отказ-по-решению (он уже назван честно в самом выводе), а M22 добираем двумя строками — счётчик открытых хэндлов и «JS-куча против нативной»? [да]

Both lines are at HEAD. packages/shared/src/diagnostics/heapdump.ts declares the field — "/** `process.getActiveResourcesInfo()` count, when the runtime provides it. */ activeResources?: number" — and computes it inside `writeHeapdump`: `const getActiveResourcesInfo = (process as {…}).getActiveResourcesInfo; const activeResources = typeof getActiveResourcesInfo === 'function' ? getActiveResourcesInfo.call(process).length : undefined`, then spreads it into the 0600 diagnostics payload. The JS-vs-native comparison is the `unaccountedNative` field's own declaration — "/** max(0, rss − heapTotal − external) — native memory no counter accounts for. */" — computed at `Math.max(0, usage.rss - usage.heapTotal - usage.external)`. And both are PRINTED in the summary a user reads: packages/server-core/src/sessions/SessionManager.ts pushes `  unaccounted native memory: ${formatHeapdumpBytes(memory.unaccountedNative)}` and, guarded on the number, `  active handles: ${row.activeResources}`. P6.453 is ✅ (`dc2509cea`).

Corrected recommendation: A′ — record the child-process capture gap as covered-by-decision (structural, already named honestly by `describeHeapdumpConversationCoverage`, which is a `Record<ModelProvider, …>` so a new runtime breaks the build) and refresh M22 ×3 to covered on landed work. Size 0-code XS, not "two lines" — which matters, because sizing an already-built thing as buildable is what turns a closed row back into a lane.

Corrected question: «Незахват дочернего процесса в /heapdump на claude и codex фиксируем как структурный отказ-по-решению (он уже назван честно в самом выводе), а M22 обновляем на covered без кода — счётчик открытых хэндлов (`activeResources`) и строка «JS-куча против нативной» (`unaccountedNative`) уже считаются в `heapdump.ts` и печатаются в сводке `SessionManager.ts`. [да]»

- **Native:** claude — TIER 1, and the ledger's “Nessy has no producer for either” was REFUTED on the wire, not in a binary: P6.462 measured against a recording upstream that a subagent sends `x-claude-code-agent-id`, a NESTED subagent adds `x-claude-code-parent-agent-id`, and main-thread turns send neither — so the child already emits them and our adapter was dropping them; forwarding an inbound header is what `inboundBeta` already does for `anthropic-beta`. Pi and codex have no producer, so TIER 2 on the host. M23 — no runtime mechanism CAN report it: the host owns the two-wire router, so the honest surface is host-side by construction.
- **Why absent today:** M23 and M36 are stale (M36 `56d798b94`; M23 both halves `565ac10d5 + 2484e4ac9 + 781cff97f`, the BYO surface marking the translated wire and naming its cost, plus the session-picker half). M2 is the ONE genuinely open row in family M: P6.181 is ⬜ and carries **Release sitting 2026-09-10 (owner ruling, point 5): gates 1.36.0 — P7.5**. It is PROOF, not code — the mechanism landed (`gatewaySessionFetch` is the adapter's `fetchImpl` on both paths and `gatewayRequestHeaders` merges `NESSY_CUSTOM_HEADERS` with the correlation id, scoped by the gateway prefix) and P2.422's own note says a packaged run remains the only thing that would prove the header reaches the gateway. A dev build is not the shipped artifact.
- **Options:**
  1. **A — keep M2 in the release sitting as a packaged-build proof with its three required controls (a BYO endpoint carries neither header; the loopback hop carries neither; the codex path driven separately) and refresh M23/M36** (size XS here, S for the sitting run) — The cell moves onto a measured verdict at 1.36.0; the run is written to `docs/testing/cdp-runs/`.
  2. **B — mark M2 covered on the static reading** (size 0) — Adds a seventh behaviour-unproven cell to a section whose own header scores six of 263 as behaviour-proven, and P6.43's second mutant showed both headers start reaching the loopback hop the moment the prefix scope is dropped — exactly the failure a static reading cannot see.
  3. **C — defer M2 past 1.36.0** (size 0) — The only open row in the family stays open with no date.
- **Reader's recommendation:** A. The control set is the point: (a) BYO endpoint, (b) loopback hop, (c) the codex path driven itself — a positive on claude does not prove codex, because the two share the loopback adapter.
- **Question (RU):** M2 остаётся в релизной посадке 1.36.0 как проверка на УПАКОВАННОЙ сборке с тремя контролями (BYO-эндпоинт, loopback-хоп, отдельный прогон codex), а M23 и M36 обновляем на измеренное? [да]
- **Coverage gain:** 6 cells: M23 ×1 and M36 ×3 → covered now; M2 ×2 → covered at the 1.36.0 sitting, or recorded as measured-and-negative if a control fires.
- **Refuter verdict:** upheld

### QM12 — Five small named diagnostics, each one sentence in a place a user reads

- **Rows:** M19, M24, M25, M26, M27
- **Cells:** M19 absent ×3; M24 Codex absent (Pi/claude n/a); M25 Pi absent / Codex absent (claude n/a); M26 Pi partial (claude/codex n/a); M27 no-verdict ×3 — 10 non-covered cells

For M24 and M25 the evidence is codex's own, at the STRONG tier: `experimentalFeature/list` in `packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/ClientRequest.ts:100` with `ExperimentalFeatureStage` exactly `"beta"|"underDevelopment"|"stable"|"deprecated"|"removed"`, and the shipped win32 binaries' own literals.

The reader must read `stage` and NEVER `enabled` — P2.374 measured that a key at stage `removed` still reports `enabled: true`, and that a bogus key yields “no error, no warning, no stderr line”. M25 — TIER 1: codex writes the log itself inside the CODEX_HOME we own and delete; the host merely never named the path, and the BINARY OUTRANKS THE PAGE — the on-disk name is `sandbox.<YYYY-MM-DD>.log` (90 kept) beside `setup_error.json` under `.sandbox\`, while `windows-sandbox.log` is only `WINDOWS_SANDBOX_LOG_ATTACHMENT_FILENAME`, the upload rename; the page's `#i-need-to-send-diagnostics-to-openai` is the WEAK tier and was measured wrong on the filename. M19, M26, M27 — no runtime mechanism, host chrome, and the data already exists (`resolveChannelPolicy` returns `{enabled, allowedSources, deniedSources}`, `buildAwsEnv` is the single `AWS_REGION` writer, `MAX_PIPED_STDIN_BYTES` is already enforced).

The one residue worth carrying is that M25's win32 arm has never been exercised on Windows by any lane here.

  1. **A — refresh M19/M24/M26/M27 to the measured verdict, and hold M25 at partial with one named residue: a live Windows run** (size XS) — Nine cells close on landed, measured work; the one cell nobody can exercise on this hardware stays honest instead of being asserted.
  2. **B — re-measure all five on a packaged build first** (size S) — Five live cases for work already measured at the point it landed; buys little beyond M25.

- **Reader's recommendation:** A. Four of the five were measured when they landed, three of them CORRECTING the ledger's own premise; only the Windows sandbox arm needs a rig this project does not have, and saying so is cheaper and truer than asserting it.
- **Question (RU):** Обновляем M19/M24/M26/M27 на измеренное, а M25 (лог windows-песочницы) помечаем как требующий одного живого прогона на Windows — до него partial с названным остатком и с именем файла из бинаря, а не со страницы? [да]

- **Refuter verdict:** corrected
- **Refuter's correction:** Substance verified, one citation does not resolve, plus the family-wide orphan audit this last question owes.

Citation defect: QM12 cites "`experimentalFeature/list` in `packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/ClientRequest.ts:100`". That path does not exist. The file is `packages/codex-protocol/generated/ClientRequest.ts` (line 100 is correct, and it is the one-line `ClientRequest` union that declares the method). `generated/v2/` does exist and does hold `ExperimentalFeatureStage.ts` with exactly `"beta" | "underDevelopment" | "stable" | "deprecated" | "removed"`, which is why the slip is easy to miss and why it must be fixed before it is copied forward — this repo's own rule is that a citation naming a path that does not resolve is a finding, not a typo.

Everything else in QM12 holds, including the claim most likely to be wrong: M25's filename is pinned in our own tests — packages/server-core/src/pi-harness/isolation-remediation.test.ts asserts `toContain('sandbox.<YYYY-MM-DD>.log')`, comments that codex "rotates `sandbox.<date>.log` daily with `MAX_LOG_FILES = 90`", and has a case titled "does not name windows-sandbox.log, which is only the upload attachment name" (`WINDOWS_SANDBOX_LOG_ATTACHMENT_FILENAME`). M24's stage reader exists (packages/codex-agent-server/src/experimental-features.ts). All three channels.md and all three amazon-bedrock.md anchors resolve on the rendered pages.

ORPHAN AUDIT (criterion f), run over the family M table: 41 rows, M1–M41. The twelve clusters cover 38. The three not in any cluster are M15, M20 and M41 — and none of them has a non-covered cell (M15 `covered ×3`; M20 `covered ×3`; M41 `covered` / `covered` / `n/a`). **There are no orphan row ids.** One rider is un-homed rather than orphaned: QM1 itself observes that M41's `covered` (short export intervals + flush on exit) "stops being automatically true now that a span stream batches, and must be re-checked", and no cluster and no plan row owns that re-check — it should be written into QM1's action or given a row of its own, or M41 will keep reading `covered ×2` on an untested premise.

Corrected question: «Обновляем M19/M24/M26/M27 на измеренное, а M25 (лог windows-песочницы) держим partial с названным остатком — один живой прогон на Windows — и с именем файла из бинаря (`sandbox.<YYYY-MM-DD>.log`, 90 хранимых), а не со страницы; ссылку на `experimentalFeature/list` правим на `packages/codex-protocol/generated/ClientRequest.ts:100`. Сирот в семействе M нет; отдельно нужен владелец для перепроверки M41 (интервалы экспорта и сброс на выходе) после появления батчинга спанов. [да]»


## Family N — Cross-session messaging

### QN1 — Пять строк уже построены и закрыты именованными лейнами — но ячейки в реестре ни разу не обновлялись (15 runtime + 4 host)

- **Rows:** N2, N6, N7, N8, N9
